# Story 12.15: Stand up live recovery/continuity fault-injection drivers and recalibrate A10

Status: in-progress

## Story

As an operations owner,
I want the continuity-drill, projection-rebuild, and scoped-outage validation coordinators to execute against real fault injection in the Aspire-composed test topology instead of a scripted fake,
so that RPO/RTO/rebuild/scope-recording targets are proven against a real environment, not merely provable by construction, and the A10 [ASSUMPTION] can be confirmed or recalibrated with logged evidence.

## Acceptance Criteria

*(Verbatim in substance from `epics.md` Story 12.15, lines 3182-3202, with numbering and sentence punctuation added for traceability. The implementation obligations below resolve repository-specific ambiguities and the Story 12.14 handoff; they do not replace these three canonical criteria.)*

1. **Given** the Story 12.11/12.12/12.13 coordinators and their deferred-driver seams, **when** a live implementation is provided against the Aspire-composed test topology (simulated EventStore outage, simulated M365 subscription failure, dependency outages per NFR58/59), **then** each drill/validation coordinator runs a real recovery/rebuild/degradation exercise against a dedicated test tenant, replacing the current `NotSupportedException` inert defaults (`DeferredContinuityDrillScenarioRunner`, `DeferredProjectionRebuildDriver`, `DeferredScopedOutageInjectionDriver`).

2. **Given** a completed live drill run, **when** the run finishes, **then** a real `ContinuityDrillReport` / `ProjectionRebuildReport` / `ScopedOutageDegradationReport` is produced with measured RPO/RTO/duration/scope-recording-latency, and the A10 [ASSUMPTION] targets (PRD/addendum) are either confirmed as commitments or revised downward with the deviation and rationale logged (NFR56, NFR57, A10).

3. **Given** a scenario cannot run against a full production-equivalent topology in the sandbox, **when** the story closes, **then** the residual gap to full production-scale fault injection is explicitly recorded as a follow-up in Completion Notes—never silently assumed closed.

### Binding implementation obligations

The following obligations make the canonical criteria testable against the repository as it exists at baseline commit `1493ff8f`:

- **Real means primary-path evidence, not a scripted measurement.** A live run must cause an observable fault at an Aspire resource or composed dependency boundary, exercise the real coordinator/evaluator/audit path, inspect post-recovery state, and retain enough provenance to reproduce the result. A test double that simply returns a preselected `*Measurement` cannot satisfy AC1 or AC2.
- **Fault authority stays outside the ChatBot process.** The Tier-3/AppHost orchestration boundary owns stopping, starting, restarting, or faulting composed resources. The Server must not request or acquire general-purpose AppHost/DCP control, expose an unauthenticated fault endpoint, or be able to target an arbitrary resource or tenant. Any bounded provider simulator/control seam is owned and invoked by the opted-in Tier-3 harness.
- **Activation is doubly opt-in and fail closed.** Live drivers and their scheduler/release-gate path are disabled by default. The Tier-3 harness may compose/run them only when an explicit live-recovery-validation option is enabled *and* the configured tenant satisfies `ReplayTenantPolicy.IsTestTenant` (`replay-test:` prefix). Missing controller capability, wrong environment, wrong tenant, empty dataset, zero scenario coverage, timeout, cleanup failure, or missing evidence yields `Unmeasurable`/stop-ship; it never falls back to the deferred or scripted implementation and never becomes a pass.
- **The two A10 drills are mandatory.** The EventStore outage and M365 subscription-failure scenarios must both execute end to end in the Aspire/DAPR sandbox. The current topology has no live Microsoft Graph/M365 resource, so the M365 exercise must fault the real ChatBot subscription-processing boundary through a controlled, topology-composed Graph/subscription simulator and record external-M365 equivalence as a residual. It must not claim production Graph fidelity.
- **The rebuild and all six scoped-outage dependencies are mandatory.** Rebuild uses a versioned baseline dataset and only immutable `ProjectConversationSourceEmailView` records plus `IWormAuditStore` history—never Graph, mailbox re-ingestion, current upstream Party/Folder state, or sibling-tenant context. Scoped-outage validation covers the canonical `graph`, `identity`, `ai-provider`, `command-execution`, `audit-store`, and `attachment-processing` tokens; `graph` also covers expired-subscription behavior and is not duplicated as a seventh token.
- **Existing verdict semantics are preserved.** Continuity keeps `met | missed | unmeasurable`; projection rebuild keeps equivalent/divergent, duration-exceeded, and unmeasurable as separate dimensions; scoped outage keeps contained/breached, late scope recording, and unmeasurable separate. An honest measurable target miss is evidence for remediation or recalibration. An unmeasurable result is always a breach signal. No evaluator threshold or fail-safe behavior may be weakened to make the run pass.
- **A10 is only RPO/RTO.** RPO ≤ 15 minutes and RTO ≤ 4 hours are the A10/NFR56 starter targets. Projection rebuild ≤ 4 hours is NFR57, and scope/dependency recording ≤ 5 minutes is NFR41. Measure all three families, but do not describe the rebuild or scope-recording targets as A10 values.
- **Story 12.14's handoff is completed safely.** The required Tier-3/release workflow—not the ChatBot process—schedules and orchestrates the destructive live validation. Release concurrency is **per-commit and non-cancelling** (`github.sha` concurrency group): that preserves a required check for every publishable commit and accepts N concurrent 5.5-hour jobs inside the window instead of a shared queue that GitHub can cancel. CI remains schedule/`workflow_dispatch` only. Its fail-closed evidence gate must cover all three coordinators. Do not add the live faults to the general `PeriodicEnforcementBackgroundService`: a Server-hosted driver cannot safely or technically stop its own Aspire/DCP resource. If the ADR also publishes live-validation status through the existing M2 readiness model, it may ingest externally produced evidence but must not acquire resource-lifecycle authority. General deployments retain inert, fail-safe defaults. The release path fails closed on disabled required validation, never-run/incomplete/stale/future evidence, exception/timeout/cleanup failure, unmeasurable or serious breach, zero coverage, or missing scenario/dataset provenance.
- **Evidence is retained and reviewable.** CI uploads the raw `.trx` test output and the retained reports/manifests as a workflow artifact even on failure — narrowed from `.trx`/logs/traces/metrics by the round-5 decision, because requiring locators for artifacts the lane never produced only guaranteed every manifest carried syntactically valid links to nothing; the gap is carried as residual `RV-EVIDENCE-KINDS`, while the story Completion Notes retain the CI run locator, artifact name, topology commit/configuration, dataset identity/volume, report summary, and residual IDs. Do not describe ignored local `TestResults` files as repository-retained evidence.
- **Recalibration is governed, not automatic.** Runtime code never mutates `RecoveryTargets`. Winston reviews the evidence and Murat validates authenticity. If A10 is confirmed, remove/resolve the A10 assumption in the canonical PRD decision record and state the committed values. If a target changes, update the PRD decision record, the owning ADR, and `RecoveryTargets` in the same reviewed change, state whether the numeric threshold became stricter or looser, and link the evidence and rationale. A miss may instead produce corrective work and a rerun; it does not automatically justify loosening the commitment.

### Required live scenario and evidence matrix

| Exercise | Required live scenario | Minimum proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Continuity | `eventstore-outage` | The orchestrator stops or otherwise makes the real sandbox EventStore resource unavailable, the application observes the fault, restoration occurs, and measured RPO/RTO, reconstructable committed operations, data-loss, tenant-isolation, and unauthorized-mutation checks are reported. |
| Continuity | `m365-subscription-failure` | A topology-composed subscription boundary expires/fails, the real subscription recovery path is exercised, and measured RPO/RTO plus no-loss/no-duplicate/no-cross-tenant assertions are reported; external Graph equivalence is explicitly residual. |
| Projection rebuild | Configured versioned baseline dataset | A fresh isolated test partition is rebuilt from immutable source metadata plus WORM history only; schema/version and structural digests are diffed; duration, resource count, tenant isolation, and absence of mailbox re-ingestion are proven. |
| Scoped outage | `graph` | Only the expected mailbox/subscription scope degrades; unrelated tenant/mailbox work remains available; recovery produces no duplicate side effects. |
| Scoped outage | `identity` | Authentication/authorization fails closed at the expected service-client/command scope; no broad fallback access or unauthorized mutation occurs. |
| Scoped outage | `ai-provider` | The AI-dependent operation degrades at the expected scope while dependency-independent paths remain usable where their dependency graph permits. |
| Scoped outage | `command-execution` | The affected operation reaches a visible recoverable state and resumes without duplicate effects after restoration. |
| Scoped outage | `audit-store` | Governed mutation fails closed when required audit evidence is unavailable; no silent data loss or unauthorized commit occurs. |
| Scoped outage | `attachment-processing` | Partial attachment failure remains bounded to the workflow item/attachment scope, is visible and recoverable, and exposes no unauthorized content. |

### Review Findings

Code review 2026-08-01 (three adversarial layers: Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Acceptance Auditor). Every finding below was re-verified against the source before rating. Central theme: several headline "live measurements" are unfalsifiable by construction, so the reports they produced — and the A10/NFR57/NFR41 governance changes made from them — do not carry the evidentiary weight the story's own binding obligations require.

#### Review decisions (Jerome, 2026-08-01)

1. **Evidence integrity (D1, D2, D3, D5) — send back to dev; build real exercises.** The story returns to `in-progress`. Required: a real projection rebuild that runs through the projection path and writes/reads a genuine isolated partition; real fault seams for `ai-provider`, `command-execution`, `audit-store`, and `attachment-processing` that degrade actual ChatBot dependency clients; real scope-recording instrumentation instead of two adjacent clock reads; and state-store end-state reads with genuinely independent assertions for the M365 drill. The corresponding NFR57 and NFR41 evidence claims cannot stand until these produce falsifiable measurements.
2. **Governance (D4, D6, D7) — correct the ADR and deferrals now; hold A10 pending CI.** Fix the `live-recovery-validation-drivers.md` scenario-to-mechanism matrix so it describes what was actually built, and re-open the predecessor deferrals in `9-11`/`9-12`/`9-13`, their three ADRs, and `deferred-work.md` that were retired on simulator-only results. The A10 confirmation stays in the PRD/decision log/addendum but must be tagged **provisional** until the CI lane produces a retained run locator; re-confirm only against that run.
3. **CI trigger (D8) — scheduled plus manual dispatch.** Add a `schedule:` trigger matching the configured 7-day `Cadence` and a `workflow_dispatch` entry; remove the job from the per-PR path. Give `ci.yml` and `release.yml` separate concurrency groups so the required release check can no longer queue behind or be cancelled by the CI run.
4. **Scope and hygiene (D9, D10, D11, D12) — all four in scope for this story.** Isolate the RecoverySandbox composition out of the shipped `src/` AppHost project; amend the Task 7 checkbox text so the negative cases are described as unit-level rather than Tier-3 (or add real live negatives); resolve the client `AllowIntegerValues = false` change (document it with deserialize-direction coverage and a Change Log entry, or lift it into its own story); and confirm-and-list or revert the `references/Hexalith.EventStore` submodule bump.

- [x] [Review][Decision] Projection rebuild performs no rebuild; `equivalent` is a tautology — `LiveProjectionRebuildDriver.RebuildAsync` sets `baseline = StructuralSnapshot(sources, auditRecords)` (`:81`) and builds the "rebuilt" partition from `StructuralSnapshot(sources, auditRecords)` again (`:85`) — the same deterministic static function over the same in-memory arguments. `freshPartition` is a local `Dictionary` never written, read back, or cleaned up; no projection store, translator, DAPR state store, or EventStore is touched, and `RebuildAsync` returns synchronously. `divergent` and `duration-exceeded` are unreachable, and the measured `0.002s` is the cost of hashing two strings. Violates AC1, AC2, and "A test double that simply returns a preselected `*Measurement` cannot satisfy AC1 or AC2". The NFR57 claim published in the addendum and `docs/adrs/projection-rebuild-validation.md` rests on this. [`tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/Recovery/LiveProjectionRebuildDriver.cs:81-99`]
- [x] [Review][Decision] Four of six scoped-outage dependencies fault nothing, and every NFR59 safety flag is a hardcoded constant — `RecoveryScopedOutageSimulatorState.Process` returns literal `crossTenantLeakageDetected = false`, `unauthorizedMutationDetected = false`, `silentDataLossDetected = false`, `duplicateSideEffectDetected = false`, `recoverable = true`, `controlAvailable = true`, and `observedScope = ExpectedScope(dependency)` — the same table `LiveScopedOutageInjectionDriver.ExpectedScope` derives its expectation from, so scope escape is structurally impossible. For `ai-provider`, `command-execution`, `audit-store`, and `attachment-processing` the entire fault is a dictionary entry in a separate web app; no ChatBot AI adapter, dispatcher, `IAuditWriter`, or attachment path is degraded. The `audit-store` matrix row ("governed mutation fails closed when required audit evidence is unavailable") is actively contradicted — the control governed command succeeds during that scenario because audit was never faulted. Violates AC1 and Task 5's "Exercise the real dependency client/component path". [`tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.RecoverySandbox/RecoveryScopedOutageSimulatorState.cs:77-91`, `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/Recovery/AspireScopedOutageOperations.cs:166-179`]
- [x] [Review][Decision] Scope-recording latency is the delta between two adjacent clock reads — `observedAtUtc = atUtc` and `scopeRecordedAtUtc = DateTimeOffset.UtcNow` are set in the same statement block and subtracted by the driver. No ChatBot monitoring or scope-recording path participates. This produces the `0.000002s`–`0.001102s` NFR41 figures now published in the addendum, and violates Task 5's "Missing monitoring evidence is unmeasurable, not a zero-millisecond latency". [`tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.RecoverySandbox/RecoveryScopedOutageSimulatorState.cs:80-81`, `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/Recovery/LiveScopedOutageInjectionDriver.cs:114`]
- [x] [Review][Decision] A10 was converted from `[ASSUMPTION]` to a confirmed commitment across the canonical PRD, decision log, addendum, and ADR on evidence this story itself classifies as non-retained — Completion Notes state the run is "under ignored `TestResults/live-recovery/` … neither is repository-retained evidence. Hosted CI was not run from this workspace." Run `01KYYB6WYWKEJD79M2PW63XSYB` is cited across governance documents but exists nowhere in the repository or CI. #### Review verification (2026-08-01)

18 of 23 patch findings were applied and verified. `UseHexalithProjectReferences=true dotnet build Hexalith.ChatBot.slnx` passed with **0 warnings / 0 errors**, and the affected suites passed: Server **1723/1723**, Architecture **72/72** (up from 70 — two new guards), Conformance **98/98**, AppHost **14/14**, Client **37/37**, Integration **93 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed**. The live Tier-3 test remains skipped in this environment and was **not** executed, so none of these patches has live-topology proof.

Two patches required a different approach than first attempted, and both are worth reviewing:

- **AppHost test-tenant predicate.** Calling `ReplayTenantPolicy.IsTestTenant` from the AppHost does not compile: Aspire treats the AppHost's references to app projects as *resources*, not compile references, so no Server type is visible. Granting `InternalsVisibleTo` does not help. The `replay-test:` literal therefore remains at the composition boundary, but `LiveRecoveryValidationArchitectureTests` now extracts `ReplayTenantPolicy.ReplayTestTenantPrefix` from source and asserts the AppHost literal matches it exactly, so the two cannot drift silently. The story's "no second predicate" intent is met as closely as the composition model allows; a genuinely single-source fix would need the prefix in a non-app assembly the AppHost can reference.
- **Environment guard.** Basing the guard on `builder.Environment.EnvironmentName` broke the topology contract test, because the AppHost's inferred host environment under `DistributedApplicationTestingBuilder` is neither Development nor Testing. The guard now requires an explicit `LiveRecoveryValidation:EnvironmentName` of `Development` or `Testing`. This is stronger than the original intent: the sandbox environment must be *declared* by the invoking workflow rather than inferred, so an inferred default can never unlock fault injection.

One regression was introduced and fixed during this pass: the guarded restoration `finally` initially wrapped `OperationCanceledException`, which broke `CanceledMeasurementStillRestoresAndCleansTheFaultedResource`. Cancellation now propagates unwrapped when restoration itself succeeded.

**Dismissed as noise (2):** (1) "The realm adds a static committed password `recovery-validator-pass`, expanding the standing credential surface" — this follows the pre-existing local test-realm convention (`actor-alpha-pass`, `actor-beta-pass`, `admin-pass` all present at baseline `1493ff8`); the adjacent client secret is a `__…__` placeholder substituted at import. (2) "Nothing consumes the release gate, so a release with no evidence is indistinguishable from one with evidence" — `semantic-release` declares `needs: live-recovery-validation`, so a missing or failed job does block the release; the accurate residual concern is captured in the gate-freshness patch item above.

### Review round 2 — v1.1 remediation (2026-08-01)

Scope: chunk 1 of the diff only — the evidence-integrity layer (`tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/Recovery/**` and `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.RecoverySandbox/**`, 51 new files / 5,822 lines). The Server product surface, AppHost, workflows, unit/architecture/conformance suites and governance docs were **not** reviewed in this round and remain outstanding. Three adversarial layers (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Acceptance Auditor); every finding below was re-verified against source by the reviewer before rating.

**Substantial credit where due.** The v1.1 pass genuinely resolved a large majority of the 23 prior `[Patch]` findings, verified against source rather than claim: the EventStore fault probe now demands a ≥500/408 dependency-boundary status and re-probes a failed stop command; `unauthorizedMutationAbsent` requires an observed 403/404 and throws otherwise; the tenant-isolation probe reads a real control-tenant resource with an independent bearer; the M365 no-loss/no-duplicate/isolation assertions now read genuine EventStore actor state and DAPR read-model sentinels using per-scenario **deltas**; `_completedEffects` resets on restore; `CheckpointAsync` moved inside the `try`; restoration `finally` is guarded with cancellation preserved; clock-skew tolerance added; the manifest no longer rejects zero coverage so unmeasurable reports persist; artifact locators reduced to what is actually uploaded; the gate now compares `MeasurementsSeconds` against `AllowedTargetsSeconds` and bounds manifests to the attempt window; options are bound to `ChatBot:LiveRecoveryValidation`; the `src/` → `tests/` AppHost reference is gone; CI moved to `schedule:` + `workflow_dispatch` with separate concurrency groups; `SweepOrder` is deterministic with Identity last; the dataset file is genuinely loaded and cross-checked; and the projection rebuild now does a real store round-trip with distinct partitions and ETag cleanup. The evidence run cited in Completion Notes (`01KYYQ42B…`) exists on disk with nine manifests and nine reports and is the newest of 27 runs.

**The problem that remains.** The four load-bearing `[Decision]` root causes from round 1 are only partly resolved. The mechanisms changed; the falsifiability did not. Independent of any prior finding, the published numbers confirm it: all six scope-recording latencies land between 66µs and 1.17ms against a 5-minute NFR41 budget, both continuity drills report `measuredRpo: 00:00:00`, and the M365 drill completes in 613ms end to end. A measurement five to six orders of magnitude below its own threshold cannot distinguish a pass from a failure.

#### Review decisions (Jerome, 2026-08-01, round 2)

1. **Scoped-outage evidence layer — send back to dev; build real seams.** The story returns to `in-progress`. Fault the actual ChatBot dependency clients rather than sandbox stubs: `IAiAssistanceProvider` through `AcceptedCommandDispatcher`, `IAuditWriter` through the `ChatBotCommandAdmissionPipeline` fail-closed gate, and the real attachment path. Derive observed scope and the four NFR59 safety flags from real instrumentation instead of a table mirrored from `ExpectedScope`. The NFR58, NFR41 and NFR59 claims cannot stand until a scope escape, an unauthorized mutation, a silent loss and a cross-tenant leak are each *reachable* outcomes.
2. **Continuity RPO and projection rebuild — send back to dev; make both measurable.** Seed a multi-record committed set and derive `CommittedCount` from observed state rather than the literal `1`, so the A10 RPO half can be exercised on a passing run. Drive the rebuild through the real `MailboxIntakeProjectionTranslator` / `AssociationProjectionHandler` so `divergent` is a reachable verdict. The NFR57 equivalence claim depends on this.
3. **RTO 4-hour cap — narrow the claim and add the missing validation.** Keep the 3-minute restoration budget; a 4-hour sandbox job is impractical on every scheduled run. Add the cross-validation Task 6 requires, publish the effective measurable recovery ceiling in the evidence manifest, and stop describing the run as confirming RTO ≤ 4h. The lane proves recovery within its own budget, not within the A10 target.
4. **Storage-tenant guard — make the guard cover the real tenant.** Give the physical tenant the `replay-test:` prefix, or extend `ReplayTenantPolicy.IsTestTenant` to recognise it, so the fail-closed predicate protects the data actually written. The current `!= tenant-alpha && != tenant-beta` name check is precisely the second test-tenant predicate Task 2 forbids.

- [x] [Review][Decision] NFR58 scope containment is structurally unfalsifiable for all six dependencies — `LiveScopedOutageInjectionDriver.ExpectedScope` and `RecoveryScopeObservationMonitor.ScopeFor` are byte-identical six-arm switches over the same dependency token. `ObservedScope` is fed from the monitor for all six paths, so `ExpectedScope == ObservedScope` unconditionally and `ScopedOutageDegradationEvaluator`'s `scope_escape` deviation is unreachable. "Observed" means "looked up in the second copy of the table". An identity outage that genuinely took down every tenant would still report `observedScope = "service-client"`. This is round-1 finding D2 surviving with a new mechanism: the Completion Note claims "an asynchronous monitor records actual application-observed scope", but the monitor hardcodes the scope. [`tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.RecoverySandbox/RecoveryScopeObservationMonitor.cs:56-66`, `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/Recovery/LiveScopedOutageInjectionDriver.cs:155-165`]

The NFR59 isolation evidence for four of six dependencies is four hardcoded `false`s wearing a boolean expression. Both bounds are generated by one process; the code comment asserting "the two bounds are stamped by different processes" is factually wrong for all six. #### Review remediation applied (2026-08-01, round 2)

**14 of the 22 patches were applied and verified; 6 were deliberately left for the dev's decision-1/decision-2 rework, and 2 became scope corrections.**

Verified after remediation with `UseHexalithProjectReferences=true dotnet build Hexalith.ChatBot.slnx` at **0 warnings / 0 errors**, and every affected suite green: Server **1724/1724** (up from 1723 — one new manifest-validation guard), Architecture **72/72**, Conformance **98/98**, AppHost **14/14**, Client **38/38**, Integration **99 passed / 4 skipped / 0 failed**. The live Tier-3 test remains skipped in this environment and was **not** executed, so none of these patches has live-topology proof.

**Deliberately NOT applied — entangled with decisions 1 and 2** (the dev should fix these inside the rework, not before it): `cleanup-complete` literal; `restore`-then-snapshot guard; `datasetVolume: 6`; rebuild cleanup cancellation token; string-asserting architecture/unit guards; `InflightItemsRecoverable`. Applying them now would churn code that the scoped-outage layer, sandbox state machine and rebuild driver rework will replace — the same trap that produced round 1's five "NOT APPLIED: entangled" items.

**Two corrections worth reviewing:**

- **The RTO patch changed shape mid-application.** I first added a `MinimumMeasurableRecoveryCeiling` floor to `LiveRecoveryValidationOptions.Validate()`, which broke five legitimate unit fixtures that configure a 5-second `RestorationTimeout` against fakes. That floor was an invented threshold nobody had agreed to, so it was removed. The substantive fix stands on its own: every manifest now publishes `MeasurableRecoveryCeilingSeconds` (validated positive, with new test coverage), and `RecoveryTargets`/`RestorationTimeout` documentation states plainly that a lane whose restoration budget is shorter than `MaxRto` can only ever report `unmeasurable` for a slower recovery — it cannot demonstrate a miss.
- **The commit-provenance patch now fails closed rather than degrading.** `ResolvedRepositoryCommit()` tries `GITHUB_SHA`, then `git rev-parse HEAD`, then **throws**. An "unknown-local" marker was the first attempt, but `RecoveryValidationEvidenceManifest.Validate()` requires a full hex commit, and writing evidence with unresolvable provenance is exactly what the original hard-coded SHA fallback did wrong.

Two patches were also narrower than their walkthrough description, and the remainder is called out rather than quietly dropped: the **release-gate** fix makes the gate's inputs honest (`LatestAttemptCompletedSuccessfully` is now derived from actual driver failures, unmeasurable counts and manifest completeness; `evaluatedAtUtc` is a real wall-clock read, so the staleness branches are reachable) — but the gate is still evaluated **in-process by the test that produced the evidence**. Moving it to a separate workflow step touches `.github/workflows/*`, which is chunk 2 and unreviewed, so it remains open. Likewise the **sandbox `/process` auth** finding was verified as largely a false alarm: `Authorized()` does a correct SHA-256 `FixedTimeEquals` against the controller secret and the forwarded bearer is validated by ChatBot itself, so only the secret-entropy half was real and fixed (CSPRNG instead of the ULID correlation-id generator).

**Dismissed as noise (2):** (1) "Manifest `daprVersion: 1.18.1` contradicts the story's pinned `1.18.4`" — the story pins the Dapr *.NET SDK*; the manifest records the measured *runtime* version, and `DaprVersion` is the one provenance field genuinely probed rather than hardcoded. (2) "The new `RecoverySandbox` project's build output is untracked and would be committed" — verified ignored via `git check-ignore`; an earlier `find`-based listing simply did not respect ignore rules.

### Review round 3 — chunk 2, the shipped product surface (2026-08-01)

Scope: `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/**`, `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Client/**`, `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.AppHost/**`, `.github/workflows/{ci,release}.yml`, `Hexalith.ChatBot.slnx` — 23 modified + 16 new files, ~2,242 diff lines. This is the complement of round 2 (which covered `tests/**` only). Governance docs (`prd.md`, `.decision-log.md`, `addendum.md`, `docs/adrs/**`, predecessor stories, `deferred-work.md`) remain unreviewed and are the outstanding chunk 3. Three adversarial layers (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Acceptance Auditor); every finding below was re-verified against source by the reviewer before rating, and two layer claims were rejected on that re-verification (see Dismissed).

**Credit where due, verified against source rather than claim.** The `src/` → `tests/` AppHost `ProjectReference` is genuinely gone and `Program.cs` no longer composes the sandbox at all; round-1's inlined `replay-test:` literal and `!= tenant-alpha && != tenant-beta` predicate are both resolved — `ReplayTenantPolicy.StorageTenantFor` now *derives* the physical tenant from the guarded logical label, so the single `IsTestTenant` predicate governs the data that is actually written. The three `Deferred*` defaults are split one-type-per-file and product DI still registers them, so the Server acquires no fault authority. `LiveRecoveryValidationOptions` is genuinely bound and `ValidateOnStart`-guarded. `SweepOrder` is deterministic with Identity last and `All` is derived from it (declaration order correct). The gate's unknown-job handling has no collapsing default branch. Both workflows upload evidence with `if: always()` and 30-day retention, and the required-lane pattern correctly throws rather than skipping when the Tier-3 runtime is absent. `RecoveryTargets` numerics are unchanged and correctly re-marked provisional, and three separate doc sites now state plainly that a restoration budget shorter than `MaxRto` can only ever report `unmeasurable`.

**The problem that remains.** The gate is the product surface's load-bearing artifact, and it is still graded by the run it is grading. Every decisive input — the allowed targets, the expected dataset set, whether deviations block, and whether the drivers were live at all — arrives inside the envelope the producing run writes, and no workflow step re-evaluates it out of process. `RecoveryTargets` is not referenced anywhere in the gate.

#### Review decisions (Jerome, 2026-08-01, round 3)

1. **Independent gate — separate chained job.** Add a `needs: live-recovery-validation` job that downloads the evidence artifact and re-runs `LiveRecoveryValidationEvidenceGate.Evaluate` out of process against a fresh wall clock, so the evaluator cannot see the producer's in-memory state and the staleness/freshness branches become genuinely reachable. Extend the architecture guard to assert that step exists, rather than pinning the current single-step shape.
2. **Gate anchoring — all four measures** (delegated to the reviewer; recorded as the reviewer's judgement, not Jerome's): anchor `AllowedTargetsSeconds` to `RecoveryTargets`; require a per-job measurement-key vocabulary that fails closed on absence, mirroring assertion names; emit a distinct reason when an allowed target exceeds `MeasurableRecoveryCeilingSeconds`; and anchor coverage, policy and provenance — derive `ExpectedProjectionDatasets` from configured options, make `TargetDeviationsBlockRelease` a gate parameter rather than a manifest field, require `DriverMode` to be the live token, and require tenant/dataset/commit agreement across an attempt. They are mutually reinforcing: omitting any one leaves a path where the run still sets its own bar.
3. **Release lane — key concurrency by `github.ref`.** Change the group to `live-recovery-validation-release-${{ github.repository }}-${{ github.ref }}` so release branches no longer contend and a queued required check cannot be cancelled by an unrelated push. The lane stays required on every release-branch push; the per-push cost is accepted as the price of a required live gate.

- [x] [Review][Decision] The release gate has no independent evaluation step; the run grades its own homework, and the architecture guard now pins that shape — both `ci.yml` and `release.yml` contain exactly one execution step, `dotnet test --filter "…LiveContinuityAspireE2eTests.LiveRecoveryValidationRunsAllThreeCoordinatorsAndPassesEvidenceGate"`, followed only by `upload-artifact` and `dapr uninstall`. No step reads the retained manifests back from `TestResults` and re-runs `LiveRecoveryValidationEvidenceGate.Evaluate` out of process, so the producer supplies `LatestAttemptCompletedSuccessfully`, `ExpectedProjectionDatasets`, `AlertsDeliveredByJob` and `evaluatedAtUtc` to its own judge. This is the round-2 item explicitly deferred to this chunk ("Moving it to a separate workflow step touches `.github/workflows/*`, which is chunk 2 and unreviewed, so it remains open"). Compounding it, `LiveRecoveryValidationArchitectureTests.RequiredWorkflowsShouldInvokeAndAlwaysRetainTheLiveEvidenceGate` asserts only that each workflow contains the test's own name, `-m:1`, `timeout-minutes: 330`, `if: always()` and `path: TestResults` — a guard that would have caught the missing step instead locks in its absence. Decision needed on shape: (a) a second step in the same job that reads `TestResults/**` and evaluates via a small entry point, (b) a `needs:`-chained job that downloads the artifact and evaluates, or (c) accept in-process evaluation and narrow the published claim. [`.github/workflows/ci.yml:134-142`, `.github/workflows/release.yml:106-114`, `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.Architecture.Tests/LiveRecoveryValidationArchitectureTests.cs:143-165`]
- [x] [Review][Decision] Every threshold the gate enforces is supplied by the run being judged — `LiveRecoveryValidationEvidenceGate.cs` contains no reference to `RecoveryTargets`. Six concrete consequences, each verified: (1) `AllowedTargetsSeconds` is a manifest field, so `rto` measured `20000` against a self-declared allowed `21600` passes although `RecoveryTargets.MaxRto` is `14400`; (2) `MeasurementsSeconds` has no required-key vocabulary — assertion *names* fail closed on absence via `Assertion()`, measurement keys do not, so a continuity manifest carrying `{"x":0}` against `{"x":1}` with verdict `met` passes with no RPO and no RTO recorded anywhere; (3) `MeasurableRecoveryCeilingSeconds` (the lane's `RestorationTimeout`, 180 s) is never compared to `AllowedTargetsSeconds` (14 400 s), so a gate pass still reads as evidence for the 4-hour target — this is the one unapplied quarter of your round-2 decision 3, the other three (ceiling published, cross-validation added, `RecoveryTargets` doc narrowed) being genuinely done; (4) `ExpectedProjectionDatasets` is producer-declared and, in the only caller, is the same variable used to run the sweep (`RunAllAsync(tenantRef, [options.DatasetRef], …)` vs `ExpectedProjectionDatasets: [options.DatasetRef]`), making `projection-rebuild:incomplete_scenario_set` unreachable; (5) `TargetDeviationsBlockRelease` is a producer field, so the run decides whether its own misses block release; (6) `DriverMode` is never inspected, and manifests within one attempt are bound to a common `RunId` but not to a common tenant, dataset, dataset version or repository commit — a `scripted-fake` manifest set is accepted as a live run. Decision needed on which of these become product-anchored constants versus remain deliberate policy inputs. [`src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Audit/LiveRecoveryValidationEvidenceGate.cs:61-105,114-120,129-132,170-189,272-275`, `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Audit/RecoveryValidationEvidenceManifest.cs:23,51,177-181`]
- [x] [Review][Decision] `release.yml` starts the destructive 5.5-hour lane on every push to a release branch, and a third rapid merge silently cancels a queued required gate — the job has no `if:` and no `needs:`, unlike its `ci.yml` twin which is deliberately gated to `schedule || workflow_dispatch` with the comment "Running it per-PR made every PR queue a 5.5-hour fault-injection job". With `cancel-in-progress: false` on a repository-wide group, two merges inside the window queue and a third causes GitHub to cancel the older *pending* run; `semantic-release` now `needs` it, so that commit publishes no version, tag or changelog and shows no failed check. Direction of failure is safe (a release is skipped, never falsely passed), which is why this is medium rather than high, but every release-branch push now also serializes behind up to 5.5 h of fault injection. Round-1 decision 3's literal ask — separate CI and release concurrency groups — *is* met. Decision needed: gate the job to publishing branches, key the concurrency group by `github.ref`, or accept the cost as the price of a required live gate. [`.github/workflows/release.yml:78-89,126-129`]

**Dismissed on re-verification (9):** (1) "`LiveRecoveryValidationOptions` is bound to a section nothing writes" — `ChatBot:LiveRecoveryValidation` matches the repository convention (`ChatBot:PeriodicEnforcement`, `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Program.cs:33`); the bare `LiveRecoveryValidation:` producers are the AppHost's own configuration and Tier-3 args to the AppHost, a different process, and the binding does fail closed for the rogue-deployment input it exists to catch. (2) "The not-Production guard is a self-declaration" — true, but it gates nothing at runtime because no product code consumes `IOptions<LiveRecoveryValidationOptions>` by design, and the round-2 note records declared-not-inferred as a deliberate strengthening. (3) "Retention inverts audit-then-deliver" — the stated contract is audit-before-*alert*, which is intact; the substantive residual is folded into the `RetainAsync` patch. (4) "The access-control change widens security posture" — those sidecars previously had no config, whose default is already allow; only the comment is wrong, folded into the disclosure patch. (5) "`expiresAt.UtcDateTime` breaks the grant-expiry claim" — `ClaimsServiceClientGrantResolver.cs:64-70` parses with `AssumeUniversal | AdjustToUniversal`, so `Z` and `+00:00` are equivalent; disclosure half folded into the same patch. (6) Per-app gRPC port collisions — operator-supplied test-topology overrides that fail fast at bind time. (7) Case-insensitive enum-name and CLR-member-name acceptance — stock `StringEnumConverter` behaviour, not introduced here; folded into the client patch as a doc-accuracy note. (8) `InternalsVisibleTo` for `Hexalith.ChatBot.RecoverySandbox` — consistent with the three existing test-assembly grants. (9) ci.yml's workflow-level `schedule`/`workflow_dispatch` expanding to all jobs — required for the new job's `needs: build`.

### Review round 4 — chunk 3, the governance documents (2026-08-01)

Scope: `docs/adrs/live-recovery-validation-drivers.md` (new), the three predecessor ADRs, `prd.md` / `.decision-log.md` / `addendum.md`, the `9-11`/`9-12`/`9-13` Completion Notes, `deferred-work.md`, `sprint-status.yaml`, and this story's own Completion Notes / File List / Change Log — 11 modified + 2 new, ~379 diff lines. This completes the three-chunk pass (round 2 = `tests/**`, round 3 = `src/**` + workflows). Three adversarial layers; every finding was re-verified against current working-tree source before rating, and five layer claims were rejected on that re-verification (see Dismissed).

**Credit where due, verified against source rather than claim.** The A10-only discipline holds everywhere: NFR57 and NFR41 have their own addendum rows, each ending "not an A10 value", and no ADR describes rebuild or scope-recording as an A10 value. Atomic recalibration is genuinely atomic — `RecoveryTargets.cs:6,20-21,26-29` reads "provisional… pending a retained hosted run locator" with numerics unchanged, matching the PRD, decision log, addendum and ADRs; the round-2 patch is really applied. Every document correctly refuses to call the ignored local `TestResults` retained. The four `RV-*` residuals each carry an owner, a release-claim impact and closure evidence. The File List is otherwise accurate — all 121 entries exist on disk, and the EventStore gitlink is disclosed in three places.

**The problem that remains.** These documents ratify, retire and publish against a single run, `01KYYQ42B23EHHD8KS542W1MAF`, that was produced before the round-2/round-3 remediation and can never be replayed through the gate this same change ships. Underneath that, the deferral retirements and the NFR41/NFR58/NFR59 numbers rest on measurement paths the project has already ruled unfalsifiable and left open — so the governance layer is further ahead of the evidence than at any prior round, while the story is still `in-progress`.

#### Review decisions (round 4, delegated to the reviewer 2026-08-01)

Jerome delegated all four; these are recorded as the reviewer's judgement, not Jerome's. The resulting edits are carried as patch work below and are **not yet applied**.

1. **Evidence citation (D1) — strip the run id and every measured figure; keep the fact of the diagnostic.** The cited bundle predates the current manifest contract, is not replayable through the shipped gate, and stamps a commit whose tree contains no recovery drivers. Re-running now would produce evidence that goes stale again the moment the round-2 decision-1/decision-2 rework lands; labelling it leaves five documents quoting numbers the gate refuses. The governance documents keep the *mechanism* description and the provisional status, and state plainly that a pre-remediation local diagnostic passed the gate **as it existed at v1.1**, that the bundle predates the current manifest contract, and that no figures are published from it.
2. **Retirement scope (D2) — narrow to what is genuinely proven, rather than blanket re-open.** The live-driver deferral retirement **stands** for `eventstore-outage`, `identity` and `graph`: those inject real faults at real boundaries through allowlisted Aspire resource commands and a real token-acquisition/Worker path. It **does not stand** for `ai-provider`, `command-execution`, `audit-store` and `attachment-processing`, whose safety flags are structurally constant and whose consumers are bypassed. Independently of dependency, the **NFR58 observed-scope** claim and the **NFR41 latency** claim are re-opened for all six (the monitor is a second copy of the expectation table, and both time bounds are minted in one process — this is worse, not better, for identity/graph, where the one honest timestamp is overwritten). For the rebuild, the **store round-trip is real and stays retired**; the **equivalence/divergence claim is re-opened** (identity-copy write, no translator, one `SourceRecords` instance on both sides). The NFR41 addendum row is withdrawn, which also resolves the `scoped-outage-degradation-validation.md:263` ↔ `addendum.md:127` contradiction.
3. **A10 branch (D3) — add the fifth branch rather than pretend the outcome fits.** The story's "A10 decision semantics" gains a fifth documented outcome, *"Remain provisional — a passing run without a retained hosted locator"*, with its exit condition (a complete passing scheduled-CI or release artifact plus its locator recorded in the decision log), its owner (Architecture/DevOps), and an explicit **un-ratification** transition covering locator expiry at the 30-day artifact boundary, a later required run that is not passing, and a hosted `Unmeasurable`/structural-breach verdict. `epics.md` Story 12.15 AC2 is amended to admit that branch — amending a canonical AC is a deliberate governance act, flagged here because AC2 as written ("confirmed as commitments or revised downward") is unsatisfiable by a sandbox lane and would otherwise force either a false confirmation or a target change made on sandbox-only evidence.
4. **ADR status (D4) — mark `Proposed`, relax the guard.** `Accepted` is premature while the story is `in-progress` with eight open decisions and while decision 2 shows the ADR's own matrix describes seams that are not built as described. `LiveRecoveryValidationArchitectureTests.cs:12` is relaxed to accept either status so the ADR's state can follow the engineering state instead of being pinned by a test — the guard as written would break the build on the correct fix.

#### Review remediation applied (2026-08-01, round 4)

**All four decisions and all 17 patches applied and verified.** `UseHexalithProjectReferences=true dotnet build Hexalith.ChatBot.slnx --no-restore -m:1` passed at **0 warnings / 0 errors**, and every affected suite is green: Architecture **72/72**, Server **1730/1730**, Conformance **98/98**, Contracts **484/484**, AppHost **14/14**, Client **40/40**, Integration **99 passed / 5 skipped / 0 failed** (three consecutive clean runs). The live Tier-3 test remains skipped in this environment and was **not** executed.

One integration run in this pass showed `Failed: 1, Passed: 98` before three consecutive `Failed: 0, Passed: 99` runs on identical code — the exact signature of the deferred `TierThreeParallelValidationShouldRetainEveryNonCancellationSiblingFailure` flake, unchanged and still out of scope.

Two changes are worth a second look because they touch code rather than documents:

- **The gate's ceiling disclosure was widened, not just documented.** `LiveRecoveryValidationEvidenceGate.cs:291` previously emitted `target_exceeds_measurable_ceiling` only for the `rto` key. It now emits for any key whose canonical target exceeds the lane's ceiling, so an NFR57 rebuild pass — measured against the same 4-hour target inside the same 180-second-bounded lane — carries the limitation too. This changes gate output on every future run; it is additive to a non-blocking channel, so no verdict flips.
- **The ADR status guard was relaxed rather than re-pinned.** `LiveRecoveryValidationArchitectureTests.cs:12` asserted the literal `"Accepted (2026-08-01, Story 12.15)"`, which meant the correct fix (downgrading to `Proposed`) broke the build — a guard that locked in the defect it should have caught. It now accepts either `Proposed` or `Accepted` for Story 12.15 and leaves the architecture invariants below it untouched.

**Story stays `in-progress`.** The 14 open round-2 items (8 `[Decision]`, 6 `[Patch]`) are untouched by this round and remain the gate on `done`. Round 4's contribution is that the governance layer no longer claims more than those items can support: with the measured figures withdrawn and the retirements narrowed, the documents and the code now describe the same system.

A residual could be silently dropped from evidence while the ADR and PRD still assert it open. Belongs with the chunk-2 gate-anchoring work already accepted in round 3. [`src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Audit/LiveRecoveryValidationEvidenceGate.cs:86-149`]
- [x] [Review][Defer] The four `RV-*` entries' "Release-claim impact: do not claim …" constraints have no enforcement point — deferred, pre-existing. Nothing in the release workflow, gate, or release-notes path checks that prohibited disaster-recovery claims are absent; the constraint lives only as ledger prose. This is a process gap that predates Story 12.15's ledger format and needs a named owner rather than a code change here. [`_bmad-output/implementation-artifacts/deferred-work.md:50-53`]

#### Dismissed as noise (5)

(1) "`prd.md:1457` NFR56 still reads as an unqualified commitment" — NFR56 never carried the `[ASSUMPTION]` marker inline in `prd.md`; the A10 row and the two M2 bullets carry the qualification, and both moved. (2) "Manifest `daprVersion: 1.18.1` contradicts the pinned `1.18.4`" — already dismissed in round 2 for the same reason: the story pins the Dapr *.NET SDK*, the manifest records the measured *runtime*. (3) "The PRD A10 row now carries implementation detail (run ULID, SDK versions, dataset version) that will rot" — Task 8 explicitly requires an evidence-linked rationale; the traceability is the point. (4) "Aspire 13.4.6 is a stale hardcoded provenance string" — `FileRecoveryValidationEvidenceSink.cs:160` now resolves it from the loaded assembly and it matches `Hexalith.ChatBot.AppHost.csproj`. (5) "`architecture.md:900` is stale" as a *substantive* error — the substance (not yet a commitment) is still true; only the wording drifted, folded into the `architecture.md`/`epics.md` patch above.

### Review round 5 — the remediation itself (2026-08-02)

Scope: the code the *reviewer* wrote while applying rounds 3 and 4 — the only surface no adversarial layer had seen. 53 files, +3,398/−158: the release evidence gate and its policy/manifest/options/tenant-policy/vocabulary types, the three coordinator retention paths, the new `live-recovery-evidence-gate` jobs in both workflows, the strict-enum client policy, the AppHost realm/Dapr changes, and their tests. Three adversarial layers; every finding below was re-verified against source by the reviewer before rating, and two layer claims were rejected on that re-verification (see Dismissed). The 14 open round-2 items were excluded from all three layer prompts and are untouched by this round.

**Credit where due, verified against source rather than claim.** Round 3's decision 1 is genuinely delivered: `live-recovery-evidence-gate` exists in both workflows, chains via `needs:`, downloads only the uploaded artifact, and evaluates against a fresh `DateTimeOffset.UtcNow`, so the staleness branches are reachable; `semantic-release` depends on the gate, not on the run that produced the evidence. Allowed targets really are anchored to `RecoveryTargets` (`LiveRecoveryValidationEvidenceGate.cs:203-220`), the per-job measurement vocabulary fails closed on absence (`:274-280`), cross-manifest tenant/dataset/commit coherence is enforced (`:141-149`), and `TargetDeviationsBlockRelease`/`ConfiguredProjectionDatasets`/`RequiredDriverMode`/`MaximumEvidenceAge` did move out of the run's envelope into `LiveRecoveryValidationGatePolicy`. `ReplayTenantPolicy` rejects the bare prefix, `ScopedOutageDependencies.BuildClosedSet` rejects duplicates, the dataset descriptor compares the configured partition, all three coordinators downgrade a retention failure to `unmeasurable` with a distinct deviation token, and the manifest ceiling is finite-checked. The File List matches `git diff --name-only` exactly for every audited path — 0 omissions, 0 phantom entries, the first round in which that has been true.

**The problem that remains.** The gate is now structurally independent of the run, and that was the right fix — but independence only matters if the gate reads the evidence. It consults 6 of the 20 assertions the sink writes, so `fault-observed: false` is invisible to it; and the job that runs it exits 0 when its filter matches nothing. Both defects point the same way: the release path can report a pass without the gate having evaluated anything.

#### Review decisions (round 5, delegated to the reviewer 2026-08-02)

Jerome delegated all four; these are recorded as the reviewer's judgement, not Jerome's. Each resolves into patch work carried below.

1. **Assertion vocabulary (D1) — all fourteen become stop-ship, keyed per job, failing closed on absence as well as falsity.** This needed less judgement than it appeared. Task 6 (`:97`) defines the *deviation* bucket exhaustively — continuity RPO/RTO misses, rebuild duration misses, late scope recording — and all three are measurement keys already handled through `MeasurementsSeconds` and the canonical-target comparison. Not one of the fourteen unread assertions is a measurable target miss: `fault-observed`, `recovery-observed`, `measurable` and `state-reconstructable` say the exercise happened at all, and `tenant-isolation-preserved`, `control-tenant-isolated`, `unauthorized-mutation-absent`, `cross-tenant-leakage-absent`, `silent-data-loss-absent`, `duplicate-side-effect-absent`, `inflight-items-recoverable`, `independent-control-succeeded`, `immutable-source-only` and `mailbox-reingestion-absent` are NFR57/NFR59 safety invariants. The story already rules on both categories — "An unmeasurable result is always a breach signal" (`:36`) — so they are structural breaches, not deviations. The required set is declared per job exactly as `CanonicalTargetsFor` declares measurement keys, so a manifest that omits a name fails closed rather than passing on absence.
2. **Release concurrency (D2) — key by `github.sha`, and correct the comment and the guard.** The lane runs on an ephemeral GitHub-hosted runner and composes its own Aspire topology in-process, so there is no shared resource that serialization protects — the group buys only cost control, and it pays for that by letting GitHub cancel a *pending* required check. For a required release gate that trade is backwards: every commit that could publish must get its own verdict, and a silently cancelled check is the one failure mode nobody sees. Keying by `github.sha` gives each commit an independent group while still collapsing a re-dispatch of the same commit. The cost is explicit and accepted: N pushes inside the window now run N concurrent 5.5-hour jobs instead of queueing. The comment at `release.yml:82-86` and the architecture guard that pins the ref-keyed string are corrected to describe what the group actually does.
3. **Timeout constraint set (D3) — fix the contradiction; do not invent a floor.** Round 2's reasoning against a `MinimumMeasurableRecoveryCeiling` still stands, so `RestorationTimeout` gains no lower bound. The genuine defect is the opposite rule: `PerScenarioTimeout >= MaxRto` (`:135`) demands a 4-hour per-scenario budget that nine serial scenarios can never afford inside a sub-5.5-hour `WorkflowTimeout`, so it encodes a promise the lane cannot honour and is silently truncated by the workflow deadline. It is replaced by `PerScenarioTimeout <= WorkflowTimeout / MinimumSweepScenarioCount`, mirroring the fair-share rule already applied to `RestorationTimeout`, so a per-scenario budget the sweep cannot afford is rejected at validation instead of at runtime. `MeasurableRecoveryCeilingSeconds` remains the honest published statement of what the lane can measure, and the D1 vocabulary plus the policy ceiling bound (patch 2) stop a manifest from concealing it.
4. **Artifact kinds (D4) — amend the obligation to two kinds and carry the gap as an owned residual.** `RecoveryValidationEvidenceManifest.cs:157`'s comment is right that requiring locators did not make the artifacts exist; the error was leaving Task 2 and obligation `:39` asserting six. Following the round-1 precedent for the Task 7 checkbox, the task text and the obligation are narrowed to `test-output` and `reports`, the narrowing gets a Change Log line, and the three stale six-locator fixtures are corrected. Wording surgery alone would be dishonest, so a new `RV-EVIDENCE-KINDS` residual records the missing logs/traces/metrics/state-store-end-state retention with an owner and its release-claim impact, per Task 8's "one durable, owned entry per remaining gap".

#### Decision findings (round 5)

- [x] [Review][Decision] **The independent gate discards 14 of the 20 assertions it retains, including every "the fault actually happened" check** — `LiveRecoveryValidationEvidenceGate.cs` calls `Assertion(...)` on exactly six names: `cleanup-complete` (`:266`), `data-loss-absent` (`:366,381`), `structurally-equivalent` (`:369`), `scope-contained` (`:372`), `duration-within-target` (`:382`), `scope-recorded-within-target` (`:383`). `FileRecoveryValidationEvidenceSink.cs:41-128` writes 20. Repo-wide grep confirms **zero readers** outside that sink for `fault-observed`, `recovery-observed`, `state-reconstructable`, `tenant-isolation-preserved`, `unauthorized-mutation-absent`, `measurable`, `immutable-source-only`, `mailbox-reingestion-absent`, `independent-control-succeeded`, `control-tenant-isolated`, `cross-tenant-leakage-absent`, `silent-data-loss-absent`, `inflight-items-recoverable`, `duplicate-side-effect-absent`. Failure scenario: a driver regression returns without stopping the Aspire resource — `FaultObserved=false`, `RecoveryObserved=false`, RPO/RTO ≈ 0, no data loss ⇒ verdict `met`, `data-loss-absent=true`, `cleanup-complete=true`; the gate returns zero stop-ship reasons and the release ships on evidence that no outage was ever injected. NFR57's defining properties (`immutable-source-only`, `mailbox-reingestion-absent`) and the NFR59 safety flags are recorded and never gated. Round 3's decision 2 closed exactly this hole for *measurement keys* — the code comment at `:271-273` says so — while leaving the assertion vocabulary open. Latent today (the drivers throw before producing such a report), but the gate exists precisely to judge artifacts it did not produce. Decision needed on the blocking partition: which assertions are stop-ship, which become target deviations, and which are informational. [`src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Audit/LiveRecoveryValidationEvidenceGate.cs:266,363-385`, `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/Recovery/FileRecoveryValidationEvidenceSink.cs:41-128`]
- [x] [Review][Decision] **Release concurrency keyed by `github.ref` does not prevent the cancellation its own comment claims to fix** — `release.yml:87` sets `group: live-recovery-validation-release-${{ github.repository }}-${{ github.ref }}` with `cancel-in-progress: false`, and the comment at `:82-86` states this stops "a third push inside the 5.5-hour window" from cancelling the older pending required check. Every push to `main` carries the same `github.ref`, so all three pushes still share one group and GitHub still cancels the older *pending* run; keying by ref separates only *different* release branches (`main`/`next`/`alpha`/`beta`). `LiveRecoveryValidationArchitectureTests.cs:178` pins the ineffective group string as if it were the remedy. Direction of failure stays safe (a release is skipped, never falsely passed). Decision needed because the fix is a real trade-off: key by `github.sha` and give up serialization of the destructive lane against itself, gate the lane to publishing branches, or accept and correct the comment and the guard. [`.github/workflows/release.yml:82-88`, `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.Architecture.Tests/LiveRecoveryValidationArchitectureTests.cs:174-178`]
- [x] [Review][Decision] **`RestorationTimeout` is unbounded below against the target it gates, and the timeout constraint set is internally unsatisfiable** — `LiveRecoveryValidationOptions.Validate()` bounds `RestorationTimeout` only from above (`:98` positive, `:140` `< PerScenarioTimeout`, `:161` `< WorkflowTimeout/9`). `RestorationTimeout = 1s` validates clean, and since it is what actually cancels a slow recovery *and* what is published as `MeasurableRecoveryCeilingSeconds`, every drill then reports `met` (instant) or `unmeasurable` — never `missed`. Separately the set cannot be satisfied as intended: `:135` demands `PerScenarioTimeout >= MaxRto` (4 h) "to permit measurement through the recovery target", while `:151` forces `WorkflowTimeout < RunnerBudget` (5.5 h) — nine serial scenarios at ≥4 h each need ≥36 h. Decision needed because round 2 deliberately **removed** an invented `MinimumMeasurableRecoveryCeiling` floor as "a threshold nobody had agreed to" (`:231`), so re-introducing one is Jerome's call, as is whether `PerScenarioTimeout >= MaxRto` should survive at all given it can never hold for the whole sweep. [`src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Audit/LiveRecoveryValidationOptions.cs:98,135-137,140,151,160-163`]
- [x] [Review][Decision] **The manifest's required raw-artifact vocabulary was narrowed from six kinds to two, undisclosed** — `RecoveryValidationEvidenceManifest.cs:157` sets `requiredArtifactKinds = ["test-output", "reports"]` with the honest comment *"Requiring logs/traces/metrics/state-end-state did not make them exist."* Task 2 (`:71`, marked `[x]`) requires manifests to link "raw CI artifacts, logs, traces, metrics, state-store end-state, and test output", and binding obligation `:39` requires CI to upload "the raw `.trx`/logs/traces/metrics". Neither workflow produces logs, traces or metrics — both upload `TestResults` only. No Change Log entry records the narrowing, and three test files still carry stale six-locator fixtures. Decision needed: emit the missing artifact kinds, or amend the Task 2 checkbox and obligation `:39` and disclose the narrowing — amending a checked task is a governance act, which is why this is not a silent patch. [`src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Audit/RecoveryValidationEvidenceManifest.cs:157`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:147-154`, `.github/workflows/release.yml:118-127`]

#### Patch findings (round 5)

- [x] [Review][Patch] A renamed or moved gate test turns the required release gate into a silent no-op — `dotnet test --filter "FullyQualifiedName=…"` exits **0** when the filter matches nothing, and no `TreatNoTestsAsError`, `--minimum-expected-tests` or runsettings equivalent exists anywhere in the repository (verified; the projects use `Microsoft.NET.Test.Sdk` + `xunit.runner.visualstudio`, i.e. VSTest, not MTP). Rename `RetainedLiveRecoveryEvidenceShouldPassTheReleaseGateOutOfProcess` or move its namespace and the gate job still checks out, still downloads the artifact, runs zero tests, exits 0, reports success, and `semantic-release` publishes with the authoritative gate never evaluated. `LiveRecoveryValidationArchitectureTests.cs:189` asserts the literal FQN appears in the YAML, not that a test with that FQN exists, so it stays green through the rename. Identical exposure on the producer filter. [`.github/workflows/release.yml:170`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml:195`, `release.yml:117`, `ci.yml:143`] — HIGH
- [x] [Review][Patch] The gate policy pins the dataset *name* but not how much was exercised, and the manifest declares its own measurable ceiling — `LiveRecoveryValidationGatePolicy` carries only `ConfiguredProjectionDatasets`, `TargetDeviationsBlockRelease`, `RequiredDriverMode`, `MaximumEvidenceAge`. `DatasetVersion`, `DatasetVolume`, `ProjectionSchemaVersion`, the tenant and `RepositoryCommit` are checked only for mutual consistency across manifests (`:141-149`), and `MeasurableRecoveryCeilingSeconds` is producer-declared. Shrink `recovery-baseline-v1.json` to one record and set `DatasetVolume = 1`: every coordinator passes trivially, coherence holds, and the gate passes; inflate the ceiling and the claim-limitation disclosure is suppressed. Add expected dataset version/volume and a `RequiredRepositoryCommit` (from `github.sha`) plus a ceiling upper bound to the policy. - [x] [Review][Patch] The round-4 ceiling widening over-fires on two keys whose targets the restoration budget does not bound — `:295` now compares **every** canonical target against `MeasurableRecoveryCeilingSeconds`, which is defined as the *restoration* budget. At the lane's 180 s that fires for `rpo` (900 s) and `scope-recording-latency` (300 s) as well as `rto` and `rebuild-duration`. RPO is a data-loss window and NFR41 latency is measured during the outage — neither is bounded by restoration, so two category-error claim limitations are emitted on every future passing run. Round 4's ask was "any key whose canonical target exceeds **the lane's reachable budget**". Compounding: `RecoveryTimeMeasurementKey = "rto"` (`:10`) now has zero references repo-wide and its doc-comment (`:6-9`) still asserts rto is "the only one whose target can exceed what a lane is structurally able to measure", contradicting the code 285 lines below; `LiveRecoveryValidationEvidenceGateTests.cs:172-180` asserts only the `rto` token, so it passes identically against the pre-widening implementation. `deferred-work.md:11` also describes the gap as two keys, not four. [`src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Audit/LiveRecoveryValidationEvidenceGate.cs:6-10,295`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] Both workflows give both destructive jobs identical `name:` values, so a required-check name is ambiguous and the CI copy is skipped on every push — `ci.yml:103` and `release.yml:79` are both `required serialized live recovery evidence gate`; `ci.yml:159` and `release.yml:133` are both `required independent live recovery evidence gate`. Branch protection matches required checks by job name, and the `ci.yml` pair is gated to `schedule`/`workflow_dispatch`, so on every push and PR that copy is skipped — which GitHub can report as satisfying the required check. Rename the CI copies. [`.github/workflows/ci.yml:103,159`, `.github/workflows/release.yml:79,133`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] The secret-bearing rendered realm is created world-readable and only then chmod'd, in a PID-keyed path — `Directory.CreateDirectory` (0755 under default umask) and `File.WriteAllText` (0644) both complete before `File.SetUnixFileMode` narrows them to 0700/0600, leaving the client secret readable by any local user in that window, which is exactly the shared-build-host threat the comment at `:236-237` names. The path `{TempPath}/hexalith-chatbot-keycloak/{ProcessId}` is predictable and the write is not `FileMode.CreateNew`, so a pre-created symlink redirects it; the `ProcessExit` cleanup never runs on SIGKILL, and a recycled PID whose 0700 directory survives under another owner makes startup throw `UnauthorizedAccessException`. Use `Directory.CreateTempSubdirectory()` plus `FileStreamOptions { Mode = FileMode.CreateNew, UnixCreateMode = UserRead | UserWrite }`. Charset is validated but length/entropy is not (`:212-225`). [`src/Hexalith.ChatBot.AppHost/Program.cs:212-260`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] Every manifest publishes an artifact locator naming an artifact neither workflow produces — `LiveContinuityAspireE2eTests.cs:137` sets `EvidenceLocator = "artifact:live-recovery-validation"`, stamped into every manifest as `…/results.trx` and `…/reports`. The uploaded artifacts are `live-recovery-validation-evidence` (`ci.yml:151`) and `release-live-recovery-validation-evidence` (`release.yml:125`). `IsSafeArtifactLocator` is a syntax check only, and all three test fixtures use a third prefix (`artifact:live-recovery/…`), so nothing compares the locator to a workflow. A reviewer following a retained manifest to its raw evidence hits nothing. The file's own doc at `:24-25` claims it mirrors that discipline. #### Review remediation applied (2026-08-02, round 5)

**All four decisions and all 20 patches applied and verified.** `UseHexalithProjectReferences=true dotnet build Hexalith.ChatBot.slnx --no-restore -m:1` passed at **0 warnings / 0 errors**, and every affected suite is green: Server **1740/1740** (up from 1730 — the assertion vocabulary, policy anchoring, ceiling scoping, sweep-vocabulary and overflow guards), Architecture **73/73** (up from 72 — the `RunnerBudget`/`Cadence` workflow agreement guard), Conformance **98/98**, Contracts **484/484**, AppHost **14/14**, Client **44/44** (up from 40 — the strict-enum dictionary and empty-string cases), Integration **99 passed / 5 skipped / 0 failed** across three consecutive runs. The live Tier-3 test remains skipped in this environment and was **not** executed, so none of these changes has live-topology proof.

One run in this pass showed `Failed: 1, Passed: 98` before three consecutive clean runs on identical code — the deferred `TierThreeParallelValidationShouldRetainEveryNonCancellationSiblingFailure` flake recorded at `:219`, unchanged and still out of scope.

Four changes are worth a second look because they alter behaviour rather than only hardening it:

- **The per-scenario timeout rule inverted.** `PerScenarioTimeout >= RecoveryTargets.MaxRto` is gone, replaced by `WorkflowTimeout >= (PerScenarioTimeout × MinimumSweepScenarioCount) + 30 min`. The old rule read as a measurement guarantee but could not hold: nine serial scenarios at four hours each need thirty-six, against a `WorkflowTimeout` bounded below `RunnerBudget` (5.5 h). Consequently the lane's configured `PerScenarioTimeout` moved from 4 h to **25 minutes** — a budget the sweep can actually afford — in the Tier-3 harness and four unit fixtures. `RestorationTimeout` still has no lower bound, deliberately: round 2 removed an invented floor and that reasoning stands. `MeasurableRecoveryCeilingSeconds` remains the honest published statement of what the lane can measure.
- **The claim limitation narrowed rather than widened.** Round 4 extended `target_exceeds_measurable_ceiling` from `rto` to every key; round 5 scopes it to `rto` and `rebuild-duration`. RPO is a data-loss window and NFR41 latency is measured *during* the outage, so neither is bounded by the restoration budget — comparing them against it emitted a category error on every passing run. Net effect on a future run: two fewer limitation tokens, both of which were wrong.
- **Release concurrency stopped serializing.** Keying by `github.sha` means N pushes inside the window now run N concurrent 5.5-hour jobs rather than queueing. That cost is accepted deliberately: keying by ref did not prevent the pending-cancellation it was added for, and a required check GitHub can cancel is worse than a concurrent one. There is no shared resource to protect — each run composes its own topology on an ephemeral runner.
- **`Hexalith.ChatBot.Server` grants `InternalsVisibleTo` to `Hexalith.ChatBot.Architecture.Tests`.** Needed so the new guard can compare workflow YAML against the actual `RunnerBudget`/`Cadence` *values* rather than against the option source text — the failure mode this round criticised elsewhere. Consistent with the four existing test-assembly grants.

**Story stays `in-progress`.** The 14 open round-2 items (8 `[Decision]`, 6 `[Patch]`) are untouched by this round and remain the gate on `done`. Round 5's contribution is that the machinery which decides whether a release ships can no longer report a pass without having evaluated anything.

#### Dismissed on re-verification (2)

(1) "`ScopedOutageDependencies.All = BuildClosedSet()` throws from a static field initializer, poisoning the type with `TypeInitializationException` for the process lifetime rather than failing the build" — the input is a compile-time-constant list of six distinct literals, so the throw cannot fire at runtime; it is a deliberate closed-set guard, and the missing coverage is folded into the guards/tests patch above. (2) "The coordinators' retention catch is filtered on `cancellationToken is { IsCancellationRequested: false }`, so a cancellation during retention discards the substituted `unmeasurable` report and the scenario is never audited or alerted" — verified, but the resulting artifact gap makes the gate emit `{job}:missing_evidence`, i.e. the failure direction is stop-ship. The filter is deliberate and documented at `ContinuityDrillCoordinator.cs:192-197`.

### Review Findings — chunk 1a live drivers (2026-08-02)

Scope: six files only — `LiveContinuityDrillScenarioRunner(.cs/.Tests.cs)`, `LiveProjectionRebuildDriver(.cs/.Tests.cs)`, `LiveScopedOutageInjectionDriver(.cs/.Tests.cs)`. Four adversarial layers (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Verification Gap, Acceptance Auditor). Aspire ops, RecoverySandbox, CI, and governance are out of this chunk.

**Credit where due.** Source-email rebuild now runs through `AssociationProjectionHandler` (round-2 decision 2 half-met); observed non-recovery is no longer coerced into a throw (round-2 patch on `InflightItemsRecoverable`); cleanup outcomes are patched into `ExecutionAssertions.CleanupComplete` after the real cleanup step; scoped-outage scope is a pass-through with a regression test that feeds a mismatched observed scope; restoration/cleanup `finally` shapes are present on all three drivers.

- [x] [Review][Decision] Continuity no-loss RPO is still a literal `TimeSpan.Zero` on every passing run — **Resolved 2026-08-02 (Administrator): option 2 — keep Zero on no-loss.** Classic RPO semantics: no detected loss ⇒ `MeasuredRpo = 0`. Passing-run `0s` must not be cited as A10 confirmation of the ≤15 min budget; only a loss-path measurement (or a separately owned drill) may. Follow-on patches: document that semantics on the runner; add a subscription committed-before-outage checkpoint for the loss-path RPO bound; governance chunk must stop citing green-run `0s` as A10 proof. [`LiveContinuityDrillScenarioRunner.cs:103-105,203-210`]
- [x] [Review][Decision] Projection rebuild still fabricates several load-bearing evidence fields after the handler rework — **Resolved 2026-08-02 (Administrator): option 2 — narrow the claims.** Only claim what the `AssociationProjectionHandler` source path proves (source digests / equivalence). Drop or relabel unobserved fields (`TenantIsolationPreserved` name-tautology, hardcoded safety flags, dataset-constant schema pair as “observed schema”, WORM identity-write equivalence). WORM rebuild fidelity becomes an owned residual. Follow-on patches below; ADR/PRD claim narrowing belongs with the governance chunk. [`LiveProjectionRebuildDriver.cs:113-133,272-278`]

- [x] [Review][Patch] Document no-loss RPO = Zero semantics on the continuity runner (and ensure loss-path RPO still uses `OrderedDuration`); stop implying green-run `0s` measures the A10 budget in code comments / measurement construction. [`LiveContinuityDrillScenarioRunner.cs:103-105,203-210`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] Subscription continuity loss-path RPO must use a committed-before-outage checkpoint bound, not harness `startedAtUtc` — seed/checkpoint like EventStore (or an equivalent witnessed bound) before expire/observe. [`LiveContinuityDrillScenarioRunner.cs:146-210`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] Narrow projection rebuild claims to handler-proven source evidence — do not publish partition-name isolation, hardcoded `UnauthorizedMutationAbsent`/`ImmutableSourceOnly`/`MailboxReingestionAbsent`, dataset-constant schema-as-observed, or WORM identity-write as proven equivalence; keep source digest comparison as the structural proof.
