### Review Findings — chunk 1c RecoverySandbox (2026-08-03)

Scope: `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.RecoverySandbox/**` only (20 files / +1,313 lines). Four adversarial layers (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Verification Gap, Acceptance Auditor). Aspire ops (1b), drivers (1a), sink/contracts (1d), Server/AppHost, workflows, and governance are out of this chunk except where a sandbox HTTP contract forces a paired Aspire fix.

**Credit where due.** The four generic deps now flow through real orchestrator types (`AcceptedCommandDispatcher`, `ChatBotCommandAdmissionPipeline`, `AttachmentCaptureCoordinator`) rather than leaf-only stubs. `Restore` intentionally returns the pre-clear snapshot so dirty-boundary checks are not tautological. Scope mapping is keyed by `FaultSignalCode` rather than the injector dependency token. Controller auth uses SHA-256 `FixedTimeEquals`. Named `HttpClient` pooling avoids per-request socket exhaustion.

- [x] [Review][Decision] Sandbox `Restore` prior-state vs Aspire post-clear — **Resolved 2026-08-03 (Administrator): option 3 — composite Restore body (`prior` + `current`).** Sandbox returns both the pre-clear snapshot and the post-clear snapshot; Aspire reads `current.faulted` for cleanliness; dirty-boundary / tests can assert `prior`. Patch items below implement the shape and consumer adoption. [`RecoveryScopedOutageState.cs:44-58`, `RecoverySubscriptionSimulatorState.cs:24-37`, `AspireScopedOutageOperations.cs:355-370`, `AspireRecoverySandboxOperations.cs:622-632`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] Implement composite Restore JSON (`prior` + `current`) on scoped-outage and subscription Restore; update Aspire restore/cleanup/prepare callers to require `current.faulted == false` (and optionally assert `prior` when expecting a dirty clear); pin with host/unit tests. [`RecoveryScopedOutageState.cs:44-58`, `RecoverySubscriptionSimulatorState.cs:24-37`, `AspireScopedOutageOperations.cs`, `AspireRecoverySandboxOperations.cs`]

- [x] [Review][Patch] `RecordEffect` / `EffectCount` use a per-tenant `HashSet` of correlation ids, so same-correlation re-emission never grows the count; `DuplicateSideEffectDetected: effectsAfter - effectsBefore > 1` is unreachable and Aspire’s `effectCount != 1` duplicate check is neutralized. Count real emissions (or leaf submit/write/capture counts), not unique ids. [`RecoveryScopedOutageState.cs:71-101`, `RecoveryDependencyExercise.cs:93`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] `AdmitAsync` constructs a fresh `InMemoryCoarseIdempotencyStore` per call, so `ReplayPriorOutcome` branches are unreachable across sequential `/process` calls; “idempotent recovery” only exercises HashSet bookkeeping. Reuse one store for the exercise/host lifetime. [`RecoveryDependencyExercise.cs:342-348`] — also seal/`Abort` admissions so replay does not hang on an unresolved `PriorOutcome` TCS.
- [x] [Review][Patch] AI-provider exercise always dispatches through `RecoveryEventStoreGatewayClient`, which records effects under `"command-execution"` even when the AI outcome is `failed` — pollutes the command-execution ledger across scenarios (Restore for `ai-provider` does not clear it). Attribute submits to the exercised dependency, or skip command-execution effect accounting on the AI path. [`RecoveryEventStoreGatewayClient.cs:27-28`, `RecoveryDependencyExercise.cs:140-156`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] Named `HttpClient` `"chatbot-forward"` mutates `DefaultRequestHeaders.Authorization` per `/process` — concurrent Graph process requests race and can leak/overwrite bearer tokens. Set Authorization on the `HttpRequestMessage` only. [`Program.cs:101-103`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] `RecoveryScopeObservationMonitor.ExecuteAsync` calls `ScopeForSignal` with no try/catch; unrecognized `faultSignalCode` (accepted by `/scope-observation` after only a whitespace check) tears down the poll loop and leaves `RecordAsync` waiters hung until cancel. Validate the closed signal set at the endpoint and `TrySetException` (or skip) on bad dequeue. [`RecoveryScopeObservationMonitor.cs:52-66,76-86`, `Program.cs:221-233`] — caller-side delay+drain (PeriodicTimer hung under xUnit).
- [x] [Review][Patch] `recoverable = result.FaultObserved || result.EffectCount == 1` uses cumulative unique-id count, so a healthy multi-correlation tenant run reports `recoverable: false`. Use per-call delta / orchestrator commit. [`Program.cs:187`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] `ExerciseCommandDispatcherAsync` catches only `HttpRequestException` when faulted — any other exception from the faulted seam escapes and fails `/process`. Broaden the faulted-path catch (or wrap the gateway throw). [`RecoveryDependencyExercise.cs:182-190`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] `RecoveryMailboxConfigurationProvider` ignores `tenantId` / `notificationMailboxId` and always returns the Active allowlisted pattern — Worker never exercises unknown-mailbox / wrong-tenant rejection inside the sandbox. Match the configured mailbox (and reject otherwise). [`RecoveryMailboxConfigurationProvider.cs:10-17`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] `ControlledGraphMailboxMessageSource` emits fixed `InternetMessageId` / conversation / attachment identities across continuity vs graph lanes (only `ProviderMessageId` varies) — downstream identity collision risk. Derive stable ids from the notification/provider message id. [`ControlledGraphMailboxMessageSource.cs:20-35`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] `SeedAttachmentCandidateAsync` re-upserts attachments as `Pending` before every capture, wiping prior stored outcome so the attachment lane never probes coordinator/folder idempotency against an already-captured item. Seed once; leave stored state for replay. [`RecoveryDependencyExercise.cs:242-312`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] Startup accepts any non-empty `Recovery:TenantRef` — never calls `ReplayTenantPolicy.IsTestTenant`, so a misconfigured non-`replay-test:` tenant is allowed when the secret matches. Fail closed at startup. [`Program.cs:12-23`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] Attachment fault signaling hard-codes `"attachment_dependency_unavailable"` whenever `DegradedCount > 0 && StoredCount == 0`, mis-mapping other degradations to `workflow-item`. Thread the coordinator/source failure code. [`RecoveryDependencyExercise.cs:237-239`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] Controller-secret / wrong-tenant deny path has no behavioral test (architecture source-text pin only). Add an in-process host test that wrong secret / wrong tenant yields 404 and does not mutate fault state; pin Restore prior-state vs `/status` post-clear when decision 1 lands. [`Program.cs:239-250`]

### Review Findings — chunk 1d sink / dataset / contracts (2026-08-03)

Scope: 25 IntegrationTests Recovery leftover files (evidence sink, dataset, topology contracts, probes, capturing decorators, ops interfaces, DTOs, csproj). Four adversarial layers (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Verification Gap, Acceptance Auditor). Drivers (1a), Aspire ops/E2E (1b), RecoverySandbox (1c), Server/AppHost, workflows, and governance are out of this chunk except where a 1d contract forces a paired consumer fix.

**Credit where due.** Continuity vs scoped capturing wrappers exist so coordinator fail-closed reduction does not erase live diagnostics. Dataset Load rejects empty categories and volume mismatch. Evidence sink narrowed artifact locators to what workflows upload and publishes `MeasurableRecoveryCeilingSeconds`. Composer derives storage tenant from `ReplayTenantPolicy` rather than a second predicate. Gate-replay test is intentionally out-of-process against retained manifests.

### Review Findings — chunk live-drivers (2026-08-04)

Scope: 9 IntegrationTests live-driver files (`Capturing*`, `LiveContinuity*`/`LiveProjection*`/`LiveScoped*` drivers + tests, `LiveContinuityAspireE2eTests`) vs baseline `1493ff8f`. Four layers (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Verification Gap, Acceptance Auditor). ~2730 diff lines.

- [x] [Review][Decision] Continuity `Combine` prefers bare `OperationCanceledException` and drops a concurrent restoration failure — cancel during observe plus a failed EventStore restart surfaces as clean cancellation, is excluded from `CapturingContinuityDrillScenarioRunner`, and can leave EventStore stopped for the rest of the sweep. **Resolved (2026-08-04): option 3** — restore succeeded + observe canceled → keep bare OCE; restore failed (with or without cancel) → throw scenario failure carrying both causes so Capturing records it. Becomes patch below.
- [x] [Review][Patch] Continuity cancel+restore: if restoration failed, never return bare OCE — throw Aggregate/IOE with both injection and restoration causes; bare OCE only when restore succeeded. Update `Combine` + cancel unit tests (restore-ok vs restore-fail). [`LiveContinuityDrillScenarioRunner.cs:278-300`, `LiveContinuityDrillScenarioRunnerTests.cs`]
- [x] [Review][Decision] Task 4 skip-erase on rebuild failure vs stranded fresh-partition keys in the shared Dapr store — driver correctly preserves failed partitions for capture, but the Aspire E2E has no compensating cleanup after evidence retention, so `:rebuild:` keys can poison later runs. **Resolved (2026-08-04): option 1** — E2E `finally` erase after capture/assert; keep Task 4 skip-erase in the driver. Becomes patch below.
- [x] [Review][Patch] Aspire E2E: after rebuild evidence capture (success or failure), erase fresh-partition keys in a `finally`/cleanup helper; expose a shared key helper on `LiveProjectionRebuildDriver` so the formula is not duplicated. Do not erase before the sink has had its chance. [`LiveContinuityAspireE2eTests.cs`, `LiveProjectionRebuildDriver.cs`]
- [x] [Review][Decision] Mailbox admission probe treats `Accepted` as success for an empty `CaptureMailboxMessageIntake` body — proves admission past auth, but may enqueue real work. **Resolved (2026-08-04): option 1** — allow only `BadRequest`/`UnprocessableEntity`; use a payload that always fails validation after admission (never `Accepted`). Becomes patch below.
- [x] [Review][Patch] Mailbox admission probe: drop `Accepted` from the allowlist; send a post-admission-invalid payload so expected status is only `400`/`422`. [`LiveContinuityAspireE2eTests.cs:544-570`]

- [x] [Review][Patch] `LatestAttemptCompletedSuccessfully` ignores continuity `Missed` — early asserts allow `Missed > 0` while `attemptSucceeded` never requires `Missed == 0`, so a target-miss run can still publish `true` on the field the gate branches on first. [`LiveContinuityAspireE2eTests.cs:181-184,301-311`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] Continuity and projection outcome asserts run before the attempt summary is written — a divergent/unmeasurable rebuild (or a future continuity assert that throws) aborts with no summary, while scoped-outage deliberately writes the summary first. Move summary write ahead of those asserts and fold rebuild equivalence/`Missed` into `attemptSucceeded`. [`LiveContinuityAspireE2eTests.cs:181-239,327-348`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] `attemptSucceeded` requires `manifestFiles.Length == expectedEvidence` while reports only require `>=` — a legitimate sink Unmeasurable retry pair flips the summary to failed on an otherwise clean measurement sweep. [`LiveContinuityAspireE2eTests.cs:273-311`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] Continuity `IndependentControlSucceeded` is aliased to `TenantIsolationPreserved` for EventStore and subscription measurements — set `false` when unobserved (same honesty as `UnauthorizedMutationAbsent`) or thread a real independent-control observation. [`LiveContinuityDrillScenarioRunner.cs:130-131,236-237`]

- [x] [Review][Patch] Projection rebuild has no capturing decorator and no post-coordinator AggregateException rethrow — add a capturing wrapper (or equivalent) and fail-closed rethrow like continuity/scoped. [`LiveContinuityAspireE2eTests.cs:215-239`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] `EvidenceArtifactLocator` never validates with `IsSafeArtifactLocator` — reject unsafe `HEXALITH_CHATBOT_RECOVERY_EVIDENCE_ARTIFACT` values before manifests publish them. [`LiveContinuityAspireE2eTests.cs:392-396`]

### Review Findings — chunk integration-tests / ops-harness (2026-08-04)

Scope: 16 IntegrationTests Recovery ops-harness files (`Aspire*Operations`, `EventStoreDurableStateProbe`, interfaces/DTOs, `RecoveryAccessTokenProvider`, topology/route helpers, `RecoverySandboxContractTests`) vs baseline `1493ff8f`. Four layers (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Verification Gap, Acceptance Auditor). ~2572 diff lines.

- [x] [Review][Patch] **[HIGH] Subscription checkpoint and recovery reuse one coarse-idempotency identity.** Every continuity `process` call carries the same provider-message id; the checkpoint consumes that key, the Worker generates a fresh intake id for recovery, and the gateway replays the checkpoint's prior `202` without committing the fresh aggregate. `ReconcileSubscriptionAsync` therefore waits for an intake that cannot materialize. Give checkpoint and recovery distinct provider notifications, then reuse only the recovery notification for the duplicate probe. [`AspireRecoverySandboxOperations.cs:512-544,636-684`; `RecoverySandbox/Program.cs:115-145`; `CoarseIdempotencyComposer.cs:111-120`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[HIGH] EventStore cleanup cannot finish inside its caller's restoration budget.** A successful run performs one full one-minute fault-probe absence window plus three serial checkpoint absence windows plus one control absence window; the live runner supplies a three-minute cleanup token. Cancellation necessarily escapes before cleanup can attest completion. Run independent absence checks concurrently or give cleanup one explicit budget that covers its worst case. [`AspireRecoverySandboxOperations.cs:433-501,960-1010`; `LiveContinuityAspireE2eTests.cs:128-145`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[HIGH] Subscription cleanup requires the successfully reconciled append-only aggregate to become absent.** Reconciliation first proves `_reconciledIntakeRef` committed, then cleanup calls `RemainsAbsentAsync` for the same aggregate, so the happy path always reports `CleanupComplete: false`. Verify durable absence only for the duplicate-probe intake and verify read-model erasure for the reconciled intake. [`AspireRecoverySandboxOperations.cs:660-688,781-793`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[HIGH] Both RTO end bounds precede the required service/data recovery condition.** EventStore RTO ends at `/health` before `ReadEventStoreEndStateAsync`; subscription RTO uses the `process` response timestamp even though durable recovery, the full duplicate window, and sentinel/status checks occur later. Stamp recovery only after every defining end-state assertion passes. [`AspireRecoverySandboxOperations.cs:268-305,636-726`; `LiveContinuityDrillScenarioRunner.cs:87-119,213-225`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[HIGH] EventStore reconstruction is inferred from surviving projections, not EventStore durable state.** `ReadEventStoreEndStateAsync` counts pre-outage governed-operation read models, so total loss of the backing aggregate/event stream can still report every committed record reconstructable while stale projections survive. Probe the durable checkpoint set (or erase/rebuild projections from the recovered store) before asserting RPO/data-loss results. [`AspireRecoverySandboxOperations.cs:308-370`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[HIGH] Fault injection and observation can be attributed to the requested dependency without proving the injected command caused it.** EventStore and Identity accept a failed stop command when the endpoint is merely unavailable; Identity additionally maps its stop-deadline cancellation to `available = false`; EventStore fault observation converts transport/deadline failure into success without rechecking the target. Require a successful allowlisted command and a dependency-specific unavailable observation, preserving caller/deadline cancellation distinctly. [`AspireRecoverySandboxOperations.cs:167-265`; `AspireScopedOutageOperations.cs:159-200,664-674`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[HIGH] Corrupt or empty actor-state responses are classified as durable absence.** `ReadActorStateAsync` returns `null` for malformed JSON and empty successful bodies, allowing corruption to satisfy duplicate-absence and no-loss checks. Only 204/404 should mean absent; malformed successful responses must fail closed. [`EventStoreDurableStateProbe.cs:105-141`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[HIGH] Governed-operation absence accepts an own-tenant `403` as proof.** An authorization regression or expired/incorrect principal can therefore pass `UnauthorizedMutationAbsent`; for the owning tenant, only an explicit not-found response is absence. Treat `403` as a verification failure unless the probe intentionally uses a foreign principal. [`AspireRecoverySandboxOperations.cs:960-1010`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[HIGH] Tenant-isolation checks cannot detect new cross-tenant writes.** Equality of two fixed sentinel rows—and one foreign authorization read—only detects mutation/deletion of those exact keys. A recovery bug can create a new affected intake under the control tenant while every isolation flag remains clean. Snapshot and compare an enumerable tenant-owned key set/digest or probe the generated operation identity in both tenants. [`AspireRecoverySandboxOperations.cs:348-370,689-726`; `AspireScopedOutageOperations.cs:255-309,386-462`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[HIGH] The independent control operation is only an HTTP-202 smoke check and is never cleaned.** `IsChatBotControlAvailableAsync` discards the generated ids as soon as submission is accepted; it never proves EventStore/read-model end state, and `CleanupAsync` cannot erase the governed-note residue accumulated by every scoped scenario. Retain the ids, wait for durable/projected state, and erase/verify the generated read model. [`AspireScopedOutageOperations.cs:677-708`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[HIGH] Scoped cleanup attests `true` while deterministic read-model residue remains.** Identity/Graph/generic branches return hardcoded success without post-erase reads, scenario fields are not cleared, and Graph intake cleanup erases the attachment-set row but not the attachment-index row written alongside it. Compute completion from readback of every generated key (including the attachment index) and reset fields only after verification. [`AspireScopedOutageOperations.cs:489-596`; `ReadModelProjectConversationProjectionStore.cs:125-145`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[MEDIUM] Pre-injection cleanliness ignores the restore response's prior fault state.** Graph/generic checkpoints inspect only `current.faulted`; continuity subscription does not establish a prior-clean boundary. A dirty scenario silently cleared by checkpoint is treated as a clean independent exercise. Reject `WasPreviouslyFaulted` or perform and record an explicit sanitation phase before measurement. [`AspireScopedOutageOperations.cs:126-155`; `RecoverySandboxRestoreResponse.cs:23-33`; `AspireRecoverySandboxOperations.cs:548-581`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[MEDIUM] Missing Graph recovery is also labeled as a duplicate side effect.** `duplicate` includes `!recoveredAggregateStillCommitted`, conflating the independent silent-loss and duplication dimensions. Restrict duplicate to same-id or late duplicate commit. [`AspireScopedOutageOperations.cs:432-462`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[MEDIUM] Subscription `UnauthorizedMutationAbsent` is coupled to simulator call counts.** `failures >= 1 && submitted >= 2` can fail the authorization-integrity dimension because of baseline arithmetic or extra calls even when observed state is unchanged. Derive this flag solely from the relevant state/authorization observations. [`AspireRecoverySandboxOperations.cs:711-726`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[MEDIUM] Partial EventStore seeding cannot clean the rows it already committed.** Note refs are accumulated per iteration, but `_checkpointCorrelationId` is assigned only after all three succeed; cleanup rejects the partial state, and the runner swallows that cleanup failure before restoration has begun. Preserve cleanup metadata before the first mutation and allow partial cleanup. [`AspireRecoverySandboxOperations.cs:126-163,381-389`; `LiveContinuityDrillScenarioRunner.cs:59-64,138-153`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[MEDIUM] Durable presence/absence polling has mismatched bounds and a final-delay race.** Presence stops at 30 seconds while sibling probes allow one minute, and `RemainsAbsentAsync` returns after its final delay without one last read, so a boundary commit can false-pass absence. Share the configured budget, perform a final observation, and add a handler-backed delayed-commit test. [`EventStoreDurableStateProbe.cs:20-69`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[MEDIUM] Scoped availability helpers swallow caller cancellation.** Identity, control, and token probes catch every `TaskCanceledException` and return `false`, changing cancellation into a measured outage/control failure and corrupting provenance. Re-throw when the supplied token is cancelled. [`AspireScopedOutageOperations.cs:664-708,729-749`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[MEDIUM] Load-bearing sandbox guards lack focused executable coverage.** Add tests for default/equal/reversed/ordered scope stamps and for composer rejection of logical tenants whose derived storage/control tenant aliases `tenant-alpha`/`tenant-beta`. [`AspireScopedOutageOperations.cs:645-661`; `RecoverySandboxTopologyComposer.cs`; `RecoverySandboxContractTests.cs`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] **[LOW] Token retries create a new `HttpClient` every 500 ms and treat HTTP 408 as a permanent identity rejection.** Pool the client for the acquisition window and retry 408 with 429/5xx/transport failures. [`RecoveryAccessTokenProvider.cs:58-115`]

- [x] [Review][Defer] Identity/Graph NFR41 stamps still overwritten from post-hoc `/scope-observation` responses — deferred, already on ledger (sandbox self-timing / product-monitoring honesty). [`AspireScopedOutageOperations.cs:235-294`]
- [x] [Review][Defer] `SeedCommittedOperationAsync` records `LastCommittedAtUtc` as harness `UtcNow` after projection wait — deferred, entangled with green-path RPO / measurement-honesty residual. [`AspireRecoverySandboxOperations.cs:137-163`]

Four independent layers (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Verification Gap, Acceptance Auditor); 19 raw findings collapsed after triage against this story's ~17 prior review rounds. Most raw findings were re-discoveries of already-fixed or already-explicitly-deferred issues (e.g. `ProcessExit`-only realm-secret cleanup is already an accepted residual at line 564) or were not reachable given the current single-process-per-attempt architecture; only one genuinely new, actionable finding survived.

### Review Findings — chunked /bmad-code-review pass, chunk Tier-3 harness/RecoverySandbox (2026-08-05)

Scope: `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests` + `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.RecoverySandbox` (`1493ff8f` → `HEAD`), second of the multi-chunk pass (remaining: Server.Tests, StoryEvidenceGate tool + CI wiring). Four independent layers (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Verification Gap, Acceptance Auditor), each briefed on this story's dense prior review history to avoid re-litigating already-fixed ground. Two findings were traced through the real production code (not just the diff) before being accepted; one of the two decision candidates was reversed after that tracing and treated as a false positive rather than implemented.

- [x] [Review][Decision] **Cross-tenant leakage during the four topology-composed scoped-outage dependencies' actual fault window is structurally undetectable.** Sequence is `FaultAsync → ObserveFaultAsync → RestoreAsync → VerifyRecoveryAsync`; `RecoveryScopedOutageState.Restore` unconditionally clears the affected dependency's entire effect ledger (`_completedEffects.Remove(dependency)`) before `VerifyRecoveryAsync`'s `HasCrossTenantEffect`-based check ever runs, so a real cross-tenant write during the fault window — the highest-risk moment — is erased before it can be observed. Verified directly: `RecordEffect` is called from `RecoveryAuditWriter`/`RecoveryEventStoreGatewayClient`/`RecoveryAttachmentContentSource`/`RecoveryAiAssistanceProvider`, all invoked during `ObserveFaultAsync`, and `RestoreAsync` runs before `VerifyRecoveryAsync` in `LiveScopedOutageInjectionDriver.InjectAndMeasureAsync`. **Resolved (2026-08-05): check before Restore.** `RecoveryScopedOutageState.Restore` now takes an `expectedTenantRef` and captures `HasCrossTenantEffect` before clearing; the sandbox `/restore` response carries the new `crossTenantEffectDetectedBeforeRestore` field; `IScopedOutageSandboxOperations.RestoreAsync` now returns `ValueTask<bool>`; `LiveScopedOutageInjectionDriver` ORs that pre-restore signal into `CrossTenantLeakageDetected`/`TenantIsolationPreserved` alongside the existing post-restore check. [`tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.RecoverySandbox/RecoveryScopedOutageState.cs`, `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.RecoverySandbox/Program.cs:205`, `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/Recovery/IScopedOutageSandboxOperations.cs`, `AspireScopedOutageOperations.cs:345-379`, `RecoverySandboxRestoreResponse.cs`, `LiveScopedOutageInjectionDriver.cs`]
- [x] [Review][Decision] **The M365 duplicate-probe's `NoDuplicateSideEffects` check was suspected of being structurally unable to report a genuine pass** (both the reconcile and duplicate-probe `/process` calls stamp the identical `RecoveryNotificationIdentity.RecoveryPhase`, so the real `CoarseIdempotencyComposer` computes the same coarse key for both). **Reversed on deeper tracing — false positive, not implemented.** `GraphMailboxIntakeWorker.ToCommand()` mints a fresh random `MailboxMessageIntakeId` on every call regardless of provider-message identity, and its returned result always reports that locally-minted id — not anything echoed by the server. `ChatBotCommandAdmissionPipeline`'s `ReplayPriorOutcome` path (triggered by the coarse-idempotency conflict) replays the first call's cached response without durably committing anything under the second command's id. So `_duplicateProbeIntakeRef` and `_reconciledIntakeRef` differ by construction on every call (the `!string.Equals(...)` clause is not the load-bearing check it looked like), and the real signal — `RemainsAbsentAsync` on the never-committed duplicate id — correctly passes when server-side dedup suppresses the resubmission and correctly fails when it doesn't. The shared identity looks deliberate (exercising real dedup behavior), not an accidental collision. No code changed; recorded here so a future reviewer does not re-litigate this from the diff alone without re-tracing through `GraphMailboxIntakeWorker`/`ChatBotCommandAdmissionPipeline`.

- [x] [Review][Patch] Tenant-isolation probe (`ReconcileSubscriptionAsync`'s cross-tenant `GET` pair) folded any unexpected HTTP status (401/429/5xx) into "isolation failed" indistinguishable from a genuine leak, instead of failing closed on the ambiguous case — mirrors the already-fixed sibling pattern in `WaitForGovernedOperationAsync`. **Resolved:** both probes now throw on any status outside their expected set. [`tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/Recovery/AspireRecoverySandboxOperations.cs:418-443`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] `EventStoreDurableStateProbe.WaitForAggregateAsync` had no non-tolerant closing read, unlike its sibling `RemainsAggregateAbsentAsync` — a commit landing during the final poll delay could spuriously time out, and a persistent metadata/event-content inconsistency was swallowed into a generic `TimeoutException` instead of surfacing its real diagnostic. **Resolved:** added the same closing read; 2 new tests (`PresenceWaitPerformsFinalReadAfterBoundaryDelay`, `PresenceWaitsFinalReadIsNotToleratedFailsClosedWithTheRealDiagnostic`). [`tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/Recovery/EventStoreDurableStateProbe.cs:63-99`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] `FailedRebuildSkipsEraseSoTheFreshPartitionRemainsForCapture` never actually asserted the fresh partition survives — `readModels.RejectWrites = true` rejected every write, so zero fresh keys were ever written, and the presence-read loop discarded its own result (`_ = present;`). A regression that erased on every failure regardless would have shipped undetected by the one test named for exactly this property. **Resolved:** switched to `FailOnWriteNumber` (letting the first fresh-partition write land before failing the second) and asserted `anyFreshKeyPresent` is true; also extended `InMemoryRecoveryReadModelStore.TrySaveAsync` to honor `FailOnWriteNumber` (the rebuild path writes through the ETag-conditional path, which the knob didn't previously cover). [`tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/Recovery/LiveProjectionRebuildDriverTests.cs:60-113`, `InMemoryRecoveryReadModelStore.cs:80-98`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] `Combine()`'s restoration-only-failure path (injection/observation succeed, only restoration throws) was untested for both continuity scenarios. **Resolved:** 2 new tests (`RestorationOnlyFailurePreservesTheRestorationDiagnosticNotABareCancel`, `SubscriptionRestorationOnlyFailurePreservesTheRestorationDiagnosticNotABareCancel`) confirming the restoration exception survives as the inner exception rather than being replaced or discarded. [`tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/Recovery/LiveContinuityDrillScenarioRunnerTests.cs`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] Seed/checkpoint failure reaching `finally` cleanup (a regression here would strand real sandbox state with no cleanup attempt) was untested for both continuity scenarios. **Resolved:** 2 new tests (`SeedFailureStillReachesCleanupAndNeverInjectsTheFault`, `CheckpointFailureStillReachesCleanupAndNeverExpiresTheSubscription`). [`tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/Recovery/LiveContinuityDrillScenarioRunnerTests.cs`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] `TenantIsolationPreserved`'s negation of `CrossTenantLeakageDetected` in the scoped-outage driver was never asserted by any test — covered as a side effect of the cross-tenant-leakage-before-restore regression test above (`ACrossTenantEffectDetectedBeforeRestoreFailsIsolationEvenWhenThePostRestoreCheckReportsClean`).

**Dismissed as noise or already-resolved/deferred (~15):** RecoverySandbox app has no self-check on its own hosting environment (defense-in-depth only — the safety net is the test-only Aspire composer, and the app is never shipped/packaged); `RecoveryAuditWriter.RecordPostCommitAsync` aliases `RecordPreCommitAsync` (currently unreachable — audit-store exercise stops at admission by design); `LiveProjectionRebuildDriver.Digest` joins fields with `|` unescaped (real but the joined values come from a fixed, versioned baseline dataset, not attacker/caller input); `RecoveryDependencyExercise._seededAttachmentIntakes` unsynchronized (latent only — the sweep is currently serialized); `FileRecoveryValidationEvidenceSink` clamps `coverage` via `Math.Max` instead of throwing; `RecoveryFolderStore`/`RecoveryAttachmentContentSource` return fixed content (forward-looking observation, no current consumer); `correlationId` route validation is length-only; several narrow edge-case-hunter findings requiring multi-fault-simultaneous or malformed-Keycloak-response conditions with no realistic trigger path in this sandbox-only tool.

### Review Findings — chunked /bmad-code-review pass, chunk A: Server/Audit + AppHost + Client generated (2026-08-24)

Scope: `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Audit/*`, `Gateway/CommandGatewayServiceCollectionExtensions.cs`, `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.AppHost/*` (`Program.cs`, `ChatBotAspireModule.cs`, DAPR access-control, Keycloak realm), `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Client/Generated/*` (`1493ff8f` → `HEAD`), first of a fresh multi-chunk `/bmad-code-review` pass (remaining: IntegrationTests/Recovery harness, RecoverySandbox app, Server.Tests/Architecture.Tests/Client.Tests/Conformance.Tests, docs/ADRs/CI). Four independent layers (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Verification Gap, Acceptance Auditor) ran in parallel against the 2538/150-line, 40-file diff. Several Blind Hunter findings were traced through the actual production code (the generated client's static-`Lazy` settings cache, the Keycloak realm's established ULID-suffix convention, `RetainAsync`'s cancellation-filtered outer catch) and reversed as false positives rather than accepted at face value.

- [x] [Review][Decision] **Undisclosed unrelated DI registration bundled into an in-scope file.** `CommandGatewayServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:297` adds `services.TryAddSingleton<ICorrectionPropagationWorkflowStatusSink, OperationStatusWorkflowStatusSink>();` inside `AddChatBotCorrectionPropagation()`. All three types (`ICorrectionPropagationWorkflowStatusSink`, `NullCorrectionPropagationWorkflowStatusSink`, `OperationStatusWorkflowStatusSink`) were introduced by an unrelated commit (`1d02b69 build: sync local changes via /pushall`), are not in Story 12.15's File List, and the wiring line carries zero comment and no mention in the story's own "Files changed in this commit but out of Story 12.15's scope" disclosure (which only discloses the 42 regenerated screenshots). **Resolved (2026-08-24): keep + disclose.** `1d02b69` already landed on `main` (it sits between this story's baseline and its later commits, not before baseline), so reverting the line would manufacture a real regression — `DaprCorrectionPropagationCoordinator` consumers would silently fall back to the no-op `NullCorrectionPropagationWorkflowStatusSink` — purely to close a paperwork gap. No code changed; disclosure added below. Follow-up (non-blocking, not owned by this story): the correction-propagation status-sink wiring itself has never been through adversarial code review since it arrived via an automated sync commit rather than a reviewed story — flagged for the `DaprCorrectionPropagationCoordinator` owner. [`src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Gateway/CommandGatewayServiceCollectionExtensions.cs:297`]

- [x] [Review][Patch] `attempt_evidence_incoherent` release-gate coherence check ORs five conditions (TenantRef/DatasetRef/DatasetVersion/ConfiguredDatasetVolume/RepositoryCommit divergence) but only the TenantRef leg has a positive test; the RepositoryCommit test only asserts a case-only difference does NOT trip it. No test exercises DatasetRef/DatasetVersion/ConfiguredDatasetVolume divergence or a genuine RepositoryCommit mismatch — a regression dropping any of those four legs from the OR would pass every existing test. **Resolved:** added 4 parallel tests (`EvidenceWithADivergentDatasetRefIsNotOneCoherentAttempt`, `EvidenceWithADivergentDatasetVersionIsNotOneCoherentAttempt`, `EvidenceWithADivergentConfiguredDatasetVolumeIsNotOneCoherentAttempt`, `EvidenceWithAGenuinelyDifferentRepositoryCommitIsNotOneCoherentAttempt`); `LiveRecoveryValidationEvidenceGateTests` now 25/25. [`src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Audit/LiveRecoveryValidationEvidenceGate.cs:201-209`, `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server.Tests/Audit/LiveRecoveryValidationEvidenceGateTests.cs:207-260`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] No regression test guards the hardened Keycloak realm secret-file permissions. `Program.cs`'s `PrepareKeycloakRealmImport` (`UnixCreateMode = UserRead|UserWrite` applied atomically at file creation, a random temp-subdirectory name, a Windows owner-only ACL) fixes a real documented prior vulnerability ("leaving the literal client secret world-readable... on exactly the shared build host"), but `RecoveryValidationTopologyContractTests.cs` only tests the input-validation branches (missing/short/invalid-char secret) — nothing asserts the resulting file's permissions or path unpredictability, so a future refactor could silently drop `UnixCreateMode` with no test failing. **Resolved:** added `PrepareKeycloakRealmImportWritesTheRenderedRealmWithOwnerOnlyPermissionsAtAnUnpredictablePath`, asserting the generated file's `UnixFileMode` (non-Windows) and that its directory name isn't the fixed `{pid}` shape; `RecoveryValidationTopologyContractTests` now 23/23. [`src/Hexalith.ChatBot.AppHost/Program.cs:252-305`, `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/Recovery/RecoveryValidationTopologyContractTests.cs`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] `ValidateUniqueInternalGrpcPorts` is only invoked from `AddHexalithChatBot`, not from `AddEventStoreAdmin`, despite `SidecarAppIds`'s own doc comment claiming coverage "across AddHexalithChatBot and AddEventStoreAdmin... even though the EventStore Admin resources are composed by a separate call." Currently harmless (the sole production caller in `Program.cs` always calls `AddHexalithChatBot` before `AddEventStoreAdmin` on the same builder, and the check reads configuration directly rather than resource state), but any future composition root calling `AddEventStoreAdmin` alone silently loses port-collision protection for its two sidecars. **Resolved:** `AddEventStoreAdmin` now also calls `ValidateUniqueInternalGrpcPorts(builder.Configuration)`; `AppHostTopologyTests` (14/14) and `RecoveryValidationTopologyContractTests` (23/23) still green. [`src/Hexalith.ChatBot.AppHost/Aspire/ChatBotAspireModule.cs:129-153,265-276`]
- [x] [Review][Patch] Cleanup of the temp directory holding the rendered Keycloak realm (embedded plaintext client secret) relies solely on an `AppDomain.CurrentDomain.ProcessExit` handler, which does not fire on SIGKILL/OOM-kill/crash, and there is no startup sweep of stale `hexalith-chatbot-keycloak-*` directories left by a prior killed run. Note: the leaked directory and file remain owner-only (0700 / `UserRead|UserWrite`), so this is a disk-accumulation/hygiene gap rather than a confidentiality leak. **Resolved:** `PrepareKeycloakRealmImport` now sweeps `hexalith-chatbot-keycloak-*` directories older than 1 hour before creating its own — age-gated rather than unconditional, since this same function runs concurrently across many `DistributedApplicationTestingBuilder`-backed test classes in this repo's own suite and an unconditional sweep would race a concurrent run's still-live directory. Full `Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests` (176 total, 5 Tier-3-skipped) still green. [`src/Hexalith.ChatBot.AppHost/Program.cs:288-302`]

- [x] [Review][Defer] `StrictEnumContractResolver` doesn't cover an enum used as a dictionary KEY (only dictionary VALUE and single-level `IEnumerable` element are handled) and doesn't recurse into nested collections (e.g. `List<List<TEnum>>`). Already self-disclosed in the class's own comment ("Latent today... but nothing guarded the next regeneration") and confirmed inert — no property of either shape exists in the current generated client. [`src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Client/Generated/StrictEnumContractResolver.cs:38-53`] — deferred, pre-existing/self-disclosed residual, no live impact today.

**Dismissed as noise or false positive on tracing (~11):** wholesale `ContractResolver` replacement risking lost NSwag naming behavior (false — `settings.ContractResolver` was never set before this line, and `StrictEnumContractResolver : DefaultContractResolver` inherits default naming unchanged); resolver/converter allocated per-call as a perf regression (false — `HexalithChatBotClient.g.cs`'s `_settings` is a `static Lazy<JsonSerializerSettings>`, computed exactly once per process); `recovery-validation-mailbox-client`'s grant-id ULID claim value looking like an unswapped placeholder (false — it matches the realm's own established incrementing-suffix convention used for every client: `...FAV→FAW→FAX→FAY→FAZ→FB1→FB2`); `RetainAsync`'s inner unfiltered `catch (Exception)` silently swallowing cancellation (the outer catch already excludes real cancellation via `when (cancellationToken is { IsCancellationRequested: false })`; the inner catch is deliberate and documented, and the caller always still receives a stop-ship report carrying `EvidenceRetentionFailedDeviation` regardless); `RetainAsync` duplicated ~35-line block across 3 coordinators (real but minor DRY observation, not a defect); `LiveRecoveryValidationEvidenceGate.Evaluate` has no accompanying tests (false — `LiveRecoveryValidationEvidenceGateTests.cs` is 606 lines); `ConfiguredProjectionDatasets` list vs. single-scalar `LiveRecoveryValidationOptions` dataset + `MinimumSweepScenarioCount` hardcoded "+1" (speculative forward-compatibility observation, no demonstrated live defect); `accesscontrol.local.yaml` comment claiming sidecars "previously ran with no Dapr configuration at all" (unverifiable from the diff either way, no functional consequence); doc comments implying A10 evidence is already collected (false — wording is explicitly "provisional... not yet-discharged... pending a retained hosted run locator," consistent with AC3); `ContinuityDrillScenarios`/`ScopedOutageDependencies` throwing from a static initializer on a duplicate `SweepOrder` entry as a confusing `TypeInitializationException` (real test coverage already exists per the verification-gap layer; this is a developer-experience nitpick about a hand-edit typo, not a live defect); Keycloak realm test password in plaintext (matches the pre-existing repo convention for every other local-only sandbox test user, not a new practice).

**Dismissed as noise, already guarded, explicitly specified, or already-ledgered (12):** requiring `semantic-release` to depend directly on the producer contradicts the ratified independent-gate design and only adds a distinct teardown-status prerequisite; current-run artifact download is same-repository/read-only and the strict parser/provenance checks execute before any artifact content is trusted as evidence; the weekly cron/C# cadence has an executable architecture drift guard; DAPR's actual runtime version is captured into every manifest; the predecessor ADRs remain Accepted for their original stories while the Story 12.15 controlling ADR correctly stays Proposed; the epics' remain-provisional A10 branch is an explicit round-4 spec decision; existing residual-enforcement, JSON-size, and producer-summary findings are already owned in the ledger; the initial-commit `HEAD^` case has no reachable future trigger in this established repository; and several suggested shell/example variants were stylistic rather than defects.

### Review Findings — /bmad-code-review pass over the post-review delta (2026-08-25)

Scope: `865e5b3` → `HEAD` (42 files, +2,784/−161, `references/` excluded) — the work produced *after* the 2026-08-24 five-chunk pass, i.e. commits `b07a40f`, `bab0218`, `f804229`, `9af2c64` and merge `476f1de`. Four layers ran in parallel (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Verification Gap, Acceptance Auditor); no layer failed. Every finding below was re-verified against source by the reviewer before rating; one Edge Case Hunter finding was dismissed on verification.

#### Decision needed — all six resolved (Jerome, 2026-08-25)

Each finding below retains its full evidence; the **Resolution** line records the chosen direction. All six now carry patch work.

- [ ] [Review][Decision] **HIGH — The 60-minute current-run freshness bound and the 265-minute in-job recovery producer are mutually exclusive, so the completion lane self-invalidates.** `story-evidence-policy.json:5` sets `maximumCurrentRunAgeMinutes: 60`, hard-pinned to exactly `60` by `StoryEvidenceValidator.cs:385` (it cannot be raised without a policy version bump). `TrxEvidenceReader.ValidateRunTimes` (`TrxEvidenceReader.cs:518-521`) throws `EvidenceStaleOrUnbound` when `nowUtc - finishUtc > 60 min` for any `source == "current-run"` lane, and `nowUtc` is captured once at the top of `RunCi` (`Program.cs:145`) — which now executes *after* up to 280 wall-clock minutes of recovery production (`ci.yml:237-267`). All four other primary-path lanes are declared `current-run` in `story-evidence-policy.json` (`browser-primary`, `signalr-primary`, `hosting-assets-primary`, `aspire-dapr-primary`), as is the `story-evidence-gate` TRX produced earlier inside the same job (`ci.yml:140-146`). This story's own diff touches `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.AppHost/**`, which triggers `aspire-dapr-primary` — so the completion path Story 12.15 must produce is structurally unpassable whenever the drill uses a meaningful fraction of its budget. No test relates the two numbers: `ScaffoldArchitectureTests.cs:825/878/917` string-match each independently, and every `GateFixture` lane TRX is written seconds before evaluation. **Decision:** raise the bound with a policy version bump / re-produce the general current-run lanes after the recovery step / split recovery production into a separate job whose output is a retained rather than current-run source. Sources: blind-hunter+verification-gap. **Resolution (2026-08-25): per-lane ceiling + recovery first.** Give `recovery-primary` its own long current-run ceiling in its `recognizedLaneBindings` entry (it is structurally a multi-hour drill) and keep the global 60 minutes for the fast lanes; reorder the completion job so the recovery producer runs *ahead* of the fast-lane production, leaving those genuinely fresh at attestation. Bump `schemaVersion`/`minimumSupportedVersion` in both pinned places (`story-evidence-policy.json`, `StoryEvidenceValidator.cs:385`) and add a contract test relating the workflow producer budget to the ceiling. Rejected: raising the global bound (weakens staleness detection ~6x for lanes where 60 minutes is both meaningful and achievable) and re-running the fast lanes after recovery (doubles the suite inside an already-constrained job).
- [ ] [Review][Decision] **HIGH — A failed hosted recovery producer retains no machine evidence at all — precisely the state this story is blocked on.** The sanitize step (`ci.yml:270-276`) carries only `if: steps.artifacts.outputs.requires_recovery == 'true'`, so GitHub's implied `success()` skips it once the producer fails; `RecoveryTrxSanitizer.Sanitize` would throw on a non-`Passed` outcome anyway (`RecoveryTrxSanitizer.cs:76-88`); and the raw TRX is staged in `${{ runner.temp }}/raw-recovery-results` (`ci.yml:266`), which no upload path covers (`ci.yml:331-335` uploads only `story-evidence-reports`, `machine-results/recovery-primary` and `**/*.provenance.json`). A red 4.5-hour run therefore yields an empty `recovery-primary` directory and step logs. `review-adversarial.md` ADV-3's own required tightening was "upload only metadata-safe gate reports **and a sanitized failure summary**" — the summary half was never built, yet ADV-3 is marked CLOSED. **Decision:** build the sanitized failure summary ADV-3 specified / upload the raw TRX under `if: always()` and accept the payload-exposure tradeoff the sanitizer exists to prevent. Source: blind-hunter. **Resolution (2026-08-25): build ADV-3's sanitized failure summary.** Add a metadata-only failure-summary mode to the gate tool emitting outcome, counters, `Times` and `TestMethod` identity with no captured StdOut/StdErr, run it under `if: always()`, and upload it unconditionally. The strict pass-only sanitizer stays as-is for the completion evidence path. This closes ADV-3 as originally specified rather than re-scoping it. Rejected: uploading the raw TRX, which carries hours of `verbosity=detailed` StdOut and would breach the story's metadata-only obligation.
- [ ] [Review][Decision] **MEDIUM — Completion-run manifests now advertise a colliding artifact name rather than a dangling one; ADV-4 is marked CLOSED but is not.** `LiveContinuityAspireE2eTests.EvidenceArtifactLocator()` (`LiveContinuityAspireE2eTests.cs:440-459`) falls back to `artifact:live-recovery-validation-evidence` when `HEXALITH_CHATBOT_RECOVERY_EVIDENCE_ARTIFACT` is unset, and the completion job deliberately does not set it (`ci.yml:238-243`) — so completion manifests name the *scheduled* lane's artifact, uploaded by a different job on a different commit. The ADR text this diff adds says completion side-products "must not impersonate the scheduled/release A10 layout". The guard is blind to it: `ScaffoldArchitectureTests` only asserts the workflow does **not** contain the literal `HEXALITH_CHATBOT_RECOVERY_EVIDENCE_ARTIFACT: story-evidence-integrity-reports`, which cannot detect the default-value path — and that same assertion forbids the obvious fix. **Decision:** suppress the locator entirely on the completion invocation / give completion its own truthful artifact name and relax the forbidding assertion. Sources: blind-hunter+edge-case-hunter+acceptance-auditor. **Resolution (2026-08-25): dedicated completion recovery artifact.** Add a separate upload in the completion job (e.g. `completion-recovery-evidence`) holding the sanitized recovery TRX, and set `HEXALITH_CHATBOT_RECOVERY_EVIDENCE_ARTIFACT` to it. Truthful, distinct from the A10 bundle, and the `ScaffoldArchitectureTests.cs:918` guard stays intact and untouched. Rejected: pointing at `story-evidence-integrity-reports` (the honest name, but it requires weakening that guard) and suppressing the locator (truthful but leaves reviewers no path back to the evidence).
- [ ] [Review][Decision] **MEDIUM — The Keycloak stale-directory sweep uses directory mtime as a liveness proxy, which is false for exactly this repo's long-running lanes.** `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.AppHost/Program.cs:260-262` deletes any `hexalith-chatbot-keycloak-*` directory whose `GetLastWriteTimeUtc` is older than one hour. The realm file is written once at startup and never touched again, so a *live* AppHost session older than an hour — the 265-minute recovery lane, or a full IntegrationTests run — presents a stale mtime and its secret-bearing realm directory can be deleted out from under a running Keycloak container by any concurrently starting AppHost. The comment added in the same hunk asserts the opposite ("A crashed run is stale for hours; a concurrent test run is stale for milliseconds"), which holds only for short runs. **Decision:** refresh the mtime on a heartbeat / use a lock or PID-liveness check / raise the threshold above the longest lane budget. Source: blind-hunter. **Resolution (2026-08-25): PID-liveness / lock file.** Record the owning process id (or hold an advisory lock) in the realm directory and skip any directory whose owner is still alive, instead of inferring death from mtime. Correct regardless of run duration and it actually expresses the property the existing code comment claims. Pairs with the coverage patch below. Rejected: mtime heartbeat (leaves a false-live window after a crash) and simply raising the threshold (the race returns whenever a lane budget grows).
- [ ] [Review][Decision] **MEDIUM — The ModelContextProtocol `1.4.1 → 2.2.0` major bump was ratified by editing an assertion, inside a story whose subject is evidence integrity.** Commit `f804229` changes `ScaffoldArchitectureTests.cs:273`, `_bmad-output/planning-artifacts/architecture.md` (two places, including the "version-verified current" line) and `epics.md` — all three sit in Story 12.15's File List — with no ADR entry, no decision-log record, and no change under `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Mcp`. The out-of-scope disclosure block names only three other files and asserts "None is Story 12.15's own surface; all were disclosed rather than folded silently into this story's File List", which is false for this change. The pinned values themselves do match the current `references/Hexalith.Builds` catalog — this is a governance gap, not drift. **Decision:** record it as a decision (ADR + decision-log) / disclose it as out-of-scope catalog drift and move it out of this story. Sources: blind-hunter+acceptance-auditor. **Resolution (2026-08-25): disclose as out-of-scope catalog drift.** Move it out of Story 12.15 — record it in the out-of-scope block alongside the other three disclosed files and correct the false claim that none of them were folded into this story's File List. The pins already match the `references/Hexalith.Builds` catalog, so no code change is required. Rejected: writing an ADR here, which pulls a package-authority decision into a story already blocked on unrelated evidence.
- [ ] [Review][Decision] **MEDIUM — Every task and subtask remains `[x]` while the same diff records that the work they describe did not happen.** The diff simultaneously adds the header note "Murat completed the independent authenticity review and withheld approval because no complete passing hosted recovery bundle exists", a new `_bmad-output/test-artifacts/nfr-assessment.md` with **Overall Status: FAIL**, and a decision-log line recording the withheld approval — while Tasks 1-8 and all subtasks stay checked and `addendum.md` still reads "Provisional" for A10/NFR56. The `review → in-progress` status move is correct per the standing constraint; the checkbox state contradicts it. **Decision:** which subtasks to uncheck is a human call. Source: acceptance-auditor. **Resolution (2026-08-25): uncheck evidence-dependent subtasks only.** Uncheck the Task 7 and Task 8 subtasks whose completion depends on a passing hosted bundle — authentic Tier-3 evidence, real CI run/artifact locators, the Winston/Murat A10 review, and deferral retirement. Leave the design and implementation subtasks in Tasks 1-6 checked, since that code genuinely exists. Rejected: unchecking Tasks 7-8 wholesale (marks genuinely finished subtasks outstanding) and leaving all checked (the mechanical gate this epic is building reads checkboxes as evidence, so the story would misreport itself to its own tooling).

#### Patch application summary (2026-08-25)

All six resolved decisions and 21 of the 23 patch findings were applied; the two exceptions are annotated inline above. Verification actually run, all on this machine:

| Suite | Result |
| --- | --- |
| `dotnet build Hexalith.ChatBot.slnx --configuration Release -m:1` | Build succeeded, 0 errors |
| `Hexalith.ChatBot.StoryEvidenceGate.Tests` | **208 passed**, 0 failed, 0 skipped (was 195; +13 new) |
| `Hexalith.ChatBot.Architecture.Tests` | **77 passed**, 0 failed, 0 skipped (was 73; +4 new regression guards) |
| `Hexalith.ChatBot.Conformance.Tests` | 98 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped |
| `Hexalith.ChatBot.AppHost.Tests` | 14 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped |
| `Hexalith.ChatBot.Server.Tests` | 1766 passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped |
| `Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests` | 190 passed, 0 failed, **5 skipped** (Tier-3, unset `HEXALITH_CHATBOT_TIER3`) |

**What this evidence does and does not establish.** The gate-tool, policy, AppHost-sweep and architecture changes are proven by executed tests — including the new Keycloak sweep test, which really builds an AppHost testing topology (107 ms) and asserts both the swept and the spared directory. The **workflow changes are not proven**: every `ci.yml`/`release.yml` fix is asserted only by source-scan contract tests, which is the same class of evidence that failed to catch the pre-checkout envelope bug in the first place. Four new guards now pin the specific properties that broke — envelope path under `runner.temp`, in-process deadline strictly inside the external interrupt, in-job current-run lane produced after the drill, and lane ceiling exceeding the producer budget — but only a hosted run can confirm the lane is green. Nothing here produces the passing hosted recovery bundle the story is blocked on, and the standing constraint in the header comment is unchanged.

**One correction made during application.** The Edge Case Hunter's proposed `<IsTestProject>true` filter for the CI lane loop was implemented, then reverted on verification: none of the 15 test projects declares that property in its own `.csproj` (it comes from the SDK), so the filter skipped every lane and the new `executed == 0` guard would have turned the required job red. Only the `executed > 0` guard was kept.
