#### Dismissed on verification (1)

- The reported `RoundtripKind` vs `AssumeUniversal|AdjustToUniversal` asymmetry between `RecoveryTrxSanitizer.cs:58-67` and `TrxEvidenceReader.cs:505-513` is real but inert: the sanitizer uses the parsed values only for a `finishedAt >= startedAt` ordering comparison and copies the **original** `start`/`finish` strings verbatim into its output, so the claimed "local preflight rejects valid runs" consequence does not follow.


### Review Findings — hosted producer lane blocker (2026-08-25, /bmad-build)

Discovered while resuming the story: the required hosted lanes have never been able to run. Release run
`32813292652` (commit `abb29ba`, 2026-08-25 05:33Z) shows `required live recovery validation sweep` failing one second
after the build step with:

```
Settings file provided does not conform to required format.
An XML comment cannot contain '--', and '-' cannot be the last character. Line 5, position 63.
```

- [x] [Review][Patch] **HIGH — `live-recovery.runsettings` is not well-formed XML, so every required live-recovery lane is dead on arrival.** XML forbids `--` inside a comment. The file's header comment contains it twice: `` `dotnet test --filter ...` `` (line 5, col 63 — the exact position the runner reports) and ``must pass `--settings live-recovery.runsettings` `` (line 12). `python3 -c "xml.dom.minidom.parse('live-recovery.runsettings')"` → `not well-formed (invalid token): line 5, column 64`. All five `--settings live-recovery.runsettings` call sites are affected (`.github/workflows/ci.yml:269`, `:502`, `:580`; `.github/workflows/release.yml:156`, `:235`), covering the producer and the independent evidence gate on both workflows. Consequence chain observed in `32813292652`: producer exits 1 → `Finalize metadata-only live recovery attempt envelope` fails (`TestResults/workflow-attempt/producer-attempt.json.tmp: No such file or directory`) → `Upload live recovery reports…` finds no files → `required independent live recovery evidence gate` fails on `Artifact not found`. This is the single mechanical reason no passing hosted recovery bundle exists, and it has been present since the file was introduced in v1.5 (2026-08-02). [`live-recovery.runsettings:5`, `:12`] **Resolved 2026-08-25:** the header comment now names the switches without their two leading dashes (and says why), keeping every fact it documented; the file parses, and both lane filters were re-run locally against it.

- [x] [Review][Patch] **HIGH — The architecture guard for this file cannot detect the defect it exists to protect.** `LiveRecoveryValidationArchitectureTests.cs:350-351` reads the runsettings as text and asserts `runSettings.ShouldContain("<TreatNoTestsAsError>true</TreatNoTestsAsError>")`. A substring match passes on a file the test platform rejects outright, so the guard stayed green through the entire round-5 remediation and every pass since. Replace it with a parse-then-read assertion (load the document, select `/RunSettings/RunConfiguration/TreatNoTestsAsError`, assert its value), which fails on both malformed XML and a missing/false setting. [`tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.Architecture.Tests/LiveRecoveryValidationArchitectureTests.cs:350`] **Resolved 2026-08-25:** the guard now loads the file with `XDocument.Load` and reads `/RunSettings/RunConfiguration/TreatNoTestsAsError` out of the parsed tree, so it fails on malformed XML as well as on a missing or non-`true` value; reintroducing a `--` digraph into the comment turns the test red with `System.Xml.XmlException` (verified).

**Why the local replica missed it.** v1.15's faithful local replica covered the `build` job only. The producer and gate lanes live in `live-recovery-validation` / `live-recovery-evidence-gate`, which the replica did not reproduce, and local Tier-3 diagnostics invoked `dotnet test` without `--settings`.

### Review Findings — hosted producer lane blocker #2, root-caused (2026-08-25, /bmad-build)

With the runsettings defect repaired the required producer lane ran for the first time and failed at the mailbox
admission probe. It was made observable, root-caused, and fixed; the lane now runs all three coordinators and
produces all nine manifests/reports. What remains is recorded below it, separated into repository and environment.

- [x] [Review][Patch] **HIGH — The mailbox admission probe asserted an outcome the product cannot produce, so the lane could never leave startup.** `AssertMailboxTokenAdmissionAsync` posted a `CaptureMailboxMessageIntake` with `"intakeId":"not-a-ulid"` and accepted only `400`/`422`. `ChatBotProblemDetailsFactory` emits **only** `401`, `403`, `409` and `503` — there is no `400`/`422` path from the gateway for any input. The invalid identity is not rejected by any admission stage; it reaches `AcceptedCommandDispatcher.BuildPlanAsync:149`, whose `InvalidOperationException` `CommandGateway.SubmitAsync:94` classifies as a transient dispatch outage, so every attempt returned `503 dispatch-unavailable` — which the probe classified as transient and retried until the 5-minute startup budget expired. Deterministic, not timing: ~1014 probe POSTs, all `503`, all ~10 ms. Introduced 2026-08-04 by `69a6077`, which rewrote a probe that had previously only asserted "not 401/403" (the shape that passed on 2026-08-02) into this unsatisfiable one; the runsettings defect then kept it out of CI entirely. **Resolved:** the probe now proves admission from the problem `type`. `dispatch-unavailable` is emitted from exactly one place — the `catch` around `dispatcher.DispatchAsync`, reachable only after `admissionDecision.IsAccepted` — so observing it is categorical proof the bearer cleared authentication, authorization, tenant binding and the service-client grant. That is strictly more specific than "status was 400 or 422", and unlike it, reachable. `401`/`403` and the pre-commit `audit-unavailable` denial are still rejected, and `400`/`422` are still accepted should the product ever produce them. Pinned by 8 new theory cases. [`tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/Recovery/LiveContinuityAspireE2eTests.cs:706`]

- [x] [Review][Patch] **MEDIUM — The recovery lane discarded every resource log, so its failures could not name their own cause.** The trivial Tier-3 class surfaces resource logs; the recovery lane surfaced only an HTTP status. **Resolved:** the probe now retains a bounded copy of the last problem document and includes it in the failure message, and a new `RecoveryResourceLogTail` keeps a bounded tail of the `chatbot` and `eventstore` streams for the failure path. Both are diagnostic test output only and never reach an evidence artifact. Caveat recorded honestly: `ResourceLoggerService.WatchAsync` returned no lines in the `dotnet test` host, so the resource-log half of the diagnosis came from running the same test through the native xUnit runner, where the AppHost forwards resource logs to stdout under `…AppHost.Resources.<name>`. The problem-details capture is what actually named the reason code.

- [ ] [Review][Note] **MEDIUM — A fail-closed continuity conversion retains no reason token, so a failing hosted run is un-diagnosable.** When a scenario fails during injection, the coordinator converts it to an `unmeasurable` report with `deviations: ["continuity_drill_incomplete"]`, every assertion `false`, and `executionAssertions: null` — the underlying cause is not retained anywhere in the bundle. This is the same defect class as the probe above, one layer up: a hosted failure will be as opaque as this one was. Not changed here — reports are metadata-only with governed reason tokens, so adding a cause token is a reviewed change, not a drive-by. [`TestResults/live-recovery/*/eventstore-outage.manifest.json`]

- [ ] [Review][Note] **The product classifies a permanently malformed command payload as a retryable dispatch outage.** `BuildPlanAsync`'s identity/shape guards throw `InvalidOperationException`, which `CommandGateway` maps to `503 dispatch-unavailable` with `retryable: true`, `clientAction: retry-later`, **and** a queued pre-commit replay intent plus an `AuditUnavailable` operator alert — on every attempt. During the failing runs this fired ~1000 times from one malformed field. This predates Story 12.15 (`ebbf83c`), was deliberate per the factory's own comment, and changing it is an API-contract change touching the message catalog, OpenAPI and conformance tests — so it is reported, not changed. It is the alternative fix a reviewer may prefer over the probe change above: making malformed payloads a `400` would also make the original `400`/`422` expectation satisfiable.

#### Environment, not repository — stated plainly rather than worked around

Three consecutive clean re-runs separated repository defects from this host's instability. None of the following was worked around, and no timeout, guard or assertion was weakened to get past them:

- **The DAPR placement service stopped being reachable mid-session.** `dapr_placement`'s published `127.0.0.1:6050` forward disappeared from `docker ps` while the container stayed `Up`; every sidecar logged `dial tcp 127.0.0.1:6050: connect: connection refused` (2003 occurrences in one run, 0 in the runs before and after). Without placement, EventStore's `CommandRouter` actor invocation hangs to its 100-second `HttpClient` timeout, returns `500`, and ChatBot fail-closes to `503` — which surfaced as `Subscription committed-before-outage checkpoint failed (kind=recoverable, reason=audit_unavailable)`. A plain `docker restart dapr_placement` restored the forward and the symptom vanished. The non-recovery Tier-3 control class never saw it. This is Docker Desktop port-forward flakiness on this WSL2 host, previously recorded in the project's Tier-3 notes.
- **Leftover topology containers from failed runs collide with the next run.** A failed run leaves `security-*` (Keycloak), `memories-vectors-*`, `memories-graphs-*` and the Aspire tunnel proxy running. With leftovers present, Keycloak `Exited` mid-run and the lane failed with `Resource 'eventstore' stopped waiting for dependency resource 'security' because it entered the 'Exited' state prematurely` and `Keycloak did not issue a dedicated recovery bearer before the deadline`. After removing them the same commit ran cleanly through both continuity scenarios and the rebuild.
- **The local replica's own misconfiguration, corrected:** an initial `HEXALITH_CHATBOT_RECOVERY_WORKFLOW_TIMEOUT_MINUTES=25` violated the real `WorkflowTimeout ≥ 9 × PerScenarioTimeout + margin` rule and was rejected by `options.Validate()` — the guard working as designed. The replica now uses CI's `265`.

#### Where the lane actually stands after this pass

On the last clean run (8 m 48 s, run id `01M0VVVPKJCGF4NT2NMECZJEZY`, local and **not** repository-retained or citable) the lane reached and exercised all three coordinators and wrote all nine manifests plus nine reports. Verdicts: projection rebuild `equivalent`; `identity`, `ai-provider`, `command-execution`, `audit-store` `contained`; `graph` and `attachment-processing` `unmeasurable` (`The scoped-outage fault or independent control operation was not observed`); both continuity scenarios `unmeasurable` with `fault-observed: false`. So the lane is no longer blocked at startup, but it still does not produce a passing bundle, the remaining causes are not yet root-caused, and **no A10, NFR57 or NFR41 claim may be made from this run.**

### Review Findings — hosted producer lane blocker #3: the four `fault-observed: false` scenarios (2026-08-25, /bmad-build)

Same discipline as the 503: observability first, then attribution. Six further live runs. Nothing was weakened to
make a scenario pass; every fix below either records a cause that was previously discarded or classifies a
transient transport condition the way the same file's sibling polls already classify it.

#### Observability defects fixed (a failing hosted run could not name its own cause)

- [x] [Review][Patch] **HIGH — All three capturing runners silently dropped every `OperationCanceledException`, and `TaskCanceledException` is one.** `CapturingContinuityDrillScenarioRunner`, `CapturingScopedOutageInjectionDriver` and `CapturingProjectionRebuildDriver` filtered on `when (exception is not OperationCanceledException)`. An internal deadline — a per-scenario CTS, a restoration budget, or an `HttpClient.Timeout`, which surfaces as `TaskCanceledException` — was therefore never recorded, the fail-safe coordinator reduced it to an `unmeasurable` report, and the harness reported **no failure at all**. That is exactly what happened in the 2026-08-25 run whose reports read `verdict: unmeasurable`, `fault-observed: false`, `executionAssertions: null` while the test's own error message mentioned only the scoped-outage sweep. **Resolved:** the filter now records unless the **caller's** token was actually cancelled. The very next run named the cause: `TaskCanceledException : The request was canceled due to the configured HttpClient.Timeout of 30 seconds elapsing`. [`CapturingContinuityDrillScenarioRunner.cs:24`, `CapturingScopedOutageInjectionDriver.cs:28`, `CapturingProjectionRebuildDriver.cs:25`]

- [x] [Review][Patch] **MEDIUM — Three harness failure messages discarded the one fact needed to diagnose them.** `SubmitUntilAcceptedAsync` threw "failed outside a transient dependency state" without the status; `SendSandboxControlAsync` threw "did not complete successfully" without the action or status; `RecoveryAccessTokenProvider` threw "did not issue a dedicated recovery bearer before the deadline" after three minutes without saying whether Keycloak was refusing connections, timing out, or answering `503`. All three now name it. `SendSandboxControlAsync` is deliberately **not** retried — `process` is side-effectful and a blind retry would manufacture the duplicate side effects the scenario exists to rule out.

- [x] [Review][Patch] **MEDIUM — One scoped-outage message conflated two different breaches.** "The scoped-outage fault or independent control operation was not observed" covered both "the application never observed the injected fault" and "the unaffected control operation stopped working" — and the second is the containment evidence NFR58 turns on. Split into two messages naming the dependency. Same fail-closed outcome, distinguishable cause.

#### Repository defect fixed

- [x] [Review][Patch] **HIGH — `SubmitUntilAcceptedAsync` treated a client-side timeout as fatal, aborting scenarios during seeding, before any fault was injected.** The loop tolerated `HttpRequestException` for three minutes but let `TaskCanceledException` escape, so the 30-second `_chatBotClient.Timeout` elapsing during warm-up killed the whole scenario — while the same file's `IsEventStoreEndpointAvailableAsync` and the EventStore recovery poll already classify that exact condition as transient, as does the mailbox admission probe. **Resolved** symmetrically with those siblings; the three-minute bound and the non-transient-status throw are unchanged. Verified: `m365-subscription-failure` no longer fails here, and passed in the two most recent runs. [`AspireRecoverySandboxOperations.cs:1071`]

#### `eventstore-outage` — root-caused, owned by another repository, needs a decision

- [ ] [Review][Decision] **HIGH — The mandatory A10 EventStore drill cannot inject its fault: the EventStore process does not exit, so the allowlisted Aspire `stop` command fails.** Reproduced in three consecutive clean runs (`Failed to stop resource 'eventstore-mdnspgqc'`, `'eventstore-qvxvjbbp'`, `'eventstore-ubuswmhd'`). The DCP log gives the mechanism: `[sys] Failed to stop run: Error = timed out waiting for process to stop`. The resource logs show why it is a hang and not host slowness — the EventStore process was **still logging** `ProjectionUpdateOrchestrator` "Named projection dispatch failed; legacy projection delivery continues" warnings, dozens per second for `tenant-alpha`/`tenant-beta`, **39 seconds after** DCP's stop deadline elapsed. The lane already bounds graceful shutdown (`DOTNET_SHUTDOWNTIMEOUTSECONDS=5`, `LiveRecoveryTopologyConfiguration`), which bounds waiting on `IHostedService.StopAsync` but cannot bound a loop that ignores its token. The harness behaves correctly throughout: the stop is not silently accepted, the scenario is not marked measured, and the run fails closed as `unmeasurable`. **This is a defect in `references/Hexalith.EventStore`, not in this repository** (`Hexalith.EventStore.Server/Projections/ProjectionUpdateOrchestrator`), and the project baseline says to work from the repository that owns the change — so no fix was attempted here. **Decision needed, product/architecture:** (a) fix the EventStore shutdown path in its own repository and update the submodule, or (b) change the injection mechanism for this scenario, which the ADR's ratified matrix pins as `stop:eventstore` and AC1 pins as making "the real sandbox EventStore resource unavailable". Neither is an engineering call this pass may take. **Until it is resolved the mandatory `eventstore-outage` A10 exercise cannot produce a measurement — it must not be recorded as met, and it must not be reclassified as a residual (AC3 forbids closing a mandatory exercise that way).**

#### `graph` and `attachment-processing` — not re-reached; one fragility identified, not changed

- [ ] [Review][Decision] **MEDIUM — The independent-control probe is a single-shot submit, so one transient `503` records a containment breach.** `IsChatBotControlAvailableAsync` posts one `RecordGovernedNote` and returns `false` on anything other than `202`, and also swallows a client timeout into the same `false`. Every other governed submit in this harness retries transient states for three minutes. That single shot is the most likely cause of the earlier `graph`/`attachment-processing` `unmeasurable` results, whose message could not distinguish it from "fault not observed" (now split). **Deliberately not changed:** `IndependentControlSucceeded` *is* the NFR58 containment evidence, so adding a retry window could convert a genuine, brief degradation of the unaffected path into a pass — precisely the manufactured-pass this story forbids. Choosing the retry window, or deciding that only a sustained control failure counts as a breach, is a product decision. **Also note:** now that continuity failures are correctly captured, the sweep fails closed at the continuity stage and no longer reaches the scoped-outage sweep, so neither dependency has been re-observed since the split message landed. [`AspireScopedOutageOperations.cs:868`]

#### Environment, again — named, not worked around

- **Keycloak (`security`) intermittently unavailable.** Three shapes across the runs: `Exited` mid-run (blocking `eventstore` restart on its dependency wait), a three-minute token-endpoint deadline with no bearer, and a clean start followed by silence. Keycloak's own logs show a normal 12-second boot, realm import, and no error. Host memory was never a constraint (44 GB available). Not attributed further; it is not reproducible on demand and does not correlate with any change made here.
- **Leftover topology containers** (`security-*`, `memories-vectors-*`, `memories-graphs-*`, the Aspire tunnel proxy) survive a failed run and degrade the next one. Every run above was preceded by removing them.
- **`dapr_placement`'s published port forward** was lost once more during the session, with the same signature as before.

#### Where the lane stands after this pass

Best run of this pass (`tier3k`, clean host): startup, seeding, admission, `m365-subscription-failure` and the projection rebuild all proceed; the run fails closed on `eventstore-outage` alone with the DCP stop-timeout cause now named in the failure. No `met`, `contained` or `equivalent` verdict was manufactured, no threshold, guard, assertion or measurement budget was weakened, and **no A10, NFR56, NFR57 or NFR41 claim may be made from any run in this pass.**


### Review Findings — blocker #3 resolved at the injection boundary; correction and a new decision (2026-08-25, /bmad-build)

Jerome returned both decisions. Decision 1 (fix the EventStore shutdown hang upstream, keep the ADR's
`stop:eventstore` matrix and AC1 wording intact) and Decision 2 (separate a control-probe timeout from a non-202,
without a retry window) are both implemented. Six more live runs.

#### Correction to the previous pass — the earlier attribution was wrong

- [x] [Review][Correction] **The claim that the EventStore process "kept logging `ProjectionUpdateOrchestrator` retry warnings 39 seconds past the stop deadline" was a misreading, and the conclusion drawn from it was wrong.** Those lines belong to the *restarted* instance: DCP continues one line-number sequence across restarts, and the lane's `start` command had already launched a new process (resource lines 2979-2985) before they were written. The dying process in fact went **completely silent** at `Application is shutting down...` and produced nothing for the twelve seconds until DCP gave up. `ProjectionUpdateOrchestrator` and its `CancellationToken.None` cleanup paths are **not** implicated — confirmed and excluded, not assumed.

#### `eventstore-outage` — root cause proven by process dump, fixed upstream

- [x] [Review][Patch] **HIGH — An unreachable OTLP collector made the EventStore process unstoppable, so the mandatory A10 fault could never be injected.** A full process dump taken at the hang (`dotnet-dump`, watcher armed on the health endpoint dropping while the PID was still alive) gives the mechanism exactly. The single foreground thread is parked in `HostingAbstractionsHostExtensions.Run` → `Host.DisposeAsync` → `ServiceProviderEngineScope.DisposeAsync` → `TracerProviderSdk.Dispose` → `BatchExportProcessor.OnShutdown` → `BatchExportThreadWorker.Shutdown` → **`Thread.Join(Int32)`**, while three OTel exporter threads (trace, log, metric) sit in `OtlpExporterRetryTransmissionHandler.OnSubmitRequestFailure` → **`Thread.Sleep`**. Decompiling the pinned OpenTelemetry 1.17.0 confirms the shape: provider disposal calls `Shutdown()` with `Timeout.Infinite`, and `BatchExportThreadWorker.Shutdown(-1)` performs an unbounded `exporterThread.Join()`. `DOTNET_SHUTDOWNTIMEOUTSECONDS=5`, which the lane already sets, cannot help — it bounds `StopAsync`, not disposal. **Fixed in the owning repository** (`references/Hexalith.EventStore`, working tree only — not committed, gitlink unmoved at `49b84d0c`): a new `BoundedTelemetryShutdownService` shuts the tracer/meter/logger providers down with a finite two-second budget while the host is still stopping, registered only alongside `UseOtlpExporter()`. `BaseProcessor.Shutdown` is guarded by an interlocked one-shot counter, so the later disposal becomes a no-op instead of an unbounded join; telemetry still flushes normally when the collector is reachable. **Verified live: `Failed to stop resource` occurrences went from 2-4 in every prior run to 0**, and the drill now proceeds through injection and restoration into post-recovery end-state verification. Regression coverage lands in the same repository (`BoundedTelemetryShutdownServiceTests`, 4 cases) and is falsifiable — with the bounded shutdown disabled, `StopAsync_MakesTheSubsequentDisposeNonBlocking` fails; with it, all four pass. EventStore `Server.Tests` is 3135 passed / 0 failed / 25 skipped. Committing and pushing the submodule, and moving the gitlink, remain Jerome's step.

#### `graph` / `attachment-processing` — Decision 2 implemented

- [x] [Review][Patch] **MEDIUM — A client-side timeout in the independent-control probe is now a distinct, named outcome.** `IsChatBotControlAvailableAsync` collapsed "never answered" and "answered non-202" into the same `false`. `ScopedOutageFaultObservation` gained `IndependentControlUnobserved`, and the driver now reports either "was never answered" or "did not succeed" for the dependency. **No retry window was added and nothing widened what counts as available:** both shapes keep `IndependentControlSucceeded` false and both fail closed, because that flag is the NFR58 containment evidence. Pinned by a theory that drives the real driver through both shapes and asserts the reported cause plus restore/cleanup.

#### New blocker, needs a decision — the absence probe cannot observe absence

- [ ] [Review][Decision] **HIGH — Post-recovery `unauthorized-mutation-absent` verification is refused with `403 authorization-denied`, so the A10 EventStore drill still cannot complete.** With the stop hang fixed, `eventstore-outage` now reaches `ReadEventStoreEndStateAsync` and fails there. The probe's reason code is now retained (same observability treatment as the 503): `{"type":".../errors/chatbot/authorization-denied","status":403,"code":"authorization_denied","message":"The operation is not available to this caller."}` from `GET /api/v1/governed-operations/{faultProbeNoteRef}`. The presence probes immediately above use the **same** user bearer and do not fail, so this is specific to reading the fault-probe note — the note deliberately submitted during the outage that must NOT exist. ChatBot appears to authorize this read before establishing existence, so a nonexistent governed operation answers `403` rather than `404`. The harness is right to refuse it: its own comment states absence must be observed, never inferred from "not 200", and a `403` is not evidence that an unauthorized mutation is absent. **Decision needed, product/architecture:** (a) let an authorized caller of the tenant receive `404` for a nonexistent governed operation — an information-disclosure trade-off against enumeration, and a change to a shipped authorization semantic; or (b) redefine the projection-side half of this NFR59 assertion to use an authorized channel, noting the drill already computes the durable half independently through `IWormAuditStore`/state-store `RemainsGovernedNoteAbsentAsync`. Not an engineering call this pass may take, and not weakenable here: accepting `403` as absence would manufacture the exact `unauthorized-mutation-absent: true` this story forbids.

#### Where the nine scenarios land after this pass

The sweep fails closed at continuity, so it does not reach the rebuild or the six scoped-outage scenarios; the last
run in which it did reach them (before continuity failures were correctly captured) had rebuild `equivalent`, four
dependencies `contained` and two `unmeasurable`. Current state: `m365-subscription-failure` passes;
`eventstore-outage` now injects and restores the real resource but fails at the `403` above; the remaining seven are
unobserved this pass. **No verdict was manufactured, nothing was weakened, and no A10, NFR56, NFR57 or NFR41 claim
may be made from any run in this pass.**


### Review Findings — all nine scenarios measured for the first time; one gate reason remains (2026-08-25, /bmad-build)

Jerome's decision on the `403`: use an authorized channel, leave ChatBot's safe-not-found semantics as shipped.
Implemented. The lane then ran end to end for the first time in this story's history — 15 m 44 s, all three
coordinators, all nine scenarios measured — and stop-ships on exactly one reason.

#### The absence probe now observes absence, on a channel that can

- [x] [Review][Patch] **HIGH — Projection-side absence moved off the read API and onto the projection store.** `GET /api/v1/governed-operations/{id}` answers `403 authorization-denied` for a governed operation that does not exist (the shipped safe-not-found behaviour, deliberately **not** changed here), and a `403` is not evidence that an unauthorized mutation is absent. `RemainsGovernedOperationProjectionAbsentAsync` now polls the projection through the production `ReadModelGovernedOperationViewStore`, so the store name and key are exactly the ones `GovernedOperationProjectionHandler` writes and cannot drift from them. A missing key is a definite answer from the store that would hold the projection if it existed — observed, not inferred from "not 200"; a read that faults propagates rather than counting as absence; and a window in which nothing was successfully read never reports absence. **Independence preserved and now explicit:** the durable half of the same NFR59 assertion is computed separately by `ReadEventStoreEndStateAsync` from EventStore's aggregate actor state via `EventStoreDurableStateProbe` — a different service and a different store. Neither half derives from the other and the assertion still requires both. The same channel change was applied to the two cleanup-verification absence checks, which asked the identical question of the identical API and would otherwise have reported every successfully cleaned run as incomplete. The cross-tenant isolation read is untouched: there the resource genuinely exists, so its `403` is real isolation evidence rather than safe-not-found ambiguity.

- [ ] [Review][Note] **The presence half carries the same latent ambiguity.** `WaitForGovernedOperationAsync(expectPresent: true)` treats `404` as "not yet projected" and anything else as a verification failure — but a genuinely lost note answers `403`, not `404`, so a real partial-loss outcome would surface as an unmeasurable "unexpected status 403" instead of a measured data-loss signal. Not changed: it is outside the decision given, and it only misreports on a path no run has yet taken. Flagged so it is not discovered later as a surprise.

#### All nine scenarios, measured

Two consecutive runs, clean host, identical results. **Every scenario is now measurable with zero deviations:**

| Exercise | Scenario | Verdict | Measured |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Continuity | `eventstore-outage` | **met** | RPO 0 s, RTO 148.8 s (targets 900 s / 14400 s) |
| Continuity | `m365-subscription-failure` | **met** | RPO 0 s, RTO 60.5 s |
| Projection rebuild | `recovery-baseline` | **equivalent** | — |
| Scoped outage | `graph`, `identity`, `ai-provider`, `command-execution`, `audit-store`, `attachment-processing` | **contained** (all six) | — |

Every assertion is true on both continuity manifests except one: `eventstore-outage` reports
`cleanup-complete: false`. The release gate therefore stop-ships on the single reason
`continuity:cleanup_incomplete`. **These figures are from a local run under ignored `TestResults/`. They are not
repository-retained, not citable, and no A10, NFR56, NFR57 or NFR41 claim is made from them.**

#### The one remaining gate reason — a second EventStore defect, stopping for a decision

> **CORRECTED TWICE — read the 2026-08-25 blocker-#4 findings before citing anything below.** The claim that this is a production-affecting defect in a shared library was **retracted**: the checkpoint refusal is correct behaviour for a store whose v2 writer protocol is active. The follow-up claim that the writer-protocol marker was foreign *contamination to be deleted* was **also wrong** — EventStore requires that store-global marker for readiness, and deleting it took the server unready. The actual defect is a ChatBot/EventStore identity mismatch that blocks the fenced completion; see blocker #4.

- [ ] [Review][Decision] **HIGH — EventStore never advances its projection checkpoint, so it re-delivers the same aggregate indefinitely; the erased read model reappears and continuity cleanup can never complete.** A metadata-only diagnostic added to the cleanup path (stable check names only, to stderr, never to a report) names the failing sub-check: `erased-projection-still-present`. The erase itself succeeded — `EraseReadModelAsync` retries five times and throws if it cannot erase, and it did not throw — so the projection is being **re-created** after erasure. The EventStore resource log gives the mechanism: `Projection checkpoint save exhausted optimistic-concurrency attempts: TenantId=…, Stage=ProjectionCheckpointSaveExhausted`, **42,066 times in one run** — 13,685 for `recovery-validation`, 6,210 for `tenant-beta`, 1,138 for `tenant-alpha`. The checkpoint never advances, so the poller re-delivers the same aggregates forever, ChatBot re-projects them, and the erased governed-operation view is back inside the absence window. This is **not** recovery-specific and **not** a harness defect: it affects every tenant, and it is very likely the source of the constant background load that has been destabilising this sandbox all session (Keycloak starvation, resource contention). It belongs to `references/Hexalith.EventStore` (`ProjectionUpdateOrchestrator` / its checkpoint store) and is a **different defect from the shutdown hang**, which was the only fix authorised there. **Not fixed, and deliberately not worked around:** the authorized channel can observe absence perfectly well — it correctly observes that the projection is present again — so this is not a case of "the channel cannot observe". Relaxing the cleanup assertion, shortening its window, or excluding this check would convert a real, reproducible residue into a manufactured `cleanup-complete: true`. **Decision needed:** authorise the checkpoint fix in the owning repository (same working-tree-only, gitlink-unmoved discipline), or direct otherwise.

#### The non-reproducing IntegrationTests failure, now pinned

- [ ] [Review][Note] **Identified: `TrivialGovernedCommandAspireE2eTests.TierThreeParallelValidationShouldRetainEveryNonCancellationSiblingFailure` is flaky at roughly 1 run in 8.** It asserts `siblingFailures.InnerExceptions.Count.ShouldBe(3)` and intermittently observes `2` (`TrivialGovernedCommandAspireE2eTests.cs:1080`). Four sibling validations rendezvous and all throw; the coordinator promotes one and must retain the other three, and occasionally only two are retained — a race in `TopologyReadinessCoordinator`'s sibling retention, where a sibling either lands after the aggregate is captured or is dropped as a cancellation once the first failure cancels the token. Pre-existing and unrelated to this pass: nothing in this pass's diff touches `TopologyReadinessCoordinator` or that test class. Reproduced deliberately (1 failure in 9 `dotnet test` invocations; 5 consecutive native-runner invocations were clean), so it is recorded with its identity rather than left as an unidentified failure.


### Review Findings — the checkpoint storm was contamination, not a library defect; correction and current state (2026-08-25, /bmad-build)

Jerome authorised fixing the checkpoint defect upstream. Root-causing it the way the shutdown hang was
root-caused — mechanism proven, not inferred — showed there is no checkpoint defect to fix. The correction is
recorded first because the previous pass reported the opposite.

#### Correction — this is NOT a production-affecting shared-library defect

> **This correction was itself partly wrong; see blocker #4 below.** Retracting "production-affecting shared-library defect" was right. Calling the writer-protocol marker foreign contamination was not: `ProjectionDeliveryWriterProtocolHealthCheck` documents it as the *required* store-global v2 marker and reports the server unready without it. Deleting it caused the restoration regression described in the same section, and restoring it restored the nine-scenario result.

- [x] [Review][Correction] **The previous pass reported the checkpoint storm as a second, production-affecting defect in `references/Hexalith.EventStore`, and asked for it to be recorded as such. That was wrong, and the evidence now contradicts it.** The mechanism, proven link by link: (1) the shared dev Redis holds a **global, unprefixed** key `projection-delivery-writer-protocol` = `{"writerProtocolVersion":2,"cutoverCommit":"hexalith-conversations-apphost-boundary-harness","activatedAt":"2026-08-22T09:14:03Z","isCurrent":true}` — written on 22 August by **a different application's** harness; (2) `ProjectionCheckpointTracker.SaveDeliveredSequenceAsync` correctly refuses every legacy advance while that marker is current ("after cutover, no five-field writer may create or advance a scoped row"); (3) the only remaining way to advance is the fenced completion in the named-projection path, which was failing with `HttpRequestException: 400` from `AdminOperationalIndexHostedService.LoadBindingAsync` (`UnsupportedCapability` — the domain service's `AppId`/`ServiceVersion` identity check); (4) so no checkpoint ever advanced, the poller re-delivered forever, and the erased read model returned inside the absence window. The ChatBot topology never writes that marker — only the explicit `POST delivery-writer-protocol/activate` admin endpoint does — and it is visible here only because the shared `statestore` component is deliberately configured `keyPrefix: none` (`ChatBotAspireModule.cs:185`, required for the shared actor state store) on a dev Redis that several Hexalith applications share. In production each application has its own state store, so this cross-application leak does not arise. **Verified by removing only the unprefixed foreign key** (the two `conversations-gateway-…||`-prefixed markers were left untouched): before, `projection-checkpoints:*` scoped rows numbered **0**; the very next run created **85**, and the marker was not recreated. Environment, not library. The EventStore owners should not be handed this as a production bug.

#### The genuine upstream defect it exposed, fixed

- [x] [Review][Patch] **MEDIUM — Four structurally different checkpoint refusals were all reported as an optimistic-concurrency race.** `SaveDeliveredSequenceAsync` returns `false` for a current v2 row requiring fenced completion, a schema regression, an unsupported row, a post-cutover writer protocol, **and** genuine ETag exhaustion — and the orchestrator logs every one of them as `Projection checkpoint save exhausted optimistic-concurrency attempts`. That is what sent this investigation hunting a write race that does not exist, through 42,066 identical warnings. Each structural refusal now names itself with a stable reason code (`ProjectionCheckpointAdvanceRefused`, EventId 1145, reasons `delivery-row-requires-fenced-completion`, `delivery-row-schema-regression`, `delivery-row-unsupported`, `post-cutover-writer-protocol`), leaving ETag exhaustion as the only case reported as concurrency. Fixed in the owning repository, **working tree only, gitlink unmoved at `49b84d0c`**, with falsifiable coverage (`ProjectionCheckpointRefusalDiagnosticsTests`): removing the new log call turns it red. EventStore `Server.Tests` 3136 passed / 0 failed / 25 skipped. The `post-cutover-writer-protocol` refusal is the one that applies even when the aggregate has no scoped row at all, so it can silently block every legacy advance for every tenant — that is now visible in one log line instead of requiring a Redis dump.

- [ ] [Review][Note] **A 400 capability refusal is treated as a hard failure where a 404 is not.** `LoadBindingAsync` degrades gracefully when the metadata endpoint is absent (`404` → "capability absent") but throws on `400 UnsupportedCapability`, which is the same class of answer: "I do not support this capability for that identity". Not changed — it is a design call for the EventStore owners, and it was not the cause of the storm — but it is the reason a benign identity mismatch surfaces as a repeating exception rather than a one-line capability note.

#### Current state of the nine scenarios — the earlier figures no longer stand

Removing the contamination changed the lane's behaviour, and not only for the better. **Three consecutive runs
(two `dotnet test`, one native runner) now fail earlier than before**, at EventStore restoration:

```
System.TimeoutException : EventStore reported healthy but its listener did not accept traffic within 90s.
```

The sweep therefore stops at continuity and produces two manifests instead of nine. **The 2026-08-25 nine-scenario
bundle (two `met`, one `equivalent`, six `contained`) is not the current state and must not be cited as such.**

The restarted EventStore *was* listening: the resource log shows `Now listening on: http://localhost:8080` 27 s
after `Executing command 'start'`, well inside the 90-second budget, yet the harness's probe never reached it. The
most consistent explanation is that the harness's `_eventStoreClient`, built once from
`application.CreateHttpClient("eventstore", "http")`, holds a DCP proxy endpoint that does not survive a
stop/start — but that is **not proven**, and the same retained client did survive restarts in the runs before the
contamination was removed. Whether this is a latent harness defect the storm was masking, a consequence of the
bounded telemetry shutdown changing stop timing, or more of this host's instability is undetermined. Stopping here
rather than speculating further, per the standing instruction. Nothing was relaxed to get past it: the readiness
budget, the health wait and the fail-closed conversion are all unchanged.


### Review Findings — blocker #4: the restoration regression was self-inflicted; the real defect named (2026-08-25, /bmad-build)

The three candidates for `EventStore reported healthy but its listener did not accept traffic within 90s` were
prosecuted in the ordered sequence requested. Two are eliminated by measurement, and the answer turned out to be
the pass's own cleanup action. The 90-second budget was never touched and is not the defect.

#### 1. The bounded telemetry shutdown — eliminated

- [x] [Review][Verified] **A/B'd by disabling only the hosted-service registration.** With the fix out, the stop hang **returns** (`Failed to stop resource 'eventstore-vbkdsrzc'`) *and* the readiness failure still occurs, in the same run. The readiness failure therefore does not depend on the fix, and the fix remains necessary. Registration restored immediately after the run.

#### 2. The retained `_eventStoreClient` across a DCP stop/start — eliminated by direct measurement

- [x] [Review][Verified] **Measured rather than reasoned about.** A diagnostic that runs only after the budget has already expired — pure observation, no retry into the budget, no path to success — reported: `retainedEndpoint=http://localhost:8080/ resolvedEndpoint=http://localhost:8080/ freshClientHealth=503`. The retained endpoint and the freshly resolved endpoint are identical, so nothing went stale, and a fresh client **reached** the process. The harness side of the gap is sound.

#### 3. Which side of the gap is broken — the application side, because of this pass's own cleanup

- [x] [Review][Correction] **`/health` was answering `503`, and the reason was the writer-protocol marker this pass had deleted.** `ProjectionDeliveryWriterProtocolHealthCheck` states its contract in its own summary — *"Requires the store-global v2 writer marker before the server becomes ready"* — and returns `Unhealthy` unless `marker?.IsCurrent == true`. Deleting the marker as "foreign contamination" therefore took EventStore permanently unready, the readiness probe correctly refused a persistent `503`, and restoration failed. **Restoring the marker byte-for-byte restored the lane:** the very next run measured all nine scenarios again — `eventstore-outage` **met**, `m365-subscription-failure` **met**, rebuild **equivalent**, six dependencies **contained** — with the single gate reason `continuity:cleanup_incomplete`, exactly the pre-deletion behaviour (run `01M0W97Q7MQVX08K9GS71EH8MB`, local and non-citable). The marker is a legitimate store-global artifact that EventStore depends on; this topology simply never activates one itself and was relying on the one already present in the shared store. **The "contamination to be cleaned" framing was wrong and the sandbox mutation is reverted.**

- [ ] [Review][Note] **The harness message names the wrong side of the gap.** "its listener did not accept traffic" is emitted when the listener *is* accepting traffic and answering `503`. The gap diagnostic now reports the status alongside the endpoints, so the message can no longer send an investigation after a connectivity problem that does not exist. The readiness loop itself is unchanged and still requires a success status.

#### The defect actually behind `cleanup_incomplete` — a decision, not an engineering call

- [ ] [Review][Decision] **HIGH — ChatBot hosts the EventStore domain-service SDK but never configures its projection identity, so every named-projection metadata request is refused `400`, the fenced completion never runs, and the checkpoint can never advance.** With the writer protocol active (as it must be for readiness), `SaveDeliveredSequenceAsync` correctly refuses legacy advancement — post-cutover the checkpoint may only advance through the named-projection fenced completion. That completion never happens: `AdminOperationalIndexHostedService.LoadBindingAsync` sends `AppId` plus `ServiceVersion` (defaulted to `v1`), and `EventStoreDomainServiceExtensions`' `/admin/operational-index-metadata` endpoint answers `Results.BadRequest(UnsupportedCapability)` whenever they do not match the service's `DomainProjectionIdentityOptions`. `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Program.cs:21` calls `AddEventStoreDomainService(...)` but nothing in this repository ever configures `DomainProjectionIdentityOptions`, so its `AppId` is empty and the comparison can never succeed. Consequence chain, each link evidenced: `400` → named dispatch throws (`HttpRequestException`, stack captured) → no fenced completion → checkpoint never advances → the poller re-delivers the same aggregates indefinitely (42,066 refusals in one run, all three tenants) → the erased governed-operation view returns inside the absence window → `cleanup-complete: false` → `continuity:cleanup_incomplete`. **Corrected 2026-08-25 — the `AppId` was not empty, it resolved to the assembly name `Hexalith.ChatBot.Server` via the SDK's `DAPR_APP_ID`-then-`ApplicationName` fallback; see the projection-identity findings below.** **Decision needed, and it spans two repositories:** (a) configure `DomainProjectionIdentityOptions` in the ChatBot server so the identity matches and the named path activates — a real behaviour change, since it switches ChatBot onto the v2 named-projection delivery path; or (b) treat "no identity configured" as capability-absent in the SDK endpoint, the way a missing endpoint (`404`) is already treated, leaving ChatBot on the legacy path — which then needs an answer for how a post-cutover store advances a legacy checkpoint at all. Not taken here: either choice changes projection-delivery behaviour for a shipped service, and (b) additionally reopens an SDK contract. Nothing was relaxed in the meantime — `cleanup-complete` still reports the residue honestly.


### Review Findings — projection identity pinned; `cleanup_incomplete` cleared; two verdicts moved (2026-08-25, /bmad-build)

Jerome's decision was to configure the identity in ChatBot and leave the SDK contract closed. Done, with the
mechanism stated as a falsifiable claim first and each falsifier then checked.

#### The hypothesis was partly wrong, and the falsification check caught it before it was reported

- [x] [Review][Correction] **The `AppId` was never empty — it resolved to `Hexalith.ChatBot.Server`.** The previous
  pass reported "nothing configures `DomainProjectionIdentityOptions`, so its `AppId` is empty". The SDK in fact
  binds `EventStore:DomainService` and defaults an unconfigured `AppId` to the `DAPR_APP_ID` environment variable,
  **falling back to `IHostEnvironment.ApplicationName`** (`EventStoreDomainServiceExtensions.cs:447-458`). Measured
  in a host without that variable, the identity resolved to the assembly name — not blank. Same consequence
  (`400 UnsupportedCapability` on every refresh), different cause, and it changes the fix: the pin must **override**
  a derived default, not merely fill an empty one. Caught by the first run of the new test, before reporting.

#### Fix

- [x] [Review][Patch] **`DomainProjectionIdentityOptions` is now pinned to the DAPR app id.** `Program.cs` post-configures `AppId = "chatbot"` and `ServiceVersion = "v1"` from `ChatBotDomainServiceIdentity`, overridable through `ChatBot:ProjectionIdentity`, with `ValidateOnStart` so a blank identity fails startup rather than degrading silently. Guarded by `ChatBotProjectionIdentityTests` (4 cases): the resolved identity is non-blank, equals the DAPR app id, **is not the assembly name**, survives a blank configured override, and honours a real one. This is a behaviour change — it switches the service onto the v2 named-projection delivery path — so it is written up as an ADR decision with **Winston's ratification explicitly marked outstanding**, not assumed.

- [x] [Review][Patch] **The readiness failure message now names the side of the gap that is broken.** "listener did not accept traffic" was emitted while the process was accepting traffic and answering `503`. The message is now derived from the observation: unreachable → "the listener is genuinely absent"; reachable but non-success → "accepting traffic and reporting itself UNHEALTHY, so the resource is not ready"; fresh client succeeds where the retained one does not → "the retained endpoint is stale". The readiness loop, its budget and its success condition are unchanged.

#### Falsifiers, stated before the run and then checked

| # | Would falsify the mechanism | Result |
| --- | --- | --- |
| F1 | `400`s persist for `chatbot` after pinning the identity | **Cleared** — named-projection dispatch failures **21,034 → 0**; the only remaining metadata refusals are `sample` (6) and `tenants` (8), services whose identities were not changed |
| F2 | `400`s stop but checkpoint refusals continue | **Cleared** — `ProjectionCheckpointSaveExhausted` **42,066 → 0**, and 0 of the new structural `ProjectionCheckpointAdvanceRefused` |
| F3 | Refusals stop but `cleanup-complete` stays false | **Cleared** — `eventstore-outage` now reports `cleanup-complete: true`, and the gate reason `continuity:cleanup_incomplete` is gone |
| F4 | A verdict changes once delivery actually advances | **Hit — reported below** |

The mechanism is therefore confirmed, with the corrected first link.

#### F4: two verdicts moved, and the gate still stop-ships

Run `01M0WDJMBFS4ESNN618SY2HSA9`, nine scenarios measured:

| Scenario | Before | After |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `eventstore-outage` | met | **met** (RPO 0 s, RTO 152.6 s; all seven assertions true, including `cleanup-complete`) |
| `m365-subscription-failure` | met | **met** |
| `recovery-baseline` | equivalent | **equivalent** |
| `graph`, `ai-provider`, `command-execution`, `audit-store` | contained | **contained** |
| `identity` | contained | **unmeasurable** |
| `attachment-processing` | contained | **unmeasurable** |

Both moved scenarios fail with *"The independent control operation did not succeed while '…' was faulted"* — the
**answered-but-not-202** branch of the message split earlier this pass, not the never-answered branch. So the
control operation is reaching ChatBot and being refused. Since switching to the v2 named-projection delivery path
changes how a governed note reaches its projection, and the control probe waits for exactly that projection, the
two are plausibly connected — **but that is a hypothesis, not a finding, and it is not investigated here.**
Nothing was relaxed: `IndependentControlSucceeded` still fails closed and still gates the scenario.

**The earlier six-`contained` bundle does not stand.** The current honest state is seven measurable-and-clean
scenarios plus two `unmeasurable`, and the gate still stop-ships — for a different reason than before.

#### Environment note

A later confirmation run failed earlier still (`governed checkpoint command was not accepted`,
`reason=audit_unavailable`) with zero placement refusals logged and the placement port open — the same
intermittent dispatch flakiness recorded throughout this pass, not a new signal. The counts in F1/F2 above are
from that run and are unaffected: they measure the absence of refusals, which is unambiguous.


### Review Findings — the two moved verdicts are intermittent, not caused by the pinning; lane passes end to end (2026-08-25, /bmad-build)

Falsifiers were stated before the runs: **F1** the control probe gets a non-`202`, so ChatBot refused it; **F2** it
throws a transport error, so it never reached ChatBot (and the message class was mislabelling that as a refusal);
**F3** reverting the pin restores `contained`, which would make the pinning causal.

#### Observability first — the split was still conflating two causes

- [x] [Review][Patch] **MEDIUM — "did not succeed" covered both a real refusal and a connection that never landed.** The earlier split separated a client timeout from everything else, but an `HttpRequestException` still fell into the refusal branch — so "the independent control operation did not succeed" could mean ChatBot answered non-`202` *or* that the probe never reached ChatBot. Those are different investigations, and during an `identity` outage the second is entirely plausible. The observation now carries `IndependentControlCause` — a stable `status-{code}`, `transport-{error}` or `client-timeout` — and a transport failure is classified as **unobserved** rather than negative evidence. The driver reports "was refused (cause=…)" versus "was never observed (cause=…)". Both still fail closed and `IndependentControlSucceeded` is untouched; only the classification and the reported cause changed.

#### F3 answered: the pinning is not the cause

- [x] [Review][Verified] **Three consecutive runs with the pin still ON came back clean.** Of the four runs since the identity was pinned, one failed on `identity` + `attachment-processing` and **three passed with all nine scenarios clean**. A deterministic consequence of the delivery-path switch cannot behave that way, so **H1 is falsified** — the pinning did not cause it, and no revert trade-off needs to go to Jerome or Winston on these grounds.

- [ ] [Review][Note] **What it actually is remains undetermined, and is recorded as such.** The single failure occurred *before* the cause diagnostic above existed, so its `status` versus `transport` class was never captured and cannot be recovered. It has not recurred in three subsequent runs. That leaves two live possibilities — an intermittent latent defect that the previous re-delivery storm was masking (the same shape as the restart path found earlier this pass), or the environment — and **one occurrence is not enough to choose between them.** The next occurrence will name its cause. Deliberately not resolved by running until it disappeared: it did not recur, and that is reported as a rate, not as a fix.

#### The lane now passes end to end

Runs `01M0WFJE0BADDQ3BMNEF0B2RJF`, `01M0WGJY0JAMMN3AVAS1G6477V` and `01M0WHJG25EQAP48ZG01DK13F7` each produced
**nine manifests, nine reports, zero deviations, and the evidence gate did not stop-ship** (`Total tests: 1,
Passed: 1`):

| Exercise | Verdict |
| --- | --- |
| `eventstore-outage` | **met** |
| `m365-subscription-failure` | **met** |
| `recovery-baseline` | **equivalent** |
| `graph`, `identity`, `ai-provider`, `command-execution`, `audit-store`, `attachment-processing` | **contained** (all six) |

This is the first time the required producer lane has run green from end to end in this story. **It is a local run
under ignored `TestResults/`: not repository-retained, not citable, and it supports no A10, NFR56, NFR57 or NFR41
claim.** A10 stays provisional, `RecoveryTargets` unchanged, the ADR stays `Proposed`, and Winston's ratification
of the projection-identity pin and Murat's authenticity approval both remain outstanding.

#### Bookkeeping carried forward

- **The 2026-08-25 six-`contained` bundle from before the pin does not stand** and must not be cited; the current
  reference is the three runs above, which are themselves local and non-citable.
- **The intermittent dispatch flake is still open, with a measured rate:** `reason=audit_unavailable` /
  "governed checkpoint command was not accepted" appeared in **3 of 30** full-topology runs today. It obstructs a
  run when it hits, is environment rather than repository (it correlates with the DAPR placement/dispatch
  instability recorded throughout this pass, not with any change), and it is recorded rather than dropped. At that
  rate a hosted lane would fail roughly one run in ten for reasons unrelated to the system under test.


### Review Findings — hosted-lane blockers and the adversarial batch (2026-08-25, /bmad-build)

#### A — hosted-lane blockers

- [x] [Review][Patch] **A1 HIGH — the Tier-3 harness now provisions the writer protocol itself, and it is proven by A/B.** A hosted runner starts against an empty Redis, where `ProjectionDeliveryWriterProtocolHealthCheck` reports EventStore `Unhealthy` until a store-global marker exists — which is why release run `32872142514` died on `freshClientHealth=503`, and why three local runs were green only on a marker another application's harness had left behind. `RecoveryWriterProtocolProvisioner` drives the operator-facing admin endpoint (`api/v1/admin/projections/delivery-writer-protocol/activate`) with the realm's global-administrator bearer, **before** the readiness waits — readiness depends on it, so waiting for health first deadlocks — with a bounded budget that tolerates a not-yet-listening store and fails closed on any non-startup refusal. **A/B, not asserted:** marker deleted and no provisioner → readiness `503` (reproduced 3× locally and once hosted); marker deleted **with** provisioner → producer exits 0, nine manifests, and the marker in the store now carries **this repository's commit** instead of the neighbouring harness's. Two false starts along the way are part of the evidence: provisioning after the health waits failed `Connection refused`, and a ChatBot-realm token was rejected `401` — the admin API validates tokens minted for the EventStore client.
- [x] [Review][Patch] **A2 — the topology-acceptance MSBuild race got the same treatment as the `build` job.** Every `dotnet test` invocation in `ci.yml` and `release.yml` that lacked it now passes `-m:1` (2 in CI, 1 in release), so parallel nodes cannot race on shared `references/**` outputs. Both workflows still parse.

#### B — evidence integrity

- [x] [Review][Patch] **B1 HIGH — raw logs no longer reach uploaded evidence.** `RecoveryResourceLogTail` rendered captured lines into an exception message on the lane that uploads its whole `TestResults` directory, so raw resource logs became retained evidence and the type's own "never written to an artifact" claim was false. `Render()` now emits **derived metadata only** — captured-line count plus the distinct logger categories and stable `Stage=` tokens, each accepted only if it is a safe stable identifier — and never captured content. The retained response body stays, because it is ChatBot's own redacted ProblemDetails. The doc comment now states what the type actually does. *(The lane-level sanitizer asymmetry is recorded as deferred defence-in-depth rather than fixed here.)*
- [x] [Review][Patch] **B2 HIGH — the absence probe now has a positive control.** `RemainsGovernedOperationProjectionAbsentAsync` had never been shown to read a view the server wrote, so a wrong tenant partition or component would return "absent" for everything and both NFR59 absence and cleanup verification would pass vacuously. Every absence result is now preceded by `AssertProjectionChannelReadsServerWritesAsync`, a known-present read of a seeded checkpoint note through the **same** store, component and key shape; if that read comes back null the drill throws instead of reporting absence.
- [x] [Review][Patch] **B3 HIGH — the cleanup diagnostic can now fire in the case it exists for.** Three of its five sub-checks were unreachable: presence was probed through the read API, where a missing governed operation answers `403` safe-not-found, which threw past both `catch (TimeoutException)` handlers and took the whole cleanup — and its `RECOVERY_CLEANUP_INCOMPLETE` line — with it. Presence now reads the projection channel, matching the absence half, with fail-closed catches that record `checkpoint-note-presence-faulted` / `control-note-presence-faulted` instead of escaping.
- [ ] [Review][Deferred] **B4 — control-probe catch scope not narrowed.** The `HttpRequestException` / `TaskCanceledException` handlers still wrap token acquisition and the post-202 durable and projection waits, so a genuine post-admission containment failure can still be attributed to the transport. The cause is now *reported* (`status-…` / `transport-…` / `client-timeout`), which makes the misattribution visible rather than silent, but the scope narrowing itself is **not done** and is called out here rather than implied.

#### C — correctness

- [x] **C1/C2/C3** — the identity pin no longer breaks non-`chatbot` deployments. Resolution is by precedence — `ChatBot:ProjectionIdentity` → `EventStore:DomainService` → `DAPR_APP_ID` → pinned constant — so SDK binding and the DAPR-supplied id are live again and a prefixed, canary or multi-instance deployment resolves correctly. `.Validate` is now reachable and meaningful: it rejects any resolved component that is not a safe stable identifier, which is configurable and therefore falsifiable. The real invariant is asserted where both assemblies are visible: `ChatBotDomainServiceIdentityContractTests` compares `ChatBotDomainServiceIdentity.AppId` against `ChatBotAspireModule.AppId` — **verified falsifiable**, renaming either literal turns it red — plus precedence, usability and end-to-end override coverage (16 cases).
- [x] **C4** — the durable half of the NFR59 absence assertion is always awaited, so a fault in it is a verdict input rather than an unobserved task exception.
- [x] **C5** — the post-erase cleanup block now catches the same exception class as its sibling; both call a helper that reads Dapr directly.
- [x] **C8** — `CheckpointAdvanceRefused` moved off the id it shared with `PollerRebuildConflict` to an unused `1152`, with `ProjectionLogEventIdUniquenessTests` guarding the subsystem. That guard found **three pre-existing collisions** (`1120`, `1121`, `4660`); renumbering shipped ids changes what operators' filters match, so they are pinned explicitly in the test and ledgered, and any **new** collision fails.
- [x] **C10 (first half)** — the dispatch-unavailable problem type is now a single `ChatBotProblemTypes` constant shared by the factory and the admission probe, so changing the URI can no longer silently stop the probe recognising admission.
- [ ] **C6, C7, C9, C10 (second half) — not done.** C7's `observedAtLeastOnce` is documented but its guard is unchanged; C6 (unbounded `DisposeAsync` join in `RecoveryResourceLogTail`, and its tails sharing the CTS that triggers the diagnostic), C9 (blocking, cancellation-ignoring `StopAsync` and the startup-failure dispose path) and the unused `Render(string? matching)` filter are untouched. Each is real; none is fixed.

#### D — tests

- [x] **D1** — `WaitUntilBlocked()` now asserts the exporter actually parked, so the two timing tests can no longer pass vacuously against an unblocked exporter; `BlockingExporter.Dispose` no longer disposes a signal the sleeping worker will still `Set()`.
- [x] **D3** — `IndependentControlCause` is now covered through the real driver for all four shapes, asserting the `(cause=…)` suffix.
- [x] **D4** — the admission proof's `503` requirement is covered alongside the body predicate.
- [ ] **D2 partially, D5/D6 not done.** D2 still covers one refusal reason of four; the other three need delivery rows the classifier reports as regressed/unsupported, which this fixture cannot yet build — a draft was written and **reverted rather than shipped half-working**, and the gap is documented in the test's own remarks. The inert `GetStateAsync` arrangement, D5 (token-identity comparison in the capture filter) and D6 (dead `expectPresent: false` path) are untouched.

#### E — the record

- [x] **E1** — the runsettings header no longer claims the malformed file killed the lanes "silently". A rejected settings file exits non-zero and those jobs went red; what kept it unnoticed is that the recovery lanes run only on `schedule`/`workflow_dispatch`, so ordinary pushes never executed them. Corrected at the source of the claim.
- [x] **E2** — the guard no longer stops at "XDocument parses it". `RunSettingsIsAcceptedByTheTestPlatform` runs the real runner against the real file and fails if the platform rejects the settings, closing the well-formed-but-schema-invalid case that would otherwise reproduce guard-green/lane-dead.

#### F — ledgered, not fixed

`deferred-work.md` gains four entries: the malformed-payload `503`-plus-alert API-contract change (F1); the ChatBot **UI** host's unbounded telemetry shutdown, **with the `internal sealed` visibility constraint stated** so the trade-off is visible rather than silently worked around (F2); the recovery lanes' missing sanitizer projection (defence in depth); and the three pre-existing duplicate event ids.

#### Verification

Fresh-store confirmation with every fix in place: marker deleted, producer exits 0, **nine manifests — two `met`, one `equivalent`, six `contained`, zero deviations** (`01M0X2404SDNBK9S69WHCQHQHQ`), and the marker left carrying this repository's commit. Server 1782/0/0, IntegrationTests 207/0/5, Architecture 78/0/0, Conformance 98/0/0, AppHost 14/0/0, solution build 0 errors; EventStore `Server.Tests` 3137/0/25. **Historical verification correction (2026-08-26): `Hexalith.ChatBot.StoryEvidenceGate.Tests` was not run or recorded in this v1.25 pass; its omission was a verification gap, not an implied green result.** The current closeout run is recorded under v1.28. **These remain local runs under ignored `TestResults/` — not repository-retained, not citable, supporting no A10/NFR56/NFR57/NFR41 claim.**


### Review Findings — C6/C9 closed, remainder ledgered (2026-08-25, /bmad-build)

- [x] [Review][Patch] **C6 HIGH — the diagnostic helper can no longer become the failure, hang the lane, or be cancelled by its own trigger.** Three defects, three fixes. (1) `CaptureAsync` rethrew a capture-loop fault, which surfaced at scope exit and **replaced the genuine test failure already unwinding** — precisely how this session kept losing diagnoses; the fault is now retained as a renderable `captureFault=` token and the loop ends quietly. (2) `DisposeAsync` awaited the capture task unbounded — the same unbounded-join pathology removed from the EventStore host — and now waits under a 5-second drain budget, swallowing everything, because disposal runs while a real failure may already be unwinding. (3) The tails were linked to `startup.Token`, the very CTS whose expiry triggers the diagnostic, so the capture raced its own trigger and could render empty exactly when needed; the tail now owns an independent lifetime.
- [x] [Review][Patch] **C9 — `StopAsync` honours the token it is handed.** Three blocking provider shutdowns ran inline for up to six seconds while ignoring the host's token, so a host with a shorter shutdown timeout could not stop. They now run off the calling thread with the wait observing the token. Abandoning that wait is safe and still achieves the point of the service: `BaseProcessor.Shutdown` takes its one-shot interlock **on entry**, so the shutdown is already claimed when the token fires and later disposal returns immediately instead of joining the exporter thread without a timeout. Covered by `StopAsync_ReturnsPromptlyWhenTheHostCancelsTheStop`, **verified falsifiable** — ignoring the token turns it red — with the assertion deliberately tighter than the budget so "under the budget" cannot pass trivially.
- [ ] [Review][Deferred] **B4, C7, C10b, D2 (3 of 4), D5, D6 stood down and ledgered.** Six entries in `deferred-work.md`, each stating what is **not** covered so none reads as done: the control probe's over-wide catch scope (mitigated only in that the cause is now reported, not in that the classification is right); `observedAtLeastOnce` guarding nothing it appears to; the unused `Render` filter; the three unasserted refusal reason codes plus that test's inert arrangement; the untested, approximate capture-filter token check; and the now-unreachable absence path whose comment still documents it.

**Verification.** Fresh-store confirmation with C6 and C9 in place: producer exits 0, nine manifests, **two `met`, one `equivalent`, six `contained`, zero deviations** (`01M0X3DYCAZK4D8AXHY6NX1AHF`) — identical to the pre-C6/C9 bundle, so neither change moved a verdict. Server 1782/0/0, IntegrationTests 207/0/5, Architecture 78/0/0, Conformance 98/0/0, AppHost 14/0/0, solution build 0 errors; EventStore `Server.Tests` 3138/0/25. **Historical verification correction (2026-08-26): `Hexalith.ChatBot.StoryEvidenceGate.Tests` was not run or recorded in this v1.26 pass; its omission was a verification gap, not an implied green result.** The current closeout run is recorded under v1.28. Local runs under ignored `TestResults/` — not repository-retained, not citable, supporting no A10/NFR56/NFR57/NFR41 claim.


### Review Findings — /bmad-code-review chunk A: Tier-3 harness and live drivers (2026-08-25)

Scope: `476f1de` → `HEAD`, files under `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/Recovery/` (1,277 diff lines) — the harness half of the post-review delta, i.e. the patch application and the A/B/C hosted-lane blocker work. Four layers ran in parallel (Blind Hunter, Edge Case Hunter, Verification Gap, Acceptance Auditor); no layer failed. Every finding below was re-verified against source before rating; two were dismissed on verification. Findings already ledgered in `deferred-work.md` by this same diff (B4, C7, C10b, D5, D6) are not re-raised.

#### Decision needed

- [x] [Review][Decision] **HIGH — `RecoveryWriterProtocolProvisioner` treats `409 Conflict` as activation success, which both breaks fail-closed and reinstates the foreign-marker contamination A1 claims to have eliminated.** `RecoveryWriterProtocolProvisioner.cs:77-82` returns on `OK or Conflict`, with the comment "Conflict means a marker for a different cutover is already current, which still satisfies the readiness contract". Upstream, `ProjectionDeliveryCutover.ActivateAsync` (`references/Hexalith.EventStore/src/Hexalith.EventStore.Server/Projections/ProjectionDeliveryCutover.cs:23-30`, and again at `:41-47` for the concurrent-write path) returns `Conflict` when a marker exists and **either** it is current with a different commit **or** it is not current at all. Two distinct outcomes are collapsed. (a) *Non-current marker:* `ProjectionDeliveryWriterProtocolHealthCheck` requires `marker?.IsCurrent == true` (`ProjectionDeliveryWriterProtocolHealthCheck.cs:16-18`), so readiness can never be satisfied; the provisioner reports "activated", the lane walks into `WaitForResourceHealthyAsync("eventstore")` and hangs to the 5-minute startup CTS, surfacing as the listener-gap diagnostic instead of naming the marker. This contradicts the class's own stated contract ("fails closed: if activation does not report success the drill must not proceed"). (b) *Current-but-foreign marker:* the run proceeds on another application's marker — exactly the shared-development-Redis contamination the A1 narrative blames for three falsely green local runs — and nothing distinguishes "activated by this run" from "inherited", so the A1 claim that "the marker in the store now carries **this repository's commit**" is not established by the code for that path. **Decision:** read the marker back after `Conflict` and fail closed on non-current, treating current-but-foreign as an explicitly recorded inherited-marker provenance value / fail closed on any `Conflict` (strict, but makes a shared dev Redis red until swept) / keep accepting but emit the inherited-vs-activated distinction into the manifest. Sources: blind-hunter+edge-case-hunter+acceptance-auditor.
- [x] [Review][Decision] **MEDIUM — the nested startup budgets now exceed the 5-minute startup deadline they run under, so the lane's own timeout ladder is internally inconsistent.** `LiveContinuityAspireE2eTests.cs:108-109` sets `startup.CancelAfter(TimeSpan.FromMinutes(5))`. Under that single token the lane now runs `AcquireGlobalAdministratorAsync` (3-minute retry loop, `RecoveryAccessTokenProvider.cs:89-95`) **plus** `ActivationBudget` (3 minutes, `RecoveryWriterProtocolProvisioner.cs:35`) at `:116-118` — 6 minutes of bounded work before the four `WaitForResourceHealthyAsync` calls at `:120-124` and two further 3-minute token loops at `:141-146`. Expiry surfaces as a bare `OperationCanceledException`, and because the log tails do not start until `:128` it carries no cause. Task 6 explicitly obliges this story to "cross-validate live-validation schedule, evidence freshness, per-scenario timeout, restoration timeout, and workflow timeout before a run", and the obligation "do not claim a target measured when a shorter harness timeout caused `Unmeasurable`" applies directly. **Decision:** raise the startup budget to cover the worst-case sum and add a contract test relating the inner budgets to it / give provisioning and token acquisition their own linked sub-budgets sized to fit inside 5 minutes / reduce the inner retry budgets. The numbers interact with the workflow-timeout ladder this story tuned in the previous round, so this is a human call. Sources: edge-case-hunter+acceptance-auditor.

### Review Findings — /bmad-code-review chunk B: Server and AppHost product surface (2026-08-25)

Scope: `476f1de` → `HEAD`, files under `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.AppHost/` and `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/` (346 diff lines) — the Keycloak owner-marker sweep, the `ChatBotProblemTypes` extraction, and the projection-identity pin. Four layers ran; no layer failed. Every finding re-verified against source before rating; three dismissed on verification.

**Binding obligations: no violations.** Nothing in this chunk gives the Server AppHost/DCP or resource-lifecycle authority (the only lifecycle-adjacent code is the AppHost's own temp-directory sweep), no live driver is registered in product DI, no fault-control type reaches Contracts/Domain/UI, and the new `InternalsVisibleTo` runs AppHost → IntegrationTests, the direction Task 1 sanctions.

#### Decision needed

- [x] [Review][Decision] **MEDIUM — `FirstUsable` filters on blank only, so a malformed value the service does not own crash-loops it instead of falling through to the pinned constant.** `ChatBotDomainServiceIdentity.cs:71-72` selects the first non-blank candidate, but the startup gate at `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Program.cs:47-50` validates with `IsUsableIdentityComponent`, which additionally rejects anything over 128 characters or outside `[A-Za-z0-9._-]`. A malformed higher-precedence candidate — notably `DAPR_APP_ID`, supplied by the platform rather than by this service — therefore short-circuits the chain and then fails `ValidateOnStart`, so the host never boots. That contradicts both the method's name and the `Program.cs:31-32` comment calling the constant "a last-resort fallback for hosts that supply neither". **Decision:** filter `FirstUsable` by `IsUsableIdentityComponent` so a malformed candidate falls through to the next tier (keeps the service up, but the gate then becomes unreachable because the constant is always valid) / keep the hard fail-fast and correct the name and comment to state that any malformed identity aborts startup deliberately, on the grounds that a silent capability refusal post-cutover is worse than a crash-loop. Both are defensible and the choice changes what the guard means. Sources: blind-hunter+edge-case-hunter.
- [x] [Review][Decision] **MEDIUM — marker-less realm directories are now spared forever, converting a bounded leak into an unbounded one.** `IsKeycloakRealmOwnerAlive` returns `true` when no `owner.marker` exists (`src/Hexalith.ChatBot.AppHost/Program.cs:390-393`). Every directory left by a pre-change AppHost, plus any run whose marker write hit the swallowed `catch` at `:349-355`, is now permanently unsweepable — precisely the "killed prior run leaves a secret-bearing directory behind" case the sweep exists for, and which the deleted one-hour age gate did collect. **Decision:** add a markers-absent-only age fallback (e.g. delete when there is no marker *and* creation time is older than a threshold well beyond the longest lane budget) / accept permanent accumulation for marker-less directories and record it as a residual / sweep marker-less directories at exit only. The first re-introduces an age component into a decision that was ratified on 2026-08-25 as "PID-liveness ... correct regardless of run duration", so it is a human call. Sources: blind-hunter+edge-case-hunter+verification-gap.
