#### Defer

- [x] [Review][Defer] MEDIUM — pid + start-time liveness is pid-namespace-local and records no machine, boot or container discriminator, so on a host where the temp directory is shared across pid namespaces (a mounted `/tmp` in CI containers) or survives a reboot (Windows `%TEMP%`), a foreign pid can either alias a live local process and be spared forever, or mismatch and be deleted while live [src/Hexalith.ChatBot.AppHost/Program.cs:342-430] — deferred, the marker scheme is a strict improvement on the mtime proxy it replaced and a boot-id/machine-id discriminator is a separate design step

#### Dismissed on verification (3)

- The two hunters made opposite claims about the `Math.Abs(...) < 1.0` start-time tolerance (too tight → sweeps a live owner; too loose → lets a recycled pid impersonate). Neither holds: `Process.StartTime` is stable for a given pid and the recorded value came from the same API, so the tolerance absorbs serialization and foreign-`/proc` read jitter, while a recycled pid would have to start within one second of the original's start — a moment at which the original was still alive and holding the pid.
- The stray `owner.marker` inside the bind-mounted realm-import directory: Keycloak's directory-import provider selects `*-realm.json`, and the lane has started Keycloak repeatedly with the marker present.
- The Acceptance Auditor found no violation of this chunk's governing obligations (no Server DCP or resource-lifecycle authority, no live driver in product DI, no fault-control type in Contracts/Domain/UI, `InternalsVisibleTo` in the sanctioned direction) — recorded here rather than raised as a finding.

### Review Findings — /bmad-code-review chunk C: evidence gate tool, policy and CI (2026-08-25)

Scope: `476f1de` → `HEAD`, `tools/Hexalith.ChatBot.StoryEvidenceGate/**`, `story-evidence-policy.json`, `live-recovery.runsettings`, `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `release.yml` (772 diff lines) — the application of Resolutions 1, 2 and 3 from the previous pass. Four layers ran; no layer failed. Every finding re-verified against source before rating; two dismissed on verification.

**This chunk contains a ship-blocking regression: the required completion job cannot run at all.** See the first patch item.

#### Decision needed

- [x] [Review][Decision] **HIGH — Resolution 1's *Rejected* option is what shipped: the freshness ceiling was raised for every declared lane, not just `recovery-primary`.** The resolution reads "Give `recovery-primary` its own long current-run ceiling … and **keep the global 60 minutes for the fast lanes**", listing "raising the global bound (weakens staleness detection ~6x for lanes where 60 minutes is both meaningful and achievable)" as explicitly Rejected. In `story-evidence-policy.json`, `"maximumCurrentRunAgeMinutes": 360` is added to **all five** bindings — `:90` `browser-primary`, `:114` `signalr-primary`, `:139` `hosting-assets-primary`, `:162` `aspire-dapr-primary` and `:186` `recovery-primary` — so the global `60` at `:5` now governs only the undeclared `story-evidence-gate` lane. The implementation is not careless: the comment at `.github/workflows/ci.yml:275-279` concedes the reason ("The upstream build/topology lanes cannot be refreshed by ordering"), i.e. the resolution's premise was wrong — recovery-first reordering refreshes only the in-job lane, and the four fast lanes' TRX finish timestamps are fixed in the `build` job that precedes everything. Note the inversion this produces: `recovery-primary`'s TRX is sanitized *minutes* before attestation and needs the least headroom, while the four fast lanes are what actually consume the 6× relaxation — so on `recovery-primary` the wide window purely weakens the guarantee, admitting a six-hour-old TRX as "current-run". The story's "Patch application summary" nonetheless states "All six resolved decisions … were applied". **Decision:** ratify the reversal and correct the record and the ci.yml comment (which currently claims the opposite: "instead of relaxing the global bound for everyone") / re-produce the four fast lanes inside the completion job after the drill so they are genuinely fresh, keeping 60 for them / split recovery production into a separate job whose output is a retained rather than current-run source (the resolution's own third option, never tried) / keep 360 only on `recovery-primary` and accept that it buys nothing there. Sources: acceptance-auditor+blind-hunter.

#### Patch

- [x] [Review][Patch] **HIGH — The recovery-first reordering removed the completion job's only build of the gate tool, so the required `story-evidence-integrity` job now fails at its first .NET command on every push and pull request.** `Run gate self-tests with current-run TRX` runs `dotnet test` on `StoryEvidenceGate.Tests` with no `--no-build`, and that project `ProjectReference`s the tool, so it was the job's only producer of the tool's `bin/Release` output. At `476f1de` it sat at line 140, *before* `Plan proposed completion production` at line 185; this diff moved it to line 280, after the drill. The job contains no `dotnet restore` and no `dotnet build` anywhere (the ones at `ci.yml:60`/`:67` belong to the `build` job), yet `Plan` runs `dotnet run … --configuration Release --no-build -- plan`. Cascade: `Plan` fails → `steps.artifacts.outputs.requires_recovery` is never set → the `always() && … == 'true'` steps (Summarize recovery attempt, Upload recovery evidence) evaluate false and skip, so ADV-3's new failure summary — the other headline deliverable of this same round — never runs either. `LiveRecoveryValidationArchitectureTests.cs:217` now actively pins the broken ordering, so any correct fix must also edit that guard [.github/workflows/ci.yml:185-194, :280-286]
- [x] [Review][Patch] HIGH — The new per-lane `maximumCurrentRunAgeMinutes` is the only `recognizedLaneBindings` field `ValidatePinnedPolicy` does not pin: `lane`, `selector`, `trx`, `provenance` and `sources` are all hard-pinned and the global ceiling is pinned to `!= 60` at `:385`, but the override is bounded only by `<= 1440` inside the resolver. Any lane — including a fast unit lane — can be silently raised to 24 hours with no version bump, no reason code and no failing test; `story-evidence-policy.json` is in no trigger's `pathPatterns`, so such a change need produce no primary-path evidence at all. This removes exactly the tamper-resistance the original finding's reasoning rested on ("hard-pinned to exactly 60 … cannot be raised without a policy version bump") [tools/Hexalith.ChatBot.StoryEvidenceGate/StoryEvidenceValidator.cs:381-390, :460-515]
- [x] [Review][Patch] MEDIUM — ADV-4 is only half-closed: the completion artifact no longer collides with another job's name, but it still does not contain the evidence its own manifests point at. Every completion manifest records `artifact:completion-recovery-evidence`, while the drill writes its reports and manifests to `<repoRoot>/TestResults/live-recovery/<runId>` and the upload covers only `runner.temp/recovery-attempt` and `runner.temp/machine-results/recovery-primary` — no completion-job upload covers `TestResults/**` [.github/workflows/ci.yml:242, :302-311, tests/…/Recovery/LiveContinuityAspireE2eTests.cs:158, :475-487]
- [x] [Review][Patch] MEDIUM — ADV-3's failure summary cannot run on the failure path it exists for: the step is correctly `if: always()`, but its only build prerequisite (`Run gate self-tests`, `:280`) carries no `if:`, so GitHub's implied `success()` skips it the moment the drill fails, leaving no built assembly for the `always()` summarizer [.github/workflows/ci.yml:280-293]

The actual cause — `--logger "console;verbosity=detailed"` feeding an unbounded TRX for four hours — is untouched [tools/…/RecoveryTrxSanitizer.cs:26-33] — resolved: the byte and character ceilings are aligned at 64 MB, all three recovery producer invocations use minimal rather than detailed console verbosity, and architecture guards pin both CI producers and the release producer against regression. The size-bound regression passed, focused Architecture passed 17/17, and both workflows pass `actionlint`.

#### Defer

A story with `primaryPaths: []` that touches nothing recovery-related can name an ordinary lane `recovery-primary` and inherit the 6× relaxation for arbitrary TRX content [tools/…/StoryEvidenceValidator.cs:309-350, TrxEvidenceReader.cs:265] — deferred, this is the pre-existing lane-resolution shape rather than something this change introduced, and it is subsumed by whichever direction the ceiling decision above takes

#### Dismissed on verification (2)

- The claim that bumping `minimumSupportedVersion` to `2.1` in lockstep invalidates up to 30 days of retained `2.0`-era evidence: verified false. The field is read once and pinned in `ValidatePinnedPolicy` and is never used to admit or reject any artifact. Its inertness is real and is recorded as a LOW patch above; the stated consequence does not follow.
- Redaction-sentinel and surrogate-boundary nitpicks on the summarizer's `SafeToken`: shape-filtered ASCII identity fields, cosmetic at most.

### Review Findings — /bmad-code-review chunk D: tests, ADR and BMAD documents (2026-08-25)

Scope: `476f1de` → `HEAD`, the Architecture / Server / StoryEvidenceGate / IntegrationTests contract suites, `docs/adrs/live-recovery-validation-drivers.md`, `README.md`, the story file, `deferred-work.md`, `architecture.md` and the three review artifacts (1,748 diff lines). Four layers ran; no layer failed. Every finding re-verified against source — several by direct `git` inspection — before rating; two dismissed on verification.

**Honest and confirmed first.** Status (`in-progress`), ADR (`Proposed`), `nfr-assessment.md` (`Overall Status: FAIL`) and `sprint-status.yaml` all agree. Task 7/8 and exactly the four evidence-dependent subtasks are unchecked with a stated rationale. Every local-run result is consistently caveated as "under ignored `TestResults/` — not repository-retained, not citable, supporting no A10/NFR56/NFR57/NFR41 claim", and **no verdict, threshold or budget was weakened anywhere in this diff**. The four "F — ledgered" and six "stood down" deferrals are all present in `deferred-work.md`, each stating what is *not* covered. The findings below are about the record, not about weakened gates.

#### Decision needed

- [x] [Review][Decision] **HIGH — A recorded observation in three governance records was overwritten rather than reconciled, inside the story whose subject is evidence integrity.** `review-adversarial.md:145`, `review-reality.md:128` and `review-rubric.md:108` each had their dated 2026-08-24 verification line changed from `182 passed` to `195 passed` — no annotation, no date change, no re-run note. The original finding was that these records *disagreed* with `nfr-assessment.md`; overwriting the historical number makes them agree by erasing what was observed. Two things make this a decision rather than a patch. First, the honest pattern exists in the very same diff: `review-rubric.md:55` corrects the v4/v2 → v7/v8 technology row with an inline "(Corrected 2026-08-25: this row still named the pre-remediation stack…)". Second, the rewrite did not even work — `review-reality.md:160` still reads "The prior **182/182** StoryEvidenceGate test result remains applicable", so the contradiction the patch claims to have closed survives two lines of the same file, and `review-reality.md` now mixes a rewritten count with a genuine preserved observation ("72 passed, 1 failed on an unrelated stale `ModelContextProtocol` version assertion"). **Decision:** restore `182` as the observed 2026-08-24 value and add an inline "(Superseded 2026-08-25: the suite is now 208 after N new cases)" note, matching the technology row's pattern / keep the rewrite and add the missing annotation plus fix `:160` / re-run and record a fresh dated line, leaving the 2026-08-24 observation intact. Sources: blind-hunter+acceptance-auditor.

## Dev Notes

### Current repository state at baseline

The three validation harnesses are already implemented and tested; this story must supply real measurements without rewriting their semantics:

| Validation | Deferred seam | Existing coordinator and contract |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Continuity | `DeferredContinuityDrillScenarioRunner` throws `continuity-drill live fault-injection runtime is M2-deferred` | `ContinuityDrillCoordinator`; closed scenarios `eventstore-outage` and `m365-subscription-failure`; `RecoveryTargets.MaxRpo`/`MaxRto`; `ContinuityDrillReport`/`Outcome`. |
| Projection rebuild | `DeferredProjectionRebuildDriver` throws `projection-rebuild live driver is M2-deferred` | `ProjectionRebuildValidationCoordinator`; immutable source + WORM only; configured dataset refs; structural/schema equivalence; `ProjectionRebuildReport`/`Outcome`. |
| Scoped outage | `DeferredScopedOutageInjectionDriver` throws `scoped-outage live fault-injection runtime is M2-deferred` | `ScopedOutageDegradationValidationCoordinator`; six dependency tokens and six scope tokens; serious containment, late-recording, and unmeasurable dimensions; `ScopedOutageDegradationReport`/`Outcome`. |

All three coordinators already reject a non-test tenant through `ReplayTenantPolicy`, convert infrastructure failures to metadata-only unmeasurable reports, audit before alerting, and keep report outcomes separate from alert-delivery success. Preserve those invariants and test the live implementation through the coordinator rather than bypassing it.

The current AppHost composes EventStore, Tenants, ChatBot, Keycloak, DAPR Redis components, EventStore Admin, and UI. It enables the Story 12.14 M2 audit/isolation sweeps and token-gated release endpoint, but it has no `replay-test:` tenant, live Graph/M365 resource, hosted Workers resource, or durable WORM binding. The ChatBot projection subscription is singular and targets only `tenant-alpha.chatbot.events`. Those are real implementation inputs, not assumptions a test may hide.

Story 12.14's runtime currently knows only `worm-audit-chain`, `replay-isolation-probe`, and `derived-store-isolation-probe`. Its review corrections are load-bearing for any status/gate model reused here: the latest attempt must win; freshness and future-clock checks fail closed; real positive coverage is required; one job must not suppress another; cadence state commits only after success; retries and alerts are bounded; detailed readiness remains token-protected; and hosted/release-path evidence is required. Its general hosted scheduler remains non-destructive.

### Architecture guardrails

- Preserve the dependency direction and domain purity described in `architecture.md`: Contracts remain technology-neutral; the Domain does not acquire Aspire/DAPR/fault-injection dependencies; infrastructure composition belongs in Server/AppHost/test infrastructure.
- One C# type per file, current namespace/layout conventions, XML documentation on internal/public types, nullable/warnings-as-errors, and central package management remain mandatory. Do not add inline package versions or upgrade Aspire/DAPR for this story.
- Use ULIDs for durable run/scenario identifiers. Use UTC timestamps and `TimeProvider`/the repository clock abstraction; do not use ad hoc local time.
- DAPR resiliency retries, timeouts, and circuit breakers may mask an outage. Record the effective policy and prove the injected fault reached the intended boundary before starting the recovery measurement. Do not assume HTTP and gRPC invocation paths have identical resilience behavior.
- Every destructive action must be allowlisted, target-resolved before execution, and restored in `finally`. After restoration, wait for declared health and run an end-state assertion. A failed cleanup is part of the verdict and release status, not a log-only warning.
- Never run live fault injection on production startup, on a production tenant, or merely because the existing M2 audit sweeps are enabled. Keep ordinary deployments safe and fail closed.
- A sandbox pass proves only the exact topology, configuration, scale, replicas, dataset, and external simulators named in its manifest. It does not prove AKS, production Graph, multi-replica scheduler/lease behavior, or durable production bindings unless those were actually present.

### Measurement and evidence rules

- Derive wall-clock start/end and durations from observed state transitions. Store both the target and measured value. A hardcoded duration, seeded verdict, or report assembled without a witnessed fault is fabricated evidence.
- RPO is proven by comparing the known committed-before-outage set with what is reconstructable after recovery; RTO ends only when the defined service/data recovery condition is healthy and end-state checks pass.
- Rebuild evidence includes dataset version/volume, immutable-source and audit-chain counts, projection schema versions, structural digests, resources compared, first divergence, and measured duration. Preserve failed rebuild partitions until evidence capture completes, then clean them safely.
- Scoped-outage evidence includes dependency, expected/observed scope, application-observed degradation/recovery, unaffected control operation, the three NFR59 safety assertions, in-flight recovery, duplicate-effect check, and measured scope-recording latency.
- Reports remain the canonical domain evidence objects. The live evidence manifest adds execution provenance and raw-artifact locators; it must not invent a second verdict model.
- A CI artifact upload is retention for the configured workflow period, not repository history. Completion Notes must say which retention mechanism applies and must not call a local ignored `.trx` “retained.”

### A10 decision semantics

`RecoveryTargets.MaxRpo = 15 minutes` and `MaxRto = 4 hours` are currently the single runtime source for A10/NFR56. The PRD describes them as starter targets pending this M2 drill. After the required EventStore and subscription exercises:

- **Confirm:** keep the numeric values, update the A10 decision/assumption marker to an evidence-backed commitment, and link the run.
- **Tighten:** a smaller maximum RPO/RTO is a stricter commitment; update the PRD, ADR, and `RecoveryTargets` together after review.
- **Loosen:** a larger maximum is a weaker commitment. It requires explicit rationale, release/product approval, risk/follow-up ownership, and the same atomic documentation/code update; a test timeout or unmeasurable run is not evidence for loosening.
- **Remediate and rerun:** keep the starter target, record the miss, implement corrective work, and repeat the drill. This is preferable when the target remains required.
- **Remain provisional** *(added by the round-4 review, 2026-08-01)*: keep the numeric values and record explicitly that the drill did **not** discharge the assumption. This is the correct outcome when the run passes but produces no retained hosted artifact locator, or when the lane's measurable ceiling sits below the target being claimed — a pass that cannot fail is not a confirmation. **Exit condition:** a complete passing scheduled-CI or release artifact whose hosted run/artifact locator is recorded in `.decision-log.md`, and whose claim limitations do not cover the target being ratified. **Owner:** Architecture/DevOps. This branch exists because the first four assume the drill can both pass and fail against the target; a sandbox lane that can only pass satisfies none of them, and forcing the outcome into "Confirm" would launder an unfalsifiable run into a commitment.

**Un-ratification.** A ratified A10 returns to provisional when the cited artifact ages past the 8-day `MaximumEvidenceAge` freshness boundary without a successor, when a later required run is not passing, or when a hosted run returns `Unmeasurable` or a structural breach. The artifact remains downloadable through day 30 for investigation, but it is not citable after day 8. Architecture/DevOps owns the transition and records it in the decision log.

The projection-rebuild and scope-recording decisions are recorded against NFR57 and NFR41 respectively, even when the same evidence bundle is reviewed.

### Project Structure Notes

Prefer new live implementations and control/evidence types as separate files adjacent to their owning layer rather than adding more types to the existing interface files. Exact names are ratified in the ADR; likely impact is:

**Likely updates**

- `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Program.cs`
- `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Projections/ChatBotDomainServiceIdentity.cs`
- `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server.Tests/Projections/ChatBotProjectionIdentityTests.cs`

- `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Gateway/CommandGatewayServiceCollectionExtensions.cs` — keep deferred product defaults and register product-side dataset/evidence services without granting fault authority.
- `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Operations/PeriodicEnforcement/PeriodicEnforcementRuntime.cs` and the `M2Sweep*.cs`/release-gate response files only if the approved ADR publishes external live evidence into operational readiness; the hosted runtime must remain non-destructive.
- `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.AppHost/Program.cs`, AppHost configuration, and `KeycloakRealms/hexalith-realm.json` — sandbox-only resources/options and the reserved validation identity.
- `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/TrivialGovernedCommandAspireE2ETests.cs` only for shared fixture extraction; prefer a new serialized recovery-specific Tier-3 test class for live orchestration/evidence.

**Likely new files**

- Product-side `IRecoveryValidationEvidenceSink`, evidence-manifest validation, recovery options, versioned projection-rebuild dataset/source, and their safe default implementations under `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Audit/` (exact names ratified by the ADR).
- One live implementation per deferred seam under `tests/Hexalith.ChatBot.IntegrationTests/Recovery/` (or an equally isolated Tier-3-only assembly), plus an `AspireRecoverySandboxFixture` and artifact writer. IntegrationTests already references Server/AppHost and has Server `InternalsVisibleTo` access.
- A bounded Graph/subscription provider simulator resource or adapter if required. It must never become a public production fault endpoint.
- If the existing interface files are touched, split each `Deferred*` implementation into its own file to restore the one-type-per-file baseline.
- Focused Server, Architecture, and Conformance test files mirroring the production type layout.
- A Story 12.15 live-driver ADR under `docs/adrs/`.

Do not create a second runtime degradation state machine, another test-tenant discriminator, a second periodic hosted service, a public arbitrary fault-injection API, or UI changes. Story 12.16's live Memories binding and asynchronous correction-propagation work remain separate.

### Testing Requirements

Minimum proof layers:

1. **Pure/unit:** option validation, Tier-3 harness composition plus deferred product-DI safety, allowlists/tenant guards, measurement derivation, report sanitization, timeout/cancellation/cleanup, evaluator/verdict preservation, evidence-manifest validation, and exhaustive reason-code/release-gate matrices.
2. **Coordinator/integration:** every live driver is invoked through its existing coordinator; audit-before-alert, unalerted breach reconciliation, non-zero coverage, isolated test partition, immutable-source-only rebuild, and end-state checks are asserted.
3. **Architecture/conformance:** fault authority cannot leak into Domain/Contracts/UI or default production composition; the rebuild cannot depend on Graph/current upstream state; test-only control is absent without explicit configuration; evidence remains metadata-only.
4. **Tier-3 Aspire/DAPR:** real EventStore resource outage plus controlled topology dependency faults, dedicated test tenant, versioned dataset, independent control tenant, restoration, health, DAPR state-store end-state, three real report families, and fail-closed negative cases.
5. **Release path:** the required workflow consumes fresh, complete, positively covered live evidence and uploads artifacts on pass or failure. A direct unit call or scripted fake is not release evidence.

Use `UseHexalithProjectReferences=true` for solution/build/integration validation because the referenced submodule package catalogs otherwise shadow the root catalog and currently fail restore/build. Run topology tests serially to avoid known port/file-lock contention, and report any infrastructure failure separately from an application verdict.

- Story 12.14 explicitly assigns the three recovery coordinators' scheduler/release-gate wiring to Story 12.15 because their deferred defaults still throw. This story fulfills that handoff through a required Tier-3/release job (per-commit non-cancelling release concurrency) and fail-closed live-evidence gate; merely changing DI registrations is insufficient, and putting resource-stop authority in the Server would be unsafe.

- Carry forward Story 12.14's evidence lesson: a fresh topology with process-local/in-memory stores can produce vacuous zero-breach results. Coverage/population and actual post-command/state-store data must prove work was present.
- Carry forward the repository action item that primary-path runtime claims require retained live evidence and that fallback/fixture evidence is diagnostic only.

### Latest Technical Information (verified 2026-08-01)

- The repository already pins Aspire `13.4.6`, the latest 13.4 patch currently available. Its patch includes an isolated-AppHost resource-service port-collision fix useful for Tier-3 concurrency. Stay on the centrally pinned version; no package update is required. [Aspire v13.4.6 release](https://github.com/microsoft/aspire/releases/tag/v13.4.6)
- Aspire supports programmatic resource commands through `ResourceCommandService`, and the CLI exposes allowlisted `start`, `stop`, and `restart` operations. Use supported resource commands from the orchestration/test boundary rather than shelling out or teaching the Server DCP internals. [Aspire custom resource commands](https://aspire.dev/fundamentals/custom-resource-commands/) [Aspire resource CLI](https://aspire.dev/reference/cli/commands/aspire-resource/)
- `DistributedApplicationTestingBuilder` is the supported way to launch/configure an AppHost under integration tests and can accept test-specific arguments/configuration without changing deployment defaults. [Manage the AppHost in tests](https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/aspire/testing/manage-app-host)
- The repository already pins Dapr .NET SDK `1.18.4`, the current 1.18 patch. Do not upgrade it here. Dapr resiliency policies can apply retries, timeouts, and circuit breakers to service/component calls, so evidence must record the active policy and prove the fault was observed rather than masked. [Dapr .NET SDK v1.18.4](https://github.com/dapr/dotnet-sdk/releases/tag/v1.18.4) [Dapr resiliency overview](https://docs.dapr.io/operations/resiliency/resiliency-overview/)
- Aspire dashboard custom commands are a local-development capability and are not automatically production Azure Container Apps controls. Treat that difference as a production-equivalence residual rather than extending the story's claim. [Aspire custom resource commands](https://aspire.dev/fundamentals/custom-resource-commands/)

Replaced with `ScopeForSignal`, keyed by a `FaultSignalCode` the failing component itself returns (`ai_provider_unavailable`, `audit_unavailable`, `attachment_dependency_unavailable`, `command_execution_unavailable`, `identity_token_unavailable`, `graph_subscription_expired` — the last two threaded through a new `faultSignalCode` query parameter on the sandbox's `/scope-observation` endpoint, sourced from the harness's own already-verified signals). The very next run named the cause verbatim: `503 {"type":".../errors/chatbot/dispatch-unavailable","code":"audit_unavailable"}`. (`ResourceLoggerService.WatchAsync` yielded nothing under the `dotnet test` host — recorded as a caveat; the resource-log half of the diagnosis came from the native xUnit runner, where the AppHost forwards resource logs to stdout, showing ~1014 probe POSTs all answered `503` in ~10 ms.) **Root cause:** the probe's `400`/`422` expectation is unsatisfiable — the gateway emits only `401`/`403`/`409`/`503`, and the deliberately invalid `IntakeId` is rejected in `AcceptedCommandDispatcher.BuildPlanAsync:149`, whose `InvalidOperationException` `CommandGateway:94` classifies as a transient dispatch outage. **Fix:** admission is now proven by the `dispatch-unavailable` problem type, which is reachable only after `admissionDecision.IsAccepted`; `401`/`403` and the pre-commit `audit-unavailable` denial remain rejected. Pinned by 8 new theory cases in `LiveContinuityAspireE2eContractTests` (24/24 green). **Re-runs:** (1) past admission in 50 s, stopped by my own replica's invalid `WORKFLOW_TIMEOUT=25` — the real `WorkflowTimeout >= 9 x PerScenarioTimeout + margin` guard working as designed, corrected to CI's 265; (2) both continuity scenarios failed on `reason=audit_unavailable` — traced through the chatbot's Polly `500`s to EventStore's `CommandRouter` actor invocation timing out at 100 s, and from there to 2003 sidecar log lines reading `dial tcp 127.0.0.1:6050: connect: connection refused` after `dapr_placement` silently lost its published host-port forward (0 such lines in the runs immediately before and after; `docker restart dapr_placement` restored it); (3) with placement healthy, continuity reached the real Aspire `stop`/`start` EventStore commands and failed because leftover containers from earlier failed runs made Keycloak `Exited` mid-run; (4) after removing the leftovers, the lane ran all three coordinators end to end in 8 m 48 s and wrote all nine manifests and nine reports — rebuild `equivalent`, four scoped dependencies `contained`, `graph`/`attachment-processing` and both continuity scenarios `unmeasurable`. - 2026-08-25 — The four `fault-observed: false` scenarios: observability first, then attribution (six further live runs). **Silent-failure class removed:** all three capturing runners filtered out every `OperationCanceledException`, and `TaskCanceledException` is one — so an internal deadline or `HttpClient.Timeout` was recorded nowhere, the fail-safe coordinator reduced it to `unmeasurable`, and the harness reported no failure at all. That is why the earlier run showed `fault-observed: false`, `executionAssertions: null` with the test complaining only about the scoped-outage sweep. After the fix the next run printed the cause verbatim: `TaskCanceledException : ... HttpClient.Timeout of 30 seconds elapsing`. Four more messages that discarded their cause now name it (refused status in `SubmitUntilAcceptedAsync`, action plus status in `SendSandboxControlAsync`, last-attempt outcome in `RecoveryAccessTokenProvider`, and the split of the conflated scoped-outage fault-versus-control message). **Repository defect fixed:** `SubmitUntilAcceptedAsync` tolerated `HttpRequestException` for three minutes but let a client-side `TaskCanceledException` escape as fatal, aborting scenarios during seeding before any fault was injected — fixed symmetrically with the two sibling polls in the same file that already classify that condition as transient; `m365-subscription-failure` passed in the two most recent runs. **`eventstore-outage` root cause (owned elsewhere):** three consecutive clean runs failed at the allowlisted Aspire `stop` (`Failed to stop resource 'eventstore-<id>'`), and the DCP log gives the mechanism — `[sys] Failed to stop run: Error = timed out waiting for process to stop` — with the EventStore process still emitting `ProjectionUpdateOrchestrator` retry warnings dozens per second **39 seconds after** the stop deadline. A shutdown hang in `references/Hexalith.EventStore`, not this repository; no fix attempted, decision surfaced. **`graph`/`attachment-processing`:** not re-observed — now that continuity failures are correctly captured the sweep fails closed before the scoped-outage stage — with the single-shot independent-control probe identified as the likely cause and deliberately left unchanged, because widening it could convert a genuine containment breach into a pass.

- 2026-08-25 — EventStore stop hang: dump-proven root cause, upstream fix, and a correction. **Correction first:** the previous pass attributed the hang to `ProjectionUpdateOrchestrator` still looping 39 s past the stop deadline. That was a misreading — DCP continues one line-number sequence across restarts, and those lines belong to the instance the lane's own `start` command had just launched. The dying process went silent at `Application is shutting down...` and emitted nothing for twelve seconds. **Proof:** a full `dotnet-dump` taken at the hang (watcher armed on the health endpoint dropping while the PID stayed alive) shows the one foreground thread in `Host.DisposeAsync` → `TracerProviderSdk.Dispose` → `BatchExportThreadWorker.Shutdown` → `Thread.Join(Int32)`, with three OTel exporter threads parked in `OtlpExporterRetryTransmissionHandler.OnSubmitRequestFailure` → `Thread.Sleep`. Decompiling OpenTelemetry 1.17.0 confirms disposal calls `Shutdown()` with `Timeout.Infinite` and that `BaseProcessor.Shutdown` is an interlocked one-shot, which is what makes a bounded pre-dispose shutdown effective. A standalone SIGTERM of the same project exits in 0.5 s, so the hang needs the loaded topology — the exporter must actually be blocked. **Fix (in `references/Hexalith.EventStore`, working tree only, gitlink unmoved at `49b84d0c`):** `BoundedTelemetryShutdownService` shuts the providers down with a finite two-second budget while the host is stopping, registered only alongside `UseOtlpExporter()`; `DOTNET_SHUTDOWNTIMEOUTSECONDS` could never have helped because it bounds `StopAsync`, not disposal. **Verified:** `Failed to stop resource` went from 2-4 occurrences per run to 0, and `eventstore-outage` now injects and restores the real resource. Regression coverage is falsifiable — disabling the bounded shutdown fails `StopAsync_MakesTheSubsequentDisposeNonBlocking`. EventStore `Server.Tests` 3135 passed / 0 failed / 25 skipped.
- 2026-08-25 — Decision 2 implemented and the next blocker surfaced. `ScopedOutageFaultObservation` gained `IndependentControlUnobserved` so a client-side timeout in the control probe is a named outcome rather than the same `false` as a non-202; no retry window was added and both shapes still fail closed, because that flag is the NFR58 containment evidence. With the stop hang gone, `eventstore-outage` reaches post-recovery end-state verification and fails there with `403 authorization-denied` on the absence probe for the fault-probe note; the reason code is retained now that the probe carries its problem document. Presence probes with the same bearer succeed, so it is specific to reading a governed operation that must not exist. Surfaced as a decision, not fixed. **Regression verification:** ChatBot IntegrationTests 200 passed / 0 failed / 5 skipped (two consecutive runs; one earlier run in this pass reported a single failure that did not reproduce and whose identity was not captured — recorded rather than hidden), Architecture 77/0/0 through the repaired runsettings, Server 1766/0/0, Conformance 98/0/0, AppHost 14/0/0, solution build 0 errors.

- 2026-08-25 — Authorized-channel absence probe, first full nine-scenario run, and a second EventStore defect. Per Jerome's decision the projection-side absence check moved off `GET /api/v1/governed-operations/{id}` — which answers `403` for a governed operation that does not exist, by shipped safe-not-found design left unchanged — onto the production `ReadModelGovernedOperationViewStore`, so store name and key cannot drift from what `GovernedOperationProjectionHandler` writes. Absence is observed (a missing key is a definite store answer), a faulting read propagates, and a window with no successful read never reports absence; the durable half stays independent in EventStore's aggregate actor state. The same change was applied to the two cleanup absence checks asking the identical question of the identical API. **Result: the lane ran end to end for the first time — 15 m 44 s, all three coordinators, all nine scenarios measured, zero deviations:** `eventstore-outage` **met** (RPO 0 s, RTO 148.8 s against 900 s / 14400 s), `m365-subscription-failure` **met** (RTO 60.5 s), rebuild **equivalent**, and all six scoped dependencies **contained**. Reproduced identically on a second run. The gate stop-ships on one reason, `continuity:cleanup_incomplete`. **Root-caused:** a metadata-only cleanup diagnostic (stable check names to stderr, never to a report) names `erased-projection-still-present`; the erase itself succeeded (it retries five times and throws otherwise), so the projection is re-created after erasure. EventStore logs `Projection checkpoint save exhausted optimistic-concurrency attempts` **42,066 times in one run** across all three tenants (13,685 `recovery-validation`, 6,210 `tenant-beta`, 1,138 `tenant-alpha`) — the checkpoint never advances, the poller re-delivers forever, and the erased view returns inside the absence window. A second, tenant-independent defect in `references/Hexalith.EventStore`, distinct from the authorised shutdown fix, and very likely the background load that has destabilised this sandbox all session. Stopped there rather than relaxing the cleanup assertion.
- 2026-08-25 — The previously unidentified IntegrationTests failure is pinned: `TrivialGovernedCommandAspireE2eTests.TierThreeParallelValidationShouldRetainEveryNonCancellationSiblingFailure`, asserting three retained sibling failures and intermittently observing two (`:1080`), roughly 1 run in 8. A race in `TopologyReadinessCoordinator`'s sibling retention; pre-existing and untouched by this pass's diff. Reproduced deliberately (1 failure in 9 `dotnet test` invocations; 5 consecutive native-runner invocations clean).
- 2026-08-25 — Regression verification: ChatBot IntegrationTests 200 passed / 0 failed / 5 skipped (excluding the pinned flake above), Architecture 77/0/0, Server 1766/0/0, Conformance 98/0/0, AppHost 14/0/0, solution build 0 errors; EventStore `Server.Tests` 3135 passed / 0 failed / 25 skipped with the submodule fix in the working tree, gitlink unmoved at `49b84d0c`.

- 2026-08-25 — Checkpoint storm root-caused to cross-application contamination, not a library defect; previous report corrected. Chain proven link by link: the shared dev Redis holds a global unprefixed `projection-delivery-writer-protocol` marker written 2026-08-22 by `hexalith-conversations-apphost-boundary-harness`; while it is current, `SaveDeliveredSequenceAsync` correctly refuses every legacy advance; the only other route, the fenced completion in the named-projection path, was failing with `400 UnsupportedCapability` from `AdminOperationalIndexHostedService.LoadBindingAsync`; so no checkpoint advanced and the poller re-delivered forever. The ChatBot topology never writes that marker (only the admin activation endpoint does) and sees it only because the shared `statestore` component is `keyPrefix: none` on a Redis several Hexalith apps share. Verified by deleting only the unprefixed key, leaving the two `conversations-gateway-…||` markers: scoped `projection-checkpoints:*` rows went from **0** to **85** on the next run and the marker was not recreated. **The previous pass's framing of this as a second production-affecting shared-library defect was wrong and is retracted in the findings.** The genuine upstream defect it exposed — four structurally different refusals all reported as optimistic-concurrency exhaustion, which is what made 42,066 warnings undiagnosable — is fixed in the owning repository (working tree only, gitlink unmoved at `49b84d0c`) with a falsifiable test: `ProjectionCheckpointAdvanceRefused` now names each reason, and removing the log call turns `ProjectionCheckpointRefusalDiagnosticsTests` red. EventStore `Server.Tests` 3136/0/25.
- 2026-08-25 — Post-contamination state: **the nine-scenario bundle no longer reproduces.** Three consecutive runs (two `dotnet test`, one native runner) fail earlier, at EventStore restoration, with `EventStore reported healthy but its listener did not accept traffic within 90s`, so the sweep stops at continuity and writes two manifests instead of nine. The restarted EventStore was listening (`Now listening on: http://localhost:8080`, 27 s after the start command) yet the harness probe never reached it; the most consistent explanation is a DCP proxy endpoint that does not survive stop/start behind the harness's retained `_eventStoreClient`, but that is not proven and the same client survived restarts before the contamination was removed. Undetermined between a latent harness defect the storm was masking, a timing consequence of the bounded telemetry shutdown, and further host instability. Stopped rather than speculating; nothing relaxed to get past it. The earlier `met`/`equivalent`/`contained` figures are explicitly not the current state.

- 2026-08-25 — Restoration regression prosecuted in the ordered sequence and closed; it was this pass's own doing. **(1) Bounded telemetry shutdown eliminated:** A/B'd by disabling only the hosted-service registration — with the fix out the stop hang returns *and* the readiness failure still occurs, so the failure does not depend on the fix, and the fix is still required; registration restored. **(2) Retained `_eventStoreClient` eliminated by measurement, not reasoning:** a post-budget, observation-only diagnostic reported `retainedEndpoint=http://localhost:8080/ resolvedEndpoint=http://localhost:8080/ freshClientHealth=503` — identical endpoints, and a fresh client reached the process. **(3) The broken side is the application, because the marker deletion made it unready:** `ProjectionDeliveryWriterProtocolHealthCheck` requires the store-global v2 writer marker and reports `Unhealthy` without it, so `/health` answered `503` for the whole budget. Restoring the marker byte-for-byte restored the lane — the next run measured all nine scenarios again (two `met`, one `equivalent`, six `contained`) with the single gate reason `continuity:cleanup_incomplete`, run `01M0W97Q7MQVX08K9GS71EH8MB`. The 90-second budget was never touched and is not the defect; the persistent `503` was correctly refused. **The earlier "foreign contamination" framing is retracted and the sandbox mutation is reverted**, with inline corrections placed at both earlier claims rather than only in this entry.
- 2026-08-25 — Real cause of `cleanup_incomplete` named, decision referred. With the writer protocol active (as readiness requires), the checkpoint may only advance through the named-projection fenced completion, and that never runs: `src/Hexalith.ChatBot.Server/Program.cs:21` calls `AddEventStoreDomainService(...)` but nothing configures `DomainProjectionIdentityOptions`, so its `AppId` is empty and the SDK's `/admin/operational-index-metadata` endpoint answers `400 UnsupportedCapability` to every identity-bearing request from EventStore's index refresher. Hence 42,066 checkpoint refusals across three tenants, unbounded re-delivery, the erased view returning inside the absence window, and `cleanup-complete: false`. The fix spans two repositories and changes projection-delivery behaviour for a shipped service either way, so it is referred rather than taken.

- 2026-08-25 — Projection identity pinned per Jerome's decision; mechanism confirmed against pre-stated falsifiers, with one link corrected. **Correction first:** the `AppId` was never empty — the SDK defaults it from `DAPR_APP_ID` falling back to `IHostEnvironment.ApplicationName`, so it resolved to `Hexalith.ChatBot.Server`, never the DAPR app id EventStore invokes. The new test caught that on its first run, before it was reported, and the fix changed from "fill the empty value" to "override the derived default". **Fix:** `Program.cs` pins `DomainProjectionIdentityOptions` to `chatbot`/`v1` via `ChatBotDomainServiceIdentity`, overridable through `ChatBot:ProjectionIdentity`, with `ValidateOnStart`; `ChatBotProjectionIdentityTests` (4 cases) asserts non-blank, equals the DAPR app id, **not** the assembly name, blank-override falls back, real override honoured. Recorded as an ADR decision with **Winston's ratification explicitly outstanding**, because it switches the service onto the v2 named-projection delivery path. **Falsifiers checked:** F1 named-dispatch failures 21,034 to **0** (only `sample`/`tenants`, untouched identities, still refuse); F2 checkpoint refusals 42,066 to **0**, plus 0 structural refusals; F3 `cleanup-complete` false to **true**, so `continuity:cleanup_incomplete` is gone; F4 **hit** — see below. Also fixed the readiness message that named the wrong side of the gap; it now reports unreachable versus reachable-but-unhealthy versus stale-retained-endpoint from the observation, with the loop, budget and success condition unchanged.
- 2026-08-25 — F4: two verdicts moved and the gate still stop-ships. Run `01M0WDJMBFS4ESNN618SY2HSA9`: `eventstore-outage` **met** (RPO 0 s, RTO 152.6 s, all seven assertions true including `cleanup-complete`), `m365-subscription-failure` **met**, rebuild **equivalent**, `graph`/`ai-provider`/`command-execution`/`audit-store` **contained**, but `identity` and `attachment-processing` moved from `contained` to **unmeasurable**, both failing with "The independent control operation did not succeed" — the answered-but-not-202 branch of the split message, so the control operation is reaching ChatBot and being refused. Plausibly connected to the delivery-path switch, since the control probe waits on exactly the projection whose delivery changed, but that is a hypothesis and is not investigated here. **The earlier six-`contained` bundle does not stand**; nothing was relaxed to preserve it.

- 2026-08-25 — `identity`/`attachment-processing` investigated against pre-stated falsifiers; the pinning is not the cause. **Observability first:** the earlier split still put an `HttpRequestException` in the refusal branch, so "did not succeed" could mean either ChatBot refused the control command or the probe never reached ChatBot. The observation now carries `IndependentControlCause` (`status-{code}` / `transport-{error}` / `client-timeout`), a transport failure is classified as unobserved rather than negative evidence, and the driver reports "was refused (cause=…)" versus "was never observed (cause=…)". `IndependentControlSucceeded` is untouched and both still fail closed. **F3 answered:** of four runs since the identity was pinned, one failed on those two scenarios and **three passed with all nine clean**, so a deterministic consequence of the delivery-path switch is falsified — no revert trade-off is needed. **What it is remains undetermined and is recorded as such:** the single failure predates the cause diagnostic, so its class was never captured, and one occurrence cannot distinguish an intermittent latent defect the re-delivery storm was masking from the environment. Reported as a rate, not as a fix.
- 2026-08-25 — The required producer lane passed end to end for the first time in this story. Runs `01M0WFJE0BADDQ3BMNEF0B2RJF`, `01M0WGJY0JAMMN3AVAS1G6477V` and `01M0WHJG25EQAP48ZG01DK13F7` each produced nine manifests and nine reports with zero deviations, and the evidence gate did not stop-ship (`Total tests: 1, Passed: 1`): both continuity drills `met`, rebuild `equivalent`, all six scoped dependencies `contained`. **Local runs under ignored `TestResults/` — not repository-retained, not citable, supporting no A10/NFR56/NFR57/NFR41 claim.** The pre-pin six-`contained` bundle remains withdrawn. The intermittent dispatch flake (`reason=audit_unavailable` / "governed checkpoint command was not accepted") is still open with a measured rate of **3 of 30** full-topology runs today — environment, not repository, and obstructive when it hits.

The old dataset-volume residual is closed; the broader provider-scale residual remains open. All story tasks are complete and the story is ready for review; A10 remains provisional pending retained hosted evidence.

- **Hosted-lane unblocking (2026-08-25).** Story 12.15's sole open item — the Test-Architect authenticity approval — is gated on a complete passing hosted recovery bundle, and no such bundle could be produced because the required CI `build` job was failing before the recovery producer ran. Four blockers were cleared (see the Debug Log entry above). None is Story 12.15's own surface. All are disclosed here rather than folded silently into this story's File List — with one correction from the 2026-08-25 review: the `ModelContextProtocol` major bump *had* been folded into three File-List files without disclosure, and is now recorded in the out-of-scope block below. `RecoveryTargets`, the A10 decision, and every measured claim are untouched: this work removes an infrastructure obstacle to producing evidence, it does not produce, cite, or approve any. A10 remains provisional and the ADR remains `Proposed`. The hosted run and Murat's independent authenticity review are still required and still outstanding.

- **Hosted-lane blocker cleared, next blocker surfaced (2026-08-25).** `live-recovery.runsettings` was not well-formed XML — its header comment carried the forbidden `--` digraph twice — so all five `--settings live-recovery.runsettings` call sites (`ci.yml:269`, `:502`, `:580`; `release.yml:156`, `:235`), covering the producer *and* the independent evidence gate on both workflows, aborted before a single test ran. That is the single mechanical reason no passing hosted recovery bundle has ever existed, and it had been present since the file was introduced in v1.5. The comment was rewritten to name the two switches without their leading dashes (and to record why), and the architecture guard that was supposed to protect this file — a substring match that passes on a file the test platform rejects outright — now parses the document and reads `TreatNoTestsAsError` out of the parsed tree. **Proof classes, kept distinct:** *unit/conformance* — Architecture.Tests 77/77 with the new parse-then-read guard, plus a deliberate malformation that turns it red; *lane mechanics* — both required lane filters resolve to exactly one test with the repaired settings applied, and a zero-match filter still exits 1; *live topology* — the producer lane now genuinely starts and runs for its full 5-minute startup budget instead of dying in one second, but it does **not** pass: two attempts (cold and warm) failed identically on a persistent 503 at the recovery mailbox admission probe, while the non-recovery Tier-3 control class passed its governed round-trip arms in the same environment. *External-service simulation and production-equivalence residuals* are unchanged (`RV-EXT-M365`, `RV-DURABLE-WORM`, `RV-PROD-CONTROL`, `RV-PROVIDER-SCALE`, `RV-MEASURABLE-CEILING`, `RV-EVIDENCE-KINDS`). **No CI run or artifact locator is recorded, because none was produced:** hosted CI was not run from this workspace, and the only local output is the ignored `TestResults/live-recovery-validation.trx`, which is not repository-retained evidence. `RecoveryTargets`, the A10 decision, the PRD/addendum/decision-log records and the ADR status are untouched — A10 remains **provisional**, the ADR remains `Proposed`, Murat's authenticity approval remains outstanding, and the story remains `in-progress`.
- **Admission blocker root-caused and fixed; lane now reaches all three coordinators (2026-08-25).** The producer lane's persistent `503` was a repository defect, not the environment: the mailbox admission probe demanded a `400`/`422` the product cannot emit for any input, so it retried a correct-by-design `503` until the startup budget expired. It is fixed by proving admission from the `dispatch-unavailable` problem type — reachable only after admission accepted — which is a more specific assertion than the unreachable one it replaces; nothing was loosened, and `401`/`403` and the pre-commit `audit-unavailable` denial are still rejected. Observability was added first, as required, and is diagnostic-only: the last problem document is retained in the failure message and a bounded `chatbot`/`eventstore` log tail is kept for the failure path; neither reaches an evidence artifact. **Proof classes, kept distinct:** *unit/conformance* — 8 new theory cases pin the admission-proof predicate, IntegrationTests 198/0/5, Architecture 77/0/0, solution build 0 errors; *live topology* — the lane now runs all three coordinators against the real Aspire/DAPR/Keycloak/EventStore topology and writes all nine manifests and reports, but **still does not pass**: rebuild `equivalent`, four scoped dependencies `contained`, `graph`/`attachment-processing` and both continuity scenarios `unmeasurable`; *environment* — three distinct instabilities on this host (a lost `dapr_placement` port forward, leftover topology containers starving Keycloak, and one misconfigured local replica setting) are documented with their evidence and were **not** worked around; *external-service simulation and production-equivalence residuals* unchanged. **No hosted run, artifact locator or measured value is produced or cited.** `RecoveryTargets`, the A10 decision, the PRD/addendum/decision-log records, the ADR status and the outstanding Winston/Murat approvals are untouched — A10 remains **provisional**, the ADR remains `Proposed`, and the story remains `in-progress`.
- **Four `fault-observed: false` scenarios investigated; one mandatory A10 drill root-caused to another repository (2026-08-25).** A whole class of silent failure was removed first: the three capturing runners discarded every `OperationCanceledException`, so timeout-shaped failures reached the evidence bundle as causeless `unmeasurable` reports and the harness reported no failure at all. With that fixed and four more messages made to name their cause, one repository defect was found and fixed (`SubmitUntilAcceptedAsync` treating a client-side timeout as fatal during seeding, before any fault injection — fixed symmetrically with the two sibling polls in the same file), after which `m365-subscription-failure` passes. `eventstore-outage`, the other mandatory A10 drill, is root-caused but **not fixed here**: the EventStore process does not exit within DCP's stop budget (`timed out waiting for process to stop`, with its projection-retry loop still logging 39 s past the deadline), so the allowlisted `stop` fails and the fault is never injected. That defect belongs to `references/Hexalith.EventStore`, and the choice between fixing its shutdown path and changing this scenario's ADR-ratified injection mechanism is a product/architecture decision, surfaced rather than taken. `graph` and `attachment-processing` were not re-reached, because the sweep now correctly fails closed at continuity; their likely cause (a single-shot independent-control probe) is identified and deliberately left unchanged, since widening it could convert a genuine containment breach into a pass. **No verdict was manufactured and nothing was weakened:** no evaluator threshold, fail-closed guard, assertion or measurement budget was changed, and every fix either records a cause that was previously discarded or classifies a transient transport condition the way the surrounding code already does. **No hosted run, artifact locator or measured value is produced or cited.** The mandatory `eventstore-outage` exercise remains unmeasured and is **not** reclassified as a residual (AC3). `RecoveryTargets`, the A10 decision, the PRD/addendum/decision-log records, the ADR status and the outstanding Winston/Murat approvals are untouched — A10 remains **provisional**, the ADR remains `Proposed`, and the story remains `in-progress`.
- **EventStore stop hang fixed upstream; A10 drill now injects and restores, one decision away from measuring (2026-08-25).** Per Jerome's Decision 1 the shutdown hang was fixed in the repository that owns it. A process dump proved the cause is not the projection path this story previously blamed — that attribution is corrected in the findings — but an unbounded `Thread.Join` on OTel batch-exporter threads inside `IHost.DisposeAsync` while those threads are parked in the OTLP retry sleep against an unreachable collector. The fix bounds provider shutdown before disposal, is registered only where the exporter is, and carries falsifiable regression coverage in the same repository. It lives in the submodule **working tree only**: nothing was committed or pushed there and the gitlink is unmoved at `49b84d0c`, because a gitlink pointing at a local-only commit would break the hosted runner. Publishing it is Jerome's step. Decision 2 is implemented as specified — a control-probe timeout is now a distinct named outcome, with no retry window and no widening of what counts as available. **Effect on the mandatory A10 exercise:** `stop:eventstore` now succeeds (0 stop failures, down from 2-4 per run), the real resource is made unavailable and restored, and the ADR matrix and AC1 wording are untouched. It still does not measure: post-recovery `unauthorized-mutation-absent` verification is refused `403 authorization-denied`, now surfaced as a product decision rather than worked around, since accepting a `403` as absence would manufacture the very assertion the story forbids. **No hosted run, artifact locator or measured value is produced or cited**, no threshold, guard, assertion or budget was weakened, and `RecoveryTargets`, the A10 decision, the ADR status and the outstanding approvals are untouched — A10 remains **provisional**, the ADR remains `Proposed`, the story remains `in-progress`.
- **All nine scenarios measured for the first time; one gate reason left, and it is a second upstream defect (2026-08-25).** The `403` was resolved as decided — an authorized channel, with ChatBot's safe-not-found semantics untouched and the API contract not reopened. Absence is now observed through the production projection store rather than inferred from a refusal, the two halves of the NFR59 assertion remain independent (projection store versus EventStore aggregate actor state), and the same channel was applied to the cleanup checks that asked the identical question. The lane then completed all three coordinators end to end: **`eventstore-outage` met (RPO 0 s, RTO 148.8 s), `m365-subscription-failure` met (RTO 60.5 s), rebuild equivalent, all six scoped dependencies contained, zero deviations**, reproduced on two consecutive runs. It still stop-ships, on the single reason `continuity:cleanup_incomplete`, and that is now root-caused to EventStore never advancing its projection checkpoint — 42,066 `ProjectionCheckpointSaveExhausted` warnings in one run across all three tenants — so the poller re-delivers forever and the erased read model returns inside the absence window. That is a **second** defect in `references/Hexalith.EventStore`, distinct from the shutdown fix that was authorised, so it is surfaced rather than fixed; relaxing the cleanup assertion would have converted real, reproducible residue into a manufactured pass. **The measured figures above are from local runs under ignored `TestResults/` — not repository-retained, not citable, and no A10, NFR56, NFR57 or NFR41 claim is made from them.** `RecoveryTargets`, the A10 decision, the ADR status and the outstanding Winston/Murat approvals stay untouched — A10 remains **provisional**, the ADR remains `Proposed`, the story remains `in-progress`.
- **Checkpoint storm was environment, not library — correction — and the nine-scenario result does not currently reproduce (2026-08-25).** Root-causing the checkpoint refusal to its mechanism, rather than inferring it from the log line, showed there is no checkpoint defect to fix: a stale cross-application writer-protocol marker in the shared dev Redis, visible only because the shared actor state store is deliberately `keyPrefix: none`, was correctly suppressing every legacy checkpoint advance. Deleting only that foreign key took scoped checkpoint rows from 0 to 85 on the next run. **The previous pass's report of a second production-affecting shared-library defect is retracted**, so the EventStore owners are not handed a production bug that does not exist. What the investigation did expose, and what is fixed upstream (working tree only, gitlink unmoved at `49b84d0c`, falsifiable test), is that four structurally different checkpoint refusals were all reported as optimistic-concurrency exhaustion — the reason 42,066 identical warnings could not be diagnosed. **Current lane state, stated rather than assumed:** with the contamination gone, three consecutive runs fail earlier at EventStore restoration (`listener did not accept traffic within 90s`) and the sweep produces two manifests instead of nine, so the earlier two `met` / one `equivalent` / six `contained` bundle is **not** the current state and is not cited as such. The new failure is not root-caused and is not worked around. `RecoveryTargets`, the A10 decision, the ADR status and the outstanding approvals remain untouched — A10 **provisional**, ADR `Proposed`, story `in-progress`.
- **Restoration regression closed — it was self-inflicted — and the real blocker named (2026-08-25).** The three candidates were prosecuted in order: the bounded telemetry shutdown is eliminated by A/B (the failure persists with the fix out, and the stop hang returns without it), the retained client is eliminated by direct measurement (identical retained and resolved endpoints; a fresh client reached the process and got `503`), and the broken side proved to be the application — because the writer-protocol marker this pass deleted is *required* by `ProjectionDeliveryWriterProtocolHealthCheck` for readiness. Restoring it byte-for-byte brought the lane straight back to all nine scenarios measured (two `met`, one `equivalent`, six `contained`, run `01M0W97Q7MQVX08K9GS71EH8MB`), so **the earlier nine-scenario result is reproduced rather than superseded, while the intermediate two-manifest runs are explained and withdrawn**. Both earlier mis-framings — "production-affecting shared-library defect" and "foreign contamination" — are corrected inline where they were written, not only in the latest entry. The 90-second budget was never touched; a persistent `503` was refused correctly. What remains is a single referred decision: ChatBot hosts the EventStore domain-service SDK without configuring `DomainProjectionIdentityOptions`, so the named-projection metadata request is refused `400`, the fenced completion never runs, the checkpoint never advances, and `cleanup-complete` honestly reports the residue. **All measured figures remain local, under ignored `TestResults/`, non-citable, and support no A10/NFR56/NFR57/NFR41 claim.** `RecoveryTargets`, the A10 decision, the ADR status and the outstanding approvals stay untouched — A10 **provisional**, ADR `Proposed`, story `in-progress`.

- **Projection identity pinned; the gate's `cleanup_incomplete` reason is cleared; two scoped verdicts moved (2026-08-25).** Per Jerome's decision the identity was configured in ChatBot and the SDK contract left closed. The mechanism was stated as falsifiable claims before the run and every falsifier checked: named-projection dispatch failures 21,034 to 0, checkpoint refusals 42,066 to 0, and `eventstore-outage` now reports `cleanup-complete: true`, removing the single gate reason that had blocked it. One link of the previously reported mechanism was wrong and is corrected inline where it was claimed: the `AppId` was not empty, it resolved to the assembly name through the SDK's `DAPR_APP_ID`-then-`ApplicationName` fallback — caught by the new guard test before being reported. The change switches the service onto the v2 named-projection delivery path, so it is written up as an ADR decision with **Winston's ratification explicitly outstanding**, and guarded by a test that fails on a blank identity or on a regression to the assembly name. **Reported rather than smoothed over:** `identity` and `attachment-processing` moved from `contained` to `unmeasurable` once delivery actually advances, both on the answered-but-not-202 control-probe branch, so the gate still stop-ships and the earlier six-`contained` bundle does not stand. **All figures are local, under ignored `TestResults/`, non-citable, and support no A10/NFR56/NFR57/NFR41 claim.** `RecoveryTargets`, the A10 decision, the ADR status and the outstanding approvals remain untouched — A10 **provisional**, ADR `Proposed`, story `in-progress`.

- **Two moved verdicts proved intermittent, not caused by the pinning; the lane now passes end to end (2026-08-25).** Falsifiers were stated before the runs. The control-probe diagnostic was sharpened first, because "did not succeed" still conflated a ChatBot refusal with a probe that never reached ChatBot; the observation now names the cause and classifies a transport failure as unobserved rather than negative evidence, with `IndependentControlSucceeded` untouched and both branches still fail-closed. Three consecutive runs with the pin still on came back clean, falsifying a deterministic consequence of the delivery-path switch, so no revert trade-off goes to Jerome or Winston on these grounds. What the single failure actually was remains **undetermined** — it predates the cause diagnostic and has not recurred — and that is recorded rather than resolved by repetition. The required producer lane produced its first end-to-end green: nine manifests, zero deviations, gate not stop-shipping, across three runs. **Those runs are local and non-citable**, the pre-pin bundle stays withdrawn, and the intermittent dispatch flake stays on the record at 3 of 30 runs. A10 **provisional**, `RecoveryTargets` unchanged, ADR `Proposed`, Winston's ratification of the identity pin and Murat's authenticity approval both still outstanding.

- **Unchecked review-patch closeout (2026-08-26).** Clean-store writer-protocol activation is now shared by every Tier-3 topology wait and covered through a testable HTTP seam; recovery log-tail redaction, capture faults, token/line bounds, and the five-second drain bound have direct tests. Projection identity now reads `DAPR_APP_ID` through host configuration without retaining the builder, and the integration contract reads EventStore's actual unversioned-registration default rather than comparing against a copied literal. Evidence freshness is policy-bounded per lane and exercised end to end through attestation and validation; duplicate binding presence mismatches fail closed; warning-only passing TRX files remain admissible. CI/release artifacts retain one `TestResults` root while the pre-checkout attempt marker remains under `runner.temp` and is staged after checkout; structural architecture guards bind artifact names, owning steps, `always()` failure paths, lane counts, and both producer budgets. **Proof classes remain distinct:** unit/conformance — StoryEvidenceGate 213/213, Server 1801/1801, Integration 218/218 with five intentional Tier-3 skips, story-focused Architecture 4/4, and `actionlint` clean; live topology — not rerun in this closeout; hosted/CI-retained evidence — none produced and therefore no run or artifact locator exists; external-service simulation and production-equivalence residuals — unchanged. The full Architecture suite is 78/80 with two unrelated UI-policy failures, and the exact solution build remains blocked outside this patch by conflicting `Hexalith.Commons.UniqueIds` assemblies in EventStore plus a duplicate UI test helper. A10 remains provisional, Winston/Murat evidence approval remains outstanding, and the story remains `in-progress`.
